<meta>This is the title page of the first English translation of the works attributed to Geber, a foundational figure in the history of chemistry and alchemy.</meta>

### THE
# WORKS
### OF
# GEBER,
### The Most Famous
## ARABIAN PRINCE
### AND
## PHILOSOPHER

---

->Faithfully Translated into English<-
->By *Richard Russell*, a Lover of Chemistry.<-

---

->This little book says:<-
->I am not indeed great, but within me lies the greatest power. <note>original: "Ait iste Libellus: Magnus quidem non sum, sed inest mihi maxima Virtus."</note><-

---

->Licensed January 28, 1677/8 by Roger L’Estrange. <note>Roger L'Estrange was the official Surveyor of the Imprimery (press censor) under King Charles II.</note><-

---

->*LONDON,*<-
->Printed for N. E. by *Thomas James*, Mathematical<-
->Printer to the King’s most Excellent Majesty, at<-
->the Printing-press in Mincing Lane; and are to be<-
->sold by *Robert Clavel* at the Peacock in St. Paul’s<-
->Churchyard; and other booksellers. 1678.<-

<summary>This is the title page for the 1678 English edition of the works of Geber, translated by Richard Russell and printed in London. It identifies Geber as a famous Arabian prince and philosopher, establishing the book's authority in the field of early chemistry.</summary>

--- Page 3 ---
<meta>This is the opening page of the translator's preface, introducing the author and the purpose of the English translation.</meta>

### THE
## TRANSLATOR
### TO THE
# READER.

The eminence and *worth* of this *author* need no apology; his *works* sufficiently commend *him*. As this present book clearly shows, he used no tautologies <note>original: "Tautologies"; the unnecessary repetition of the same idea in different words</note>, circumlocutions, or fruitless ambiguities <note>original: "Ambages"; roundabout or deceptive ways of speaking</note> in his *writings*. Instead, like a good *master* intending to inform rather than perplex the *minds* of his *disciples*, he speaks so succinctly of all things as is rarely seen in any other *author*. 

The *end* <note>purpose</note> why he *wrote* in his *time* was, as he himself declares, not only to *teach* and *direct* the *ingenious* <note>the talented or intellectually gifted</note>, but also to *detect* and *weaken* <note>original: "Enervate"</note> the fallacious descriptions of *sophisters* <note>original: "Sophiſters"; those who use clever but false arguments, particularly in alchemy</note>, whom he pronounces cursed. He even says he would be accursed himself if he did not discover <note>reveal</note> their frauds. 

For a similar *end*, I was incited by a worthy *friend* of mine to produce this *translation*, so that the *WORKS* of so ancient and venerable an *author*—comprising so many and various most needful *preparations*—might now at length be published in the *English tongue*. This is especially necessary because at this very *day*, there are so many persons publicly exposing their *chemical preparations* (as they call them). If these were truly what they are presented to be, or even in some competent measure corresponded to the specious *titles* with which they are marked <note>original: "inſignized"</note>, they would commend their *authors* rather than needing to be commended by them. For *chemistry* <note>original: "Chymiſtry"</note> is a true and real *art*, and when handled by prudent *artists*, it produces true and real *effects*.

<summary>The translator introduces the author as a concise and honest master of chemistry whose work aims to guide the gifted and expose the frauds of deceptive alchemists. The translator explains that this English version was created to provide a reliable resource in an age of many false chemical claims.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, chemistry, translation, sophisters, chemical preparations, scientific method, integrity</keywords>

--- Page 4 ---
<meta>This page continues the translator's preface, establishing the ethical and professional standards required for the study of chemistry and medicine.</meta>

<header>The Translator</header>

> *But alas! It is found by experience,*
> *That our empty vessels make the loudest sound.*

And (what is more to be lamented) men who have scarcely seen the first entrances of *Chemistry*, or at most are but *beginners* <note>original: "Tyro's"</note> in that most necessary (though much abused) *science*, account themselves sufficiently accomplished if they are confident enough to boast at the rate of *masters*. Indeed, they boast more than good *masters* in that *art* dare to do. For every conscientious man practiced in *chemistry* sees *reason* <note>original: "cause"</note> enough to lay his *hand* upon his *mouth* <note>A gesture of humility or silence.</note>; and yet he does not neglect to do what *good* he can without blowing a *trumpet*. He who does otherwise may very well be suspected; for (according to the common *proverb*) *good wine needs no bush* <note>An old proverb meaning that a good product does not need an advertisement or a sign (the "bush") to attract customers.</note>, and every well-performed *work* commends its own *worker*. 

It is recorded of the famous *painter Apelles* that after he finished any eminent *piece*, he always exposed it to public *view* before he dared to commend it (as a perfect *work*) to the person for whom he made it. His *goal* in this, as *histories* amply relate, was that he might hear himself praised or dispraised from the *mouths* of others. From this, he would be able (prudently guessing by what he heard) to correct and amend his own *errors*. 

If *physicians* of our *time* did, in imitation of *Apelles*, expose their *works* for the same *end*, and would—upon just and undeniable *criticism* <note>original: "censures"</note>—endeavor to amend their *errors*, then their frequent *publications* would be highly commendable; but it is otherwise. Yet *charity* forbids me to attribute this to *greed* <note>original: "avarice"</note>, *fraud*, or *ambition* (*vices* abominable in all men, especially in *physicians*), but rather to other less injurious *causes*, such as *ignorance* and a want of certain *experience*. In amending these, this *book* may prove very serviceable: be—

<summary>The translator criticizes arrogant beginners in chemistry and invokes the example of the painter Apelles to argue that physicians should subject their work to public scrutiny to correct their errors.</summary>
<keywords>Chemistry, Apelles, medical ethics, humility, professional standards, physicians, experience vs. ignorance</keywords>

--- Page 5 ---
<meta>This page continues the translator's preface to the reader, emphasizing the practical and medicinal value of the following work by Geber.</meta>

<header>To the Reader.</header>

because the *ignorant* and *unexpert* may possibly be informed by it, and from there learn true *experiences* <note>original: "Experiences"; here meaning practical experiments or observations</note>, by which (if *industrious*) they will in *time* be able to *correct* their own *errors*, just as well as if they had used the *policy* of *Apelles* <note>Apelles was a renowned ancient Greek painter known for his diligence and for inviting public criticism to improve his work.</note>. But of *covetous*, *deceitful*, and *ambitious men*, there is no such *hope*. Therefore, omitting these, I do not doubt but that to every *man* studious of *truth* <note>original: "Verity"</note>, the present *work* will be highly acceptable: because herein he will find *instructions* sufficient to inform his *judgment* in preparing medicines truly *chemical* <note>original: "Chymical"</note>. 

For this *author* (excelling others in this regard) has clearly and candidly, though briefly, taught the *methods* of purifying all *metals*, *minerals*, *salts*, *alums* <note>original: "Allomes"</note>, etc. In which true and perfect *purification*, the *virtue* <note>The inherent medicinal power or essential quality of a substance.</note> of each *subject* (its *vice* and *impurity* being separated) is rendered ten times more efficacious in *medicinal use* than the same *subject* could have been without such preceding *preparation*; as *experience* daily proves. 

But if any *man* objects and says, "This *author* taught these *purifications* only in *order* to find the great *Philosophers' Stone*"; to him I answer this: All *philosophers* (ancient and modern) unanimously affirm that *impurity* tends toward *corruption* and *death*, but *purity* toward *incorruption* and *life*. Therefore, if they—in order to amend imperfect *metals*, namely to heal their *diseases*—so strictly enjoined the *separation* of *heterogeneous* <note>original: "Heterogeneals"; substances of a different or foreign nature.</note> parts and the *purification* of *homogeneous* <note>original: "Homogenal"; substances of the same essential nature.</note> things; how much more ought every faithful *physician* labor in purifying the *subjects* of *medicine* for the *human body* (which is more precious than all *metals*)? Since the substances specified here are no small *part* of that medicine, I leave this to the *judgment* of all. And having introduced these points, I crave the *reader's patience* in the perusal of what follows.

When I had perfected this *translation* of *GEBER*, my *purpose* was to have concealed my *name*, as I did in the *translation* of *Royal and Practical Chemistry*...

<summary>The translator argues that Geber's methods for purifying metals and minerals are essential for creating potent medicines, asserting that the pursuit of purity is as vital for human health as it is for alchemy.</summary>
<keywords>Geber, alchemy, purification, medicinal chemistry, Philosophers' Stone, Apelles, metals, minerals</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues the translator's preface, where he lists his previous and forthcoming works in the field of alchemy and chemistry.</meta>

<page-type>preface</page-type>
<header>The Translator, etc.</header>

chemistry (which, being my first *essay* <note>original: "Essay"; an initial attempt or trial</note> in that kind of *learning*, I must confess was not as well performed as I intend that *book* shall be if a *second edition* <note>original: "Impression"</note> of it is made in my time); of *Beguinus* his *Tyrocinium* <note>Jean Beguin’s *Tyrocinium Chymicum* (1610), a foundational chemistry textbook</note>; of *Helvetius* his *Golden Calf* <note>Johann Friedrich Helvetius’s *Vitulus Aureus* (1667), a famous account of alchemical transmutation</note>; all made public some years ago; together with that of the *Triumphant Chariot of Antimony* <note>A famous work by Basil Valentine</note>, with *Kerckring*’s <note>original: "Kirkringius"; Theodor Kerckring, a Dutch anatomist and chemist</note> *notes* on it (though not the small *piece* by *Synesius* annexed to it) lately published. 

But finding some people too ready, as the saying goes, to thrust their *sickle* into another *man's harvest* <note>A metaphor for taking credit for someone else's work or interfering in their business</note>, I am now at last forced to add my *name* here. I am resolved to do so from now on whenever any other *works* translated by me (through the favor of *God*) shall be exposed to public *view*. 

For, besides the large *volume* of the *works* of *Raymond Lully* <note>Ramon Llull (c. 1232–1315), a Majorcan philosopher and mystic to whom many alchemical works were attributed</note> now ready for the *press*—not to mention other *pieces* on various *subjects*, of which I have translated many for private *individuals*—I have *translated into English* the greatest *part* of the *works* of *Paracelsus* <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a Swiss physician and alchemist</note>. I have completed two of his three *volumes*, and about half of the *third*, which I intend to finish (if *God* permits) as *time*, *opportunity*, or *encouragement* shall be offered. 

*Reader*, I could mention more here; but considering that no *man* has anything that he has not received <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 4:7</note>, and timely remembering that *poetic admonition*:

---

->It is fitting for no one to be proud;<-
->He who is so puffed up, abandons every good thing:<-
<note>original Latin: *Nullum decet esse superbum; / Qui sic inflatur, deferit omne Bonum*</note>

---

I here conclude, subscribing myself (as I always desire to be) your real friend,

*May 3rd, 1678. From my* &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Richard Russell.
*house at the Star in*
*Newmarket in Wap-*
*ping, near the Dock.*

### The PREFACE of the AUTHOR, Showing the Reasons why he wrote this BOOK.

WE, *with continued and frequent diligence of labor, and equivalent great study, not without the most profound and serious thoughts, etc.* publicly expose to your view the *Investigation* of this most noble science, so that the subsequent *volumes* may be better and more clearly understood by you; and that once understood, searched into, and found, they may more easily and rea-

The author's preface explains that the text is the result of deep study and labor intended to make the science of alchemy more accessible and understandable.</summary>

<page-num>2</page-num>

dily be brought to effect. And because finding out the *Reason* of *Art* is one thing, and attempting and proving the *subtleties* and *complexities* of *these* things is another, until by *operating*, *searching*, and *experiencing*, the intended *completion* is attained; therefore, whatever we found out *by* the things declared (*I mean*, of things perfecting *Art*), we have written here according to the intention of our *Mind*. *Yet*, let no man think that we composed this *Investigation* before our *Book*, which is *entitled* *The Sum of the Perfection of the Magistery*; in which, whatever we saw and handled, we have *completely* described, according to the *Order* of *Science*, *with experience and certain knowledge*, which we acquired by our *scrutiny* exercised <unclear>about</unclear> the *effects* of *natural* and *mineral things*, and the *various transmutations* apparent in the *work*. *And* we have explained our *science*, previously composed, with this *Commentary of Investigation*, which we purposely wrote for that end; therefore, by right, this must precede that, since by this book I am to make an **Inquiry about the Thing Perfecting.**

## C H A P. I.

### Of Things Perfecting and Corrupting Metallic Bodies.

Therefore, seeing this *Science* treats of the *imperfect bodies* of *minerals*, and teaches how to perfect them; we in the first place consider two *things*, namely *imperfection* and *perfection*. Our intention is occupied with these two, and we purpose to treat them. We compose this *book* of *things perfecting* and *corrupting* (according as we have found by *experience*) because *contraries* set near each other are the more manifest.

The *thing* which perfects in *minerals* is the substance of *Argentvive* <note>Quicksilver or Mercury</note> and *Sulphur* mixed in proportion by long and temperate decoction in the bowels of clean, thickened, and fixed *Earth* (with the conservation of its *radical humidity* not corrupting) and brought to a solid, fusible substance, with due ignition, and rendered malleable. By the *definition* of this perfecting *nature*, we may more easily come to the *knowledge* of the *thing corrupting*. And this is that which is to be understood in a contrary *sense*, namely the pure substance of

<meta>catchword: Sulphur</meta>

<summary>The author defines the focus of the science as the perfection of minerals, explaining that perfection arises from the balanced combination and slow heating of mercury and sulfur within the earth. He notes that understanding the nature of perfection allows one to identify what causes corruption or imperfection in metals.</summary>

<keywords>perfection, imperfection, minerals, Argentvive, Sulphur, decoction, radical humidity, malleable, metallic bodies, alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 20 ---
<meta>This page continues the definition of imperfection in metallic bodies, identifying the specific metals classified as perfect or imperfect and outlining the necessary preparations for their transformation.</meta>

<page-num>( 4 )</page-num>

*Sulphur* and *Argentvive* <note>Quicksilver or Mercury</note>, mixed without due proportion, or not sufficiently cooked in the bowels of unclean, not rightly thickened or fixed *Earth*, having a combustible and corrupting humidity, and being of a rare and porous substance; or having fusion without due ignition, or no fusion, and not being sufficiently malleable.
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The first definition I find included in these two bodies, namely in *Sol* <note>Gold</note> and *Luna* <note>Silver</note>, according to the perfection of each; but the second in these four, namely *Tin, Lead, Copper*, and *Iron*, according to the *imperfection* of each. And because these imperfect bodies are not reducible to health and *perfection* unless the contrary is operated in them—that is, the *manifest* be made *occult* <note>hidden</note>, and the *occult* be made *manifest*—which operation, or *contrariation*, is made by *preparation*; therefore they must be prepared, superfluities in them removed, and what is lacking supplied; and so the known *perfection* inserted in them. But *perfect bodies* do not need this preparation; yet they need such preparation by which their parts may be more *subtiliated* <note>refined or made subtle</note>, and they reduced from their *corporality* to a fixed *spirituality*. The intention of which is to make from them a spiritual fixed body, that is, much more attenuated and refined than it was before. Of all these preparations (according to our *investigation*), we shall sufficiently treat in their proper place in this book.

<meta>catchword: What</meta>

<summary>The author identifies gold and silver as perfect bodies, while tin, lead, copper, and iron are imperfect due to improper elemental proportions and heating. He explains that imperfect metals require preparation to remove excess and supply deficiencies, whereas perfect metals are refined to reach a more subtle, spiritual state.</summary>

What shall be (as is mentioned hereafter) sufficiently prepared, will be fit to make the *White* or great *Red Elixir*.

### CHAP. II.

->*Of the Philosophers' Stone, that it*<-
->*is only one, for the White, and for*<-
->*the Red, and from what things it is*<-
->*extracted. And of the possibility*<-
->*and way of perfection.*<-

We find modern artists describe to us only one *Stone*, both for the *White* and for the *Red*; which we grant to be true: for in every Elixir that is prepared, *White* or *Red*, there is no other thing than *Argentvive* <note>Quicksilver or Mercury</note> and *Sulphur*, of which one cannot act, nor exist, without the other. Therefore it is called by *Philosophers* one *Stone*, although it is extracted from many bodies or things. For it would be a foolish and vain thing to think to extract it from a thing in which it is not, as some infatuated men have imagined; for it never was the intention of *Philosophers*; yet they speak many things by similitude <note>analogy or likeness</note>. And because all *metallic bodies* are compounded of *Argentvive* and *Sulphur*, pure or impure, by accident,

<summary>The author explains that there is only one Philosophers' Stone for both the White and Red Elixirs, fundamentally composed of mercury and sulfur. He emphasizes that the Stone must be extracted from materials that naturally contain these elements, as all metals are composed of them.</summary>
<keywords>Philosophers' Stone, White Elixir, Red Elixir, Argentvive, Sulphur, metallic bodies, alchemy, preparation</keywords>

--- Page 22 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the preparation of metallic bodies, explaining that because impurities are accidental rather than innate, they can be removed through specific chemical processes to achieve perfection.</meta>

and not innate in their original nature; therefore, by suitable *preparation*, it is possible to take away such impurity. For the *expoliation* <note>stripping away</note> of *accidents* <note>non-essential properties</note> is not impossible; therefore, the goal of *preparation* is to take away *excess* and supply the *deficiency* in perfect bodies. But *preparation* is varied according to the *diversity* of things needing it. For experience has taught us various ways of acting, namely: *calcination, sublimation, descension, solution, distillation, coagulation, fixation,* and *inceration*. All of these we sufficiently explain in the *Sum of the Perfection of the Magistery*. For these are works helpful in *preparation*.

## CHAPTER III.

### *Of things that aid preparation, and of their cleansing.*

Things aiding preparation are these: namely, all kinds of *salt, alum, atraments* <note>metallic sulfates or vitriols</note>; also *glass, borax*, and whatever are of this *nature*, along with very sharp *vinegar* and *fire*.

->*Cleansing of Common Salt.*<-

Common *salt* is cleansed thus: First, burn it, and cast the burnt salt into hot *water* to be dissolved; filter the *solution*, and then solidify it by

<summary>The author describes how various chemical processes like calcination and distillation can remove accidental impurities from metals to achieve perfection. He also lists materials that aid in these preparations, such as salts, borax, and vinegar, and begins instructions for cleansing common salt.</summary>

by gentle fire. Calcine the *congelate* <note>solidified substance</note> for a day and night in a moderate fire, and keep it for use.

### *Cleansing of Salt-Alkali.*

*Salt-Alkali* is cleansed in the same way as *common salt*, and it is *Sagimen Vitri* <note>glass-gall or salt of glass</note>. First it is ground, and then the whole is dissolved in hot *common water*; afterward filtered, solidified, and calcined with a moderate fire.

### *Cleansing of Salt-Gem.*

First it is ground, and then it is proceeded with as with *common salt*.

### *Cleansing of Salarmoniac.*

Grind it first with the *preparation* of cleansed *common salt*; then let it be sublimed in a high *body* and *head* <note>an alembic</note>, until it all ascends pure. Afterward dissolve it upon a *porphyry* <note>a hard stone slab</note> in the open air, if you would make *water* <note>liquid</note> of it; or keep the *sublimate* sufficiently pure.

### *Cleansing of other Salts:*

There are various other kinds of *salt* found, which are prepared and cleansed as is said above.

### *Cleansing of Alloms.*

First of *rock alum*: Many things may be prepared by it without its cleansing; yet it may be cleansed thus: Put it in an *alem-*

### *Cleansing of Jamenous Alum.*

*Jamenous Alum* is prepared like the former; but in this *Art* it is of greater virtue <note>efficacy or power</note>. There are yet many other *alums* found, all of which are prepared and cleansed as is said above.

### *Cleansing of Atraments.*

First, concerning black *atrament* <note>a black vitriol or ink-like substance</note>, which is cleansed thus: First, it must be dissolved in clean *vinegar*, afterward distilled and solidified. Or let it be first distilled by an *alembic*, and all its *moisture* extracted. The *residues* calcined in this distillation must be dissolved upon a *porphyry*, or dissolved in their own *water*, and then solidified. Or that *water* (if the artist so wills) may be kept.

### *Cleansing of Copperas.*

*Copperas* or *vitriol* is cleansed like black *atrament*; yet black *atrament* has greater *earthiness* than the green. There are various *atraments*, and they are found in various *colors*, all of which are cleansed as the aforementioned.

( 9 )

### *Of Glass and Boraxes.*

Glass and boraxes, if made in a proper manner, do not need preparation.

### *The Cleansing of most sharp Vinegars.*

Vinegars of any kind, whether acute <note>sharp or intense</note> and harsh, are cleansed by subtiliation <note>refining or making subtle</note>; and their virtue, or effect, is improved by distillation. We have now spoken sufficiently of the cleansing and purifying of all the aforementioned substances, with which the imperfect bodies may be prepared, purified, improved, and refined, with *fire* always acting as a proper mediator.

# C H A P. IV.

## *Of Preparation and Improvement of Bodies in General.*

They are prepared and purified by the aforementioned substances, according to the intention of *fire*, in this manner: These imperfect bodies have superfluous *humidities* and a combustible *sulphureity*, with *blackness* generated in them and corrupting them; also, they have in them an unclean, foul, combustible, and very *gross earthiness*, hindering *ingress* <note>penetration or entry</note> and *fusion*. These, and things such as these, are superfluous in the aforementioned bodies, which are

<summary>This page concludes instructions on purifying auxiliary materials like glass and vinegar before introducing the general preparation of imperfect bodies. It explains that these bodies contain impurities like excess moisture, sulfur, and gross earthiness that must be removed through the mediation of fire to allow for proper fusion.</summary>
<keywords>glass, borax, vinegar, subtiliation, distillation, fire, imperfect bodies, humidities, sulphureity, earthiness, fusion</keywords>

--- Page 26 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for the general preparation and improvement of imperfect bodies, following the explanation of their impurities.</meta>

found to be in them through our *Experience* and certain and ingenious *Investigation*. And because these *Superfluities* have access to these *Bodies* accidentally and not fundamentally, and the *Removal* <note>originally "Supoliation," meaning to strip or deprive</note> of *Accidentals* is possible, it is therefore necessary for us, with *Artificial Fire* and by the previously mentioned cleansed substances, to remove all superfluous *Accidents*, so that only the fundamental substance of *Argentvive* <note>quicksilver or mercury</note> and *Sulphur* remains. This is the entire *Preparation* and perfect *Purification* of *Imperfect Bodies*. The *Improvement*, *Purification*, and *Refinement* of these (once the pure substance remains) are achieved in many ways, depending on what the *Elixir* requires.

Therefore, the general way of preparing and purifying is this: First, with a proportional *Fire*, all the superfluous and corrupt *Moisture* in their essence must be raised; also, their subtle and burning excess must be removed, and this is done through *Calcination*. Afterward, the entire corrupt substance of their superfluous burning *Moisture* and *Blackness* remaining in their *Calx*, must be corroded with those previously mentioned cleansed, corrosive, sharp, or harsh substances, until the *Calx* is white or red (or colored according to the nature and properties of the *Body*) and clean and pure from all excess or corruption. These *Calxes* must be cleansed with these *Corrosives* by grinding, soaking, and washing. Afterward, the entire unclean *earthi-*

<summary>This page describes the process of removing non-essential impurities from "imperfect bodies" using artificial fire and corrosive substances. By using techniques like calcination and washing, the alchemist isolates the pure radical substances of mercury and sulfur required for the elixir.</summary>
<keywords>Superfluities, Argentvive, Sulphur, Calcination, Calx, Purification, Imperfect Bodies, Moisture, Corrosives</keywords>

ness, and combustible and gross filth <note>feculency</note> must be taken away and deposited with the previously mentioned cleansed or pure things that do not have *metallic fusion*. These should be mixed and well ground together with the previously mentioned *calx*, which has been purified in the manner described before. For these substances, during the *fusion* or *reduction* of the *calx*, will retain the gross and unclean earthiness within themselves, leaving the body pure and cleansed of all corrupting excess; and this is done by *descending*.

The way of *improving* and *refining* the pure substance of these is generally this: First, this purged and *reduced body* is calcined again with *fire*, using the *cleansing* <note>mundative</note> aids mentioned before; and then it must be dissolved with those substances that are *solvents* <note>solutive</note>. For this *water* is our *stone*, and *quicksilver* <note>Argent-vive</note> of *quicksilver*, and *sulphur* of *sulphur*, extracted from the *spiritual body*, and *refined* or *thinned*. This may be *improved* by strengthening the *elemental virtues* within it with other prepared things of its own kind, and by increasing the color, fixation, weight, purity, fusion, and all other qualities that belong to a perfect *elixir*. And this is the way (discovered only by us) of the *preparation*, *purification*, *refinement*, and *improvement* of *mineral bodies* in general. Now we proceed to the *special* or *particular preparation* of every *imperfect body*, with all its methods, as well as the *perfect mineral bodies*. And first, concerning *Jupiter* <note>the alchemical name for tin</note>.

## C H A P. V.

### *Of the Preparation of Tin Specifically.*

Jupiter <note>the alchemical name for tin</note> is prepared in many ways, yet best in this manner: Put it in a suitable vessel in a *furnace* of *calcination*, and under it make a fire sufficient for the proper *fusion* of the *body*; stirring the liquefied *body* with an iron *spatula* full of holes, and drawing off the *scum* that rises, and again stirring the *body* while maintaining that same heat of the fire, until a good quantity of that *scum* or *powder* is gathered together on the surface; take this off, and continue stirring again until the whole *body* is reduced to *powder*. Sift this *powder* and replace it again in the *furnace*, adding fire that does not exceed the fire of its *fusion*, and stir it often. Keep it in this *fire of calcination* for a natural day <note>24 hours</note>, or thereabouts, until all its accidental and superfluous *humidity* is removed, along with its combustible and corrupting *sulphur*. For the fire elevates and consumes every fugitive and flammable *substance*; then wash it well and often with the substances mentioned before—namely, with cleansed *common salt* and *alum*, and with purified and harsh *vinegar*—and dry it in the *sun* or in the *air*; and then again grind, wash, and dry it:

<vocab>Jupiter, Tin, Calcination, Fusion, Body, Spatula, Scum, Powder, Humidity, Sulphur, Common-Salt, Alum, Vinegar</vocab>

<summary>This chapter details the specific method for preparing tin (Jupiter) through a process of melting, constant stirring to produce a powder, and prolonged calcination to remove impurities. The resulting substance is then purified through repeated washing with salt, alum, and vinegar before being dried.</summary>
<keywords>Jupiter, tin, calcination, fusion, alchemical preparation, purification, sulfur, humidity, metallurgy</keywords>

--- Page 29 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific instructions for preparing tin (Jupiter), moving from the initial calcination and washing to the separation of its pure substance from earthy impurities and its final dissolution.</meta>

<page-num>13</page-num>
<meta>catchword: we</meta>

and do this repeatedly, until by the sharpness of the *Salts*, *Alums*, and *Vinegar*, its entire *Humidity*, *Blackness*, and *Impurity* shall be consumed, corroded, and removed. When this is done, add *Glass* crushed into *Powder* to these previously mentioned substances; and when you have made the whole into a paste together, then with sufficient *Fire*, make it melt in a *Crucible* with a hole in its bottom, set within another vessel. The pure and clean *Body* will descend, while the entire earthy and foul substance remains above with the *Glass*, and *Salts*, or *Alums*; for in that descended and reduced *Body* is an equal and perfect proportion of clean *Quicksilver* <note>argentvive</note> and white, non-burning *Sulphur*. This is because the *Fire* and the *Corrosives* have separated the entire *Humidity*, as well as the fleeting, flammable, and corrupting substance and *Blackness*. Through that *Descensory* <note>a vessel used for distillation by descent</note>, by mixing it into a paste with *Salts*, *Alums*, and *Glass*, the entire foul, earthy substance is separated, and the pure substance remains in its proper proportions.

Afterward, calcine this pure reduced body again with pure and clean *Sal Ammoniac* <note>salarmoniac</note> until it is equal in weight, or nearly so. When it is well and perfectly calcined, then grind the whole well and for a long time upon a *Porphyry-stone*, and place it in the open *Air* in a cold and damp place; or in *Glass Vessels* in a *Furnace* of *Solution*, or in *Horse-dung*, until the whole is dissolved, adding more *Salt* if necessary. We ought to honor this *Water*, for it is what

## CHAPTER VI.

### Of the *Preparation of Saturn*.

Lead is prepared in this way: Set it in a similar *Furnace of Calcination*, stirring it while it is in flux (as you did with the *Tin*), until it is converted into a very fine *Powder*. Sift this and again set it in the *Fire of its Calcination*, as mentioned before, until its volatile <note>fugitive</note> and flammable substance is removed. Afterward, take out your red *Calx* <note>the powder produced by heating a metal</note>, which you should soak and grind often with cleansed *Common Salt*, purified *Atrament* <note>a black substance, typically vitriol or a metallic salt</note>, and very sharp *Vinegar*. For the *Red*, you must use these; just as for the *White*, you used *Common Salt*, *Jamenous Alum* <note>likely alum from Yemen, known for its high quality</note>, and *Vinegar*. Also, as was said regarding *Tin*, your matter must be often soaked, dried, and ground until, by the benefit of the substances mentioned above, this impurity is totally removed. Then mix *Glass* with the substances mentioned above and, as you did with the *Tin*, cause the pure body to descend, so that by descending it may be reduced. Again, calcine it with pure <term>Sal Ammoniac</term> <note>salarmoniac</note> (as was said regarding <term>Jupiter</term> <note>tin</note>), and very finely grind and dissolve it by the method mentioned before. For that is the *Water of Quicksilver* <note>argentvive</note> and *Sulphur* made in proper proportions, which we use in the composition of the *Red Elixir*.

# CHAP. VII.

## Of the Preparation of Venus.

Venus, or *Copper*, is prepared in this way:
Make a layer of *Common Salt*, well-cleansed,
in a *Crucible*, and upon that put a piece of
*Copper Plate*, and over that a *layer of Salt*, and

until the *Vessel* is full: which being covered
and firmly luted <note>sealed with airtight clay or paste</note>, place in a *Calcining*

and separate and scrape off what has been *calcined*;
and again *calcine* the *Plates* with *New*
*Salt*, as before, repeating the *calcination* so
often until all the *Plates* are consumed
and corroded by the benefit of the *Salt* and
*Fire*: for the *Salt* corrodes the <insert>~~*excessive*~~</insert>

free from *blackness*. Another time, soak it
with new *salt* and *vinegar*, and *grind* it, and
after *attrition* (or *grinding*), put it in a *calcining furnace* in an open *vessel*, and let it stand

# CHAP. VIII.

## Of the *Preparation of Mars*.

Mars, or *Iron*, is best prepared thus: Let it be
calcined just as *Venus* was, with cleansed *Common Salt*,
and let it be washed with pure *Vinegar*;
once it is washed, dry it in the *Sun*, and when
dried, grind and soak <note>imbibe</note> it with new *Salt* and
*Vinegar*, and then put it in the same *Furnace*,
as was said of *Venus*, for three days. Honor
this *Solution*, namely the *Water of fixed Sulfur*,
which wonderfully augments the *color* of the *El-
ixir*. These words may suffice to be spoken of the
*Preparation of Imperfect Bodies*.

## C H A P. I X.

### Of the Preparation of *Sol*.

*Perfect bodies* do not need *preparation* in relation to their further *perfection*, being already perfect; but so that they may be more refined and thinned <note>subtiliated and attenuated</note>, we apply this *preparation* to them:
Take *Sol* or *Gold* beaten into thin *plates*, and with them and *common salt* very well prepared, make *layer upon layer* in a *vessel of calcination*; set this into a *furnace* and *calcine* it well for three days, until the whole is subtly *calcined*. Then take it out, grind it well, wash it with *vinegar*, and dry it in the *sun*. Afterward, grind it well with half its weight of cleansed *sal ammoniac*; then set it to be dissolved until the whole (by the *benefit* of *common salt* and *ammoniac*) is dissolved into a moist clear *water*. This is the precious *ferment* for the *Red Elixir*, and the true *body* made *spiritual*.

## ->C H A P. X I.<-

### ->Of the Properties of the Greater Elixir.<-

WE have now sufficiently determined the *Preparation* and refinement <note>subtiliation</note> of *perfect Bodies*, so that every *Discreet Operator* may be enabled to attain his *Intention*. Therefore, let him attend to the *Properties* and *Ways* of *Action* of the *Composition* of the *Greater Elixir*: For we endeavor to make one *Substance*, yet compounded and composed of many; so permanently fixed that, being placed upon the *Fire*, the *Fire* cannot injure it; and so that it may be mixed with *Metals* in *Flux* <note>melting</note>, and flow with them, and enter with that which in them is of an ingressible *Substance*, and be mixed with that which in them is of a mixable *Substance*; and be consolidated with that which in them is of a consolidated *Substance*; and be fixed with that which in them is of a fixable *Substance*; and not be burned by those *Things* which do not burn *Gold* and *Silver*; and take away *Consolidations* and *Weights* with due *Ignition*.

## The Epilogue and Conclusion of the Work.

Therefore, from what has been set forth above, those *Things* are manifest in which the *Truth* <note>Verity</note> of the *Work* is near; and we have considered the *Things* that perfect this *Work* through our true *Investigation* and certain *Experience*, by which we are assured that all the *Words* now written (by us alone) in our *Columns* are true, just as we found them through *Experiment* and *Reason* related therein. But those *Things* which we have performed through our *Experience*, seen with our *Eyes*, and handled with our *Hands*, we have written in the *Summary* <note>Sum</note> of the *Perfection* of our *Mastery* <note>Magistery</note>. Therefore, let the *Wise* <note>Sapient</note> *Craftsman* <note>Artificer</note> carefully study our *Books*, gathering our scattered *Intention*, which we have described in various places so that we might not expose it to *Malicious* <note>Malignant</note> and *Ignorant Men*; and let him prove what he has gathered even unto *Knowledge*, studying and experimenting with the *persistence* <note>Instance</note> of *Ingenious Labor*, until he comes to an entire understanding of the whole. Let the *Craftsman* <note>Artificer</note> exercise himself and find out this *Way* of *Investigation* now proposed (out of great *Love*) by our *Consideration*; and also acquire a full <note>plenary</note> *Knowledge* of the *Truth* <note>Verity</note> of the perfecting and corrupting *Matter* and *Form*. For we, in our *Investigation*, have considered the

## *The Division of this First Book into Four Parts.*

### CHAPTER III.

#### *Of the Impediments on the Part of the Artist's Soul.*

**W**e set forth one chapter in which we absolutely and clearly declared the *Impediments* depending on the part of the *Body* of the *Artificer*: It now remains that we briefly declare the *Impediments* on the *Part* of his *Soul*, which mostly hinder the completion of this *Work*. Therefore, we say, he who does not have a *Natural Ingenuity* and *Soul* searching and subtly examining *Natural Principles*, the *Fundamentals* of *Nature*, and *Artifices* which can follow *Nature* in the properties of her *Action*, cannot find the true *Radix* of this most precious *Science*. As there are many who have a *stiff neck*, void of *Ingenuity* in every investigation; and who can scarcely understand *Common Speech*, and likewise learn *Works* that are commonly *Common* with difficulty. Besides these,

## C H A P. IV.

### *Of External Impediments Hindering the Work of this Art.*

WE have reduced to two headings all *impediments* retarding the *end* of this *art*, all of which arise from *radical principles* according to the *nature* of the *artificer* of this most precious *business*. Therefore, it concerns us now at length to declare those *impediments* that are externally supervening <note>Occurring or arriving from outside the artist's own mind or nature</note>, and happening by *chances* and *casualties*, by which this most *glorious work* is hindered. We see some subtle and ingenious *men*, skilled in the *works* of *nature*, and, as far as is possible, followers of her in her *principles* and *works*; in whom also there is an *investigation* that is not *fantastical* into all *things* beneath the *lunar circle* <note>The terrestrial realm below the moon, subject to change and decay</note> that are regulated by the *motions* and *actions* of *nature*. Yet these, oppressed with extreme *poverty* and lying under a state of *indigence*, are compelled to postpone or neglect this *excellent magistery*. There are many *others* besides the aforementioned *curious men* who are detained by the various *cares* and *solicitudes* of this *world*, occupying themselves wholly in

## CHAP. V.

### *The Conclusion of this First Part, containing the Qualifications of the Artificer.*

Therefore, from what is said above, we conclude that the *artificer* of this *work* ought to be well-skilled and perfect in the *sciences* of *natural philosophy*. For no matter how much *money* he has, and although he be endowed with a naturally profound *wit* and *desire* for this *artifice*, he still cannot attain his *end* unless he has acquired *natural philosophy* through *learning*. For the defect of that which is not acquired by *natural ingenuity* must be supplied by *learning*. Therefore, the *artificer* must be helped by the deepest *search* and *natural industry*. For, by reason of his *learning* alone, no matter how much *science* he has acquired, unless he is also helped by *natural industry*, he will not be invited to such a precious *banquet*. # The Second Part of this First Book: in which are related the reasons of men denying this Art, which are afterward refuted.

---

## *A General Division of the Following.*

Having already in one summary of this little book comprised all the *impediments* of this *work*, and given you a *doctrine* sufficient for the *adherence* to this *Art*: it is now appropriate, according to the *intent* of our *purpose*, to argue against *sophisters* and *ignorant men*. First, setting down their *reasons*, just as we promised we would do from the *beginning*. Lastly, we weaken all those *reasons* so that it shall be evident to the *wise* that they contain nothing of *truth* in them.

---

### C H A P. I.

## *The true reasons of men simply denying this Art.*

There are various people who deny and dismiss this *Art*. Some do so simply, others only by supposing

...supposing it to be. For some, simply affirming this *Art* does not exist, sophistically strengthen their *argument* in this way: they say there are distinct *species* and *diversities* of *things* because the *proportions* in the *mixing* of *elements* with each other are diverse and distinct. For an *ass* is a different *species* from a *man* because, in its *composition*, it has a different *proportion* of *elements*. This is also true in all other *diversities* of *things*, and therefore in *minerals*. Therefore, since the *proportion* of *things* *mixable* (by which the *form* and *perfection* of the *thing* is acquired) is unknown, how can we know both the *mixture* and how to form what is to be mixed? But we are ignorant of the true *proportion* of the *elements* of *Sol* <note>gold</note> and *Luna* <note>silver</note>; therefore, we must be ignorant of how to form them.

   In the same manner, they also argue otherwise, condemning our *magistery*. For they say, although you might know the *proportion* of *elements*, yet you do not know the way of mixing them together because *Nature* procreates these in *caverns*, in *mines*, and in hidden *places*; therefore, seeing as you do not know the *way* (or *method*) of their *mixing*, you are also ignorant of how to make them. Likewise, they argue again: although you might duly know this, yet in the *action* of *mixing*, you do not understand how to equalize the *agent heat* by whose mediation the *thing* is perfected. For there is no *Preparation* of any *Work*, for receiving *Form* by *Artifice*, that happens in an instant, but rather it is successive. Therefore *Form* cannot be given to the *Work*, since the *Preparation* is not made in an *Instant*.

Therefore, being of a most hard and most strong *Composition*, they are also of most difficult *Resolution* <note>dissolution or breaking down</note>; but this is so that the *Inspissation* (or *Thickening*) and *Induration* (or *Hardening*) of them with each other may be made in such a way that they may undergo *Contusion* <note>pounding</note> and *Extension* <note>stretching</note> by *Malleable Compulsion* <note>hammering</note>, and not be broken. By this, nothing else is meant than that in the *Commixtion* <note>mixing</note> of them with each other, their *Viscous Humidity* <note>sticky moisture</note> is preserved by *Successive Decoction* <note>heating or boiling</note> in the *Mine*.

Therefore, most Dear Son, We give you this *General Rule*, namely: that *Inspissation* of any *Humidity* cannot be achieved unless first, an *Exaltation* <note>refinement or raising to a higher state</note> of the *most subtle parts* is made with the *Humidity*; and also, with that same *Humidity*, there is a *Conservation* of the *grosser parts* (if the *Humid* in the *Commixtion* exceeds the *Dry*) and a true *Mixtion* of the *Dry* and *Humid*; so that the *Humidity* may be tempered by the *Dryness*, and the *Dryness* by the *Humidity*, and both become one *Substance*; *Homogeneous in its parts*, temperate between hard and soft, and capable of extension during *Contusion*. But this is not done except by *Diuturnal Mixtion* <note>long-lasting or daily mixing</note> of the viscous *Humidity* and subtle *Earthiness* through their smallest parts, until the *Humid* becomes one with the *Dry*, and the *Dry* one with the *Humid*. *Uniform Substance of the Principles of Nature.*
For if the *Resolution* <note>separation or dissolution</note> of the superfluous *Humidity* from them should be made suddenly (seeing that the *Humid* does not differ from the *Dry* because of the strong *Mixtion* <note>mixture</note> which they have), the *Humidity* of the *Mixtion* would be resolved with the *Dryness*, and so the whole would vanish into *Fume*; nor could the *Humidity* be separated from the *Dryness* in *Resolution*, by reason of the strong *Union* which they have with each other.

Of this, we see a manifest *Experiment* in the *Sublimation of Spirits*; for when a sudden *Resolution* is made in them by *Sublimation*, the *Humid* is not separated from the *Dry*, nor the *Dry* from the *Humid*, being divided into all the *Parts* of their *Mixtion*; but their whole *Substance* ascends, or little of the *Mixture* is dissolved. Therefore, the successive, long-lasting, and equal *Resolution* of the subtle, smoky *Humidity* is the *Cause* of the *Inspissation* <note>thickening</note> of *Metals*. But we also cannot perform this *Inspissation* in this manner; therefore, in this we cannot follow *Nature*, for we cannot imitate *Nature* in all the *Differences* of the *Properties of Action*. Therefore, our *Intention* is not to follow *Nature* in the *Principles*, nor in the *Proportion* of mixable *Elements*, nor in the manner of mixing them with each other, nor in the equation of inspissating (or thickening) *Heat*; all these *Things* are impossible and wholly unknown to us. Therefore, it now remains that we set about refuting the aforementioned *Reasons*

# C H A P. I I I.

## *A Refutation of the Reasons of Men simply denying Art.*

If they say we do not know the *Proportion* of *Elements*, and the way of *Mixing* them with each other, also the *Equation* of *Heat* thickening <note>inspissating</note> *Metals*; and many other *Causes* and consequent *Accidents* of the *Actions* of *Nature*: We grant the same. Yet by reason of this they do not weaken <note>enervate</note> *Our Divine Science*, because they are *Things* we are neither willing nor able to know, any more than they can reach to *Our Work*. But we assume to ourselves another *Principle*, and another *Method* of *Generation* of *Metals*, in which we are able to follow *Nature*.

If they say *Philosophers* and *Princes* of this *World* have desired this *Science* and could not find it, we answer: *They lie*. For some *Princes* (though few) and especially the *Ancient* and *wise Men* found in *Our Time* have (as is manifest) by their *Industry* found out this *Science*; but would never by *Word* or *Writing* reveal the same to such *Men*, because they are unworthy of it. Therefore *they*

He also asserts that wise men and certain princes have indeed discovered this science but keep it secret from the unworthy.</summary>

not seeing anyone who possesses this *Science*, they conceive an *Error* in their *Minds*, and therefore judge that no one has found it. 

Further, if they argue fantastically, asserting our *Impotency*, that we cannot imitate *Nature* even in weak *Mixtures*, such as in the *Mixture* of an *Ass* or *Ox*; therefore [we cannot do so] in the strong: We answer, detecting their manifold *Error*, that there is no necessity, from their way of arguing, for us to grant that our *Art* does not exist; because they strengthen their *Error* and *Fantasy* by arguing from a *Similarity*, or from a *Greater* to a *Lesser*, in which there is no *Necessity*, but only *Contingency*; as in many things. This we also show by another way, demonstrating that they assign no apparent *Similarity* between a weak *Commixture* of *Animals* and the firm and strong *Composition* of *Minerals*. For in *Animals* and other *Living Things*, in which the *Composition* is weak, there is not a perfecting proportion, nor *Miscibles* <note>substances capable of being mixed</note> of proportion, nor *Qualities* of *Miscibles*, nor a *Commixture* which follows from the action and passion of those things with each other, which comes from the aggregation of those first *Qualities*; but there is (according to the opinion of *many*) a *Soul*, which comes from the *Occult Recesses* of *Nature*, as if from a *Quintessence*, or from the *First Mover*. And of this we also speak according to the *opinion* of many, and do not know the *Secret* of it. Therefore, we cannot perform such things as these, although there is a weak *Mixture* in them; because we do not know how

He argues that while living creatures possess a "soul" from the occult recesses of nature, mineral composition follows different principles that can be mastered.</summary>

to infuse the *Perfective*, which is the *Soul*. Yet from this it follows that the defect in us—the fact that we cannot compound or make an *Ox* or a *Goat*—is not on the *part* of the *Mixture*, but through the defect of the *Infusion* of the *Soul*: because, just as we know how to make a weak and weaker *Composition*, so we also know how to make the strong and stronger, imitating the *Way* and *Course* of *Nature* according to our *Artifice*. In *Metals*, there is less *Perfection* than in *Animals*; and their *Perfection* consists more in *Proportion* and *Composition* than in anything else. Therefore, seeing that there is less *Perfection* in them than in those others just mentioned, we can more freely perfect these, but not the others. For the most *High* and *Glorious* GOD has distinguished *Perfections* from each other in many *Forms*. And those things in which the *Composition* (which is according to *Nature*) was weak are endowed by GOD with a greater and more noble *Perfection*, namely, that which is according to the *Soul*: and other things made by him, of a firmer and stronger *Composition*, such as *Stones* and *Minerals*, are endowed with a lesser and more ignoble *Perfection*, namely, that which is from the *Way* of *Mixture*. Therefore, from this it is evident that the *Analogy* of these *men* is not good: for we are not ignorant of how to form an *Ox* or a *Goat* in respect of their *Composition*, but only in respect of their *Perfective Form*. Because *Perfection* in an *Ox* or in a *Goat* is more noble and more hidden than the *Perfection* consisting in a *Metal*.

But if they argue otherwise—that *Species* is not changed into *Species*—we say again that they lie (as they are more accustomed to do than to speak truly about these things). For *Species* is changed into *Species* in this manner: namely, when the *Individual* of one *Species* is changed into the *Individual* of another. We see a *Worm*, both naturally and by natural *Artifice*, turned into a *Fly*, which differs from it in *Species*; and a strangled *Calf* turned into *Bees*; *Wheat* into *Darnel*; and a strangled *Dog* into *Worms*, through the putrefaction of *Ebullition* <note>the state of boiling or bubbling up, here referring to the fermentation of decay</note>. Yet we do not do this ourselves; rather, *Nature*, to whom we act as administrators, does it. Likewise, we also do not alter *Metals*, but *Nature* does, for whom we prepare the *Matter* according to *Art*. For she acts by herself, not us; yet we are her *Administrators*.

And if they argue by another reason and strengthen their own *Sophistical Opinion*, saying: *Nature* perfects *Metals* over thousands of years, but you cannot extend your life that long. We say that *Nature*, acting on her own *Principles* (according to the opinion of *Philosophers*), perfects them in thousands of *Years*; but because we cannot follow those *Principles*, their persuasion determines nothing—whether *Nature* perfects these in a thousand *Years*, or in more or fewer, or in a moment. That we cannot imitate *Nature* in her *Principles*, we have already sufficiently declared in brief in the preceding *Negative Discourse*, and in a more complete speech in the

Therefore it is not necessary to wait for the *Site* of *Stars*, yet it would be profitable; but it is sufficient for *Nature* only to dispose; for she herself being *Wise*, disposes her *Work* by the convenient *Sites* of moveable *Bodies*: Yea, *Nature* cannot perform her own *Motion* without the *Motion* and *Site* of *Things* moveable. Therefore if you dispose the *Artifice* of *Nature*, and consider whatsoever shall fall in from the *Contingents* of this <term>Magistery</term> <note>the alchemical process or mastery of the art</note>, the *Work* will be duly perfected by *Nature*, under a due *Site* convenient for it, without any previous *Consideration*

## CHAPTER IV.

->*Various Opinions of those who suppose*<-
->*the Art to exist.*<-

WE find very many *men* with different *intentions* assuming this *art*. Some indeed affirm that this *art* and magistery <note>the alchemical process or mastery of the art</note> is to be found in *spirits*, but others in *bodies*; some in *salts*, *alums*, *niters*, and *boraxes*, but others in all *vegetable things*. And among all the aforementioned, some judging this *divine magistery* partly well and partly poorly, others altogether badly, leave their *judgment* to *posterity*. Yet from the multiplicity of their *errors*, we have gathered the *truth*; and this has happened to us with difficult and laborious conjectural *caution*, and long and tedious *experience*, with the intervention of great *expenses*; for their *error* has very often disturbed the *disposition* of our *mind* and *reason*, and almost brought us to *despair*. May they therefore be cursed to *eternity*, because they have left to their *posterity blasphemies* and a *curse*, and by their *error* brought the same on *men philosophizing*. For they did not leave...

Therefore We shall treat of it in such *Words* as may not be hidden from the *Wise*, but to *Men* of average *Capacity* it will be most profound, and *Fools* shall be absolutely barred from *Entrance* therein. And for the most part We find either of these *Sects* void of *Reason*.

## C H A P. V.

### *Of the Reasons of Men denying the Art supposed in Sulphur.*

Some, supposing to find this *Art* in *Sulphur*, employ their whole labor in *Sulphur*; and being ignorant of the *Perfection* of the *Preparation*, they leave the *Preparation* itself incomplete; for they conceive that *Cleansing* and *Purifying* only will be a *Preparation* of *Perfection*. But this is done by *Sublimation*, and therefore brought to their *Intention*, as they think; because they judge that *Sublimation* alone in *Sulphur* is the *Perfection* of its *Preparation*; and likewise in its *Companion* <note>compeer</note>, namely *Arsenic*, they are led to a similar *Judgment*. Therefore, coming to *Projection*, which is with the *Intention* of *Alteration*, they see that substance to be burned and vanish, and not to abide long in *Bodies*, and those *Bodies* to be left more unclean than they were before the *Projection* of their *Matter* upon them. Now seeing this *Delusion* in the *Completion* of their *Work*, and that over a long time—whereas before they concluded in their *Minds* that this *Science* was to be found in *Sulphur* only; but now having not found it therein, they argue it is impossible to find it in another *Thing*; why, being not found in

<summary>The author explains that many alchemists fail by incorrectly assuming that simple sublimation of sulphur or arsenic constitutes a perfect preparation. When these substances vanish during projection, the practitioners mistakenly conclude that the entire science of alchemy is impossible.</summary>

this or that, they conclude it is nowhere to be found.

### The Refutation of the Preceding Reasons.

To these We briefly answer and say, that in this they understand little and are less wise, because they suppose *Sulphur* only to be the *Matter of Our Stone*. If this, their *supposition*, were true, they are still deceived in the method of *Preparation*, because they imagine *Sublimation* alone to be sufficient. For they are like a *child* who, from his first *birth* until *old age*, is shut up within a *house*, not thinking the *extent* of the *world* to be extended beyond the *extent* of his *house*, or beyond what he can see with his own *eyes* in the *house*. So they, having not employed their *labor* on many *stones*, could not discern from which *Our Medicine* should be extracted and from which it should not; by abstaining from the latter, they might have spared themselves much *labor* of their *hands*. Therefore, they are deservedly ignorant of what *labor* would be perfecting or not perfecting. But why was their *work* defective? We say it is because they left the burning property and volatility in the *Sulphur*; both of which not only fail to perfect the work, but also dissipate and destroy it.

## CHAP. VI.

### *Regarding the Reasons why Men deny the Art supposed in Arsenic, and their Refutation.*

But others, judging that this *Stone* must necessarily be found in the same, and in its *companion*, *Arsenic*, and being more profoundly intent on the *completion* of the *Work*, do not only cleanse the burning *sulphureity* by a *sublimation*, but also endeavor to remove the *terrestreity*, leaving the *flight* (or *volatility*) within it. These, in a similar way, when they come to *projection*, find it to be a *delusion*; because their *Medicine* does not adhere stably in those very *Bodies*, but successively and bit by bit vanishes, leaving such a *Body* in its former condition. Hence these people also, condemning the *Art*, argue like the former group; and to them We answer as We did to the *first*, affirming the *Art*, and that We know it to exist because We have seen and touched the *truth* of it.

## C H A P.  V I I.

->*The Reasons why Men deny the Art regarding *<term>Sulphur</term>*, *<term>Argentvive</term>*, *<term>Tutia</term>*, *<term>Magnesia</term>*, *<term>Marchasite</term>*, and *<term>Salarmoniac</term>*, with their Refutation.*<-

## CHAP. VIII.

### *The Reasons why Men deny the Art, assuming that Spirits cannot be fixed together with Bodies; and their Refutation.*

There are others also endeavoring to fix *Spirits* <note>volatile substances</note> in *Bodies* <note>metals</note> without any other prior *preparation*. Therefore some of these *Men*, imagining *Tin* or *Jupiter* to be very like *Luna* or *Silver* (differing only in its harsh sound, softness, and very rapid *Liquefaction* <note>melting</note>), believe it is easily melted because of the excess of its *Humidity* <note>moisture or fluidity</note>; and soft, because of the *Fugitive Substance* <note>volatile substance</note> of *Argentvive* <note>Quicksilver or Mercury</note> in it, residing in the *Parts* that possess that harsh *Sound* <note>the "tin-cry" or crackling noise of tin</note>; they *Calcine* <note>burn to a powder</note> the same, keeping it in such a *Fire* as it can endure, until it becomes *White* in its *Calx*, which they afterward attempted to reduce, but could not;

## CHAP. X.

### *The Reasons why Men deny the Art supposed in Black Lead or Saturn.*

The same *Delusion* they also find in Black *Lead*, or *Saturn*; except only that it does not break *Bodies*, and is sooner reduced to a *Calx* <note>A powder or oxide produced by heating a metal</note> than *Jupiter* <note>Tin</note>. Yet they cannot remove its *Livid Color* <note>Bluish-gray</note>, because they are ignorant of how to do so; therefore they cannot whiten it with good *Dealbation* <note>Whitening or purification</note>; nor could they ever, by their *Fantasy*, so stably associate it with stable *Bodies* that it would not, by the strong *Expression of Fire* <note>Application of intense heat</note>, recede from the *Mixture*. And indeed *Lead* does exceedingly deceive them (supposing that in its *Preparation* much is fixed <note>Rendered solid or non-volatile</note>, and that this *Science* can be found in no metal but it) because, after two *Reductions* from its *Calx*, it receives no further *Hardening*, but rather greater *softness* than it had at first; and in other *Differences* <note>Qualities or characteristics</note> likewise, they do not see it improved. Therefore when they, thinking in it

# C H A P. II.

## *The Reasons Why Men Deny the Art Based on the Mixing of Hard Bodies with Hard, and Soft with Soft.*

But others combine hard *Bodies* with hard, and soft with soft, for the sake of *Convenience*, wishing for *Bodies* to be transmuted into one another and to effect transmutation; but they could not achieve this because of their *Ignorance*. For they, mixing *Sol* <note>Gold</note> or *Luna* <note>Silver</note> with *Venus* <note>Copper</note> or with any other of the *Metals*, could not transmute them into *Gold* or *Silver* with a stable *Transmutation*; instead, they found that every one of them, through the intense application of *Fire*, was separated from the *Mixture* and either burned or reduced to its former *Nature*. Yet some of these *Bodies* endure in the *Mixture*, while others do not, as is well known to us. Therefore, these *Delusions*, arising through *Ignorance*, cause such *Men* to despair of the *Art* and argue that it does not exist.

<meta>catchword: C H A P.</meta>

<summary>The author explains that many people deny the existence of alchemy because their ignorant attempts to mix metals result in the components separating or burning under heat. Failing to achieve a stable transmutation between hard and soft bodies, they conclude the entire science is a delusion.</summary>

## C H A P. XII.

### *The Reasons why Men deny the supposed Art, regarding the Mixing of Hard Bodies with Soft, and of Perfect Bodies with the Imperfect.*

**O**thers, searching more intimately and profoundly, have thought and been persuaded that they could find a way to cause Hard Bodies united with Soft to endure firmly, and Imperfect Bodies united with the Perfect to be reduced to *Perfection*; and that generally they would be transmuted into one another, and transmute with a firm transmutation. Therefore they would seek to find the Affinity and Similitude of them, both by *Medicines* and by the Administration of *Fire*, thinning the *Gross* (such as *Venus* <note>Copper</note> and *Mars* <note>Iron</note>) and thickening the *Subtle* (as is *Jupiter* <note>Tin</note> and its like). And some of them, believing they could complete this administration, were deluded in the Mixing of these *Hard Bodies* with the *Soft*, either because one made the other altogether breakable; or else the Soft was not at all altered by the Hard, or the Hard was in no way altered by the Soft. Thus they, not finding the *Compatibility*, therefore denied the *Art* to exist.

<summary>The author explains that many people reject alchemy because they fail to successfully combine hard and soft or perfect and imperfect metals. Their inability to find the proper affinity between these substances leads to brittle results or no change at all, causing them to believe the science is impossible.</summary>

### C H A P. XIII.

->*The Reasons why Men deny the supposed Art, regarding the Extraction of the Soul or the Regulation of Fire.*<-

But others, inspecting even more intimately and profoundly, wanted to alter *Bodies* through the extraction of their *Soul*, and with that extracted *Soul*, to alter all other things as well. Yet the *experience* of those *men* could not achieve that; they were deluded in their *intentions* and consequently judged the *Art* impossible to discover. And others, endeavoring to perfect *Bodies* by *Fire* alone, were deluded in their *opinion* because they did not know how to achieve that result. These people also think the *Art* does not exist. To all of these, we answer as we did to the first group.

---

### The Third Part of this First Book; Of Natural Principles, and their Effect.

### CHAP. I.

### Of the *Natural Principles* of *Metallic Bodies*, according to the Opinion of the *Ancients*.

WE now notify you that (according to the opinion of the ancients who were of our *Sect*, studious of *Art*) *Natural Principles* in the *Work* of *Nature* are a *Fetid Spirit* <note>Or "Fetent Spirit"; a sulfurous or foul-smelling substance</note> and *Living Water*, which is also named *Dry Water*. These we grant, and thus define the *Fetid Spirit*. It is white in *Occulto* <note>In the hidden or internal state</note>, and Red and Black on either side in the *Magistery* <note>The master-process or practice of alchemy</note> of this *Work*; but in *Manifesto* <note>In the open or manifest state</note>, on either side, tending toward *Redness*. Therefore, in brief,

Yet we must so far enlarge and expand our *discourse* as to deliver a specific *chapter* for each individual *natural principle*. In general, we now say that every one of these is of a most strong *composition* and uniform *substance*; and that is because the *earthy* parts in them are, through their smallest particles, united with the *airy*, *watery*, and *fiery*; so that in *resolution*, none of them can be separated, but each with all and every one is dissolved, by reason of the strong *union* which they have with each other in their smallest particles; and that slowly, in the *mineral bowels* of the *earth*, condensed by *heat*, multiplied, and (according to the proper *course* of *nature*) equalized to the *requirement* of their *essence*, according to the *opinion* of *certain ancient philosophers*.

# C H A P. III.

## *The Division of what is to be spoken, touching the Three Principles, namely Sulphur, Arsenic, and Argentvive.*

NOW, having finished our *Universal Discourse* of the *Natural Principles* of *Metals*; it remains that we here give a specific *Chapter* to each one of the *Principles*. Therefore, since there are **Three**, *namely Sulphur, Arsenic, and Argentvive*; the first shall be of *Sulphur*, the second of *Arsenic*, and the third of *Argentvive*. Afterward, for each of the *Metals*, which are the *Effects* of these *Principles*, we give a specific *Chapter*, according to what it is from the *Work* of *Nature*. This being done, we, moving on to those things which are the Fundamentals of this *Magistery*, and to their *Operations*, will assign the causes of all these.

# C H A P. I V.

## *Of Sulphur.*

THEREFORE we say, that *Sulphur* is a fatness of the *Earth*, thickened by temperate *Decoction* in the *Mine* of the *Earth*, until it is

## C H A P. V.

### Of *Arsenic*.

IT now remains that we at present speak of *Arsenic*. We say it is of a subtle *Matter*, and similar to *Sulphur*; therefore it does not need to be defined any differently than *Sulphur*. But it is distinguished from *Sulphur* in this, namely: because it easily produces a *Tincture* of *Whiteness*, but a *Tincture* of *Redness* with great difficulty; whereas *Sulphur* produces *Whiteness* with great difficulty, but *Redness* easily. Of *Sulphur* and *Arsenic* there are two *Kinds*, namely: *Citrine* <note>lemon-yellow</note> and *Red*, which are useful to this *Art*; but the many other *Kinds* are not. *Arsenic* is fixed just like *Sulphur*; but the *Sublimation* of either is best performed from the *Calx* <note>the powdery residue left after a metal is heated</note> of *Metals*. Yet *Sulphur* and *Arsenic* are not the perfecting *Matter* of this *Work*: for they are not complete enough for *Perfection*; yet they may be a help toward *Perfection* in this case. But the *Lucid*, *Scaly*, and *Scissile* <note>easily split or layered</note> types must be taken.

## CHAP. VI.

### Of *Argent-vive*, or *Mercury*.

*Argent-vive*, which is also called *Mercury* by the *ancients*, is a viscous *water* in the *bowels* of the *earth*, united by a very temperate *heat*, in a total *union* through its smallest parts, with the substance of white, subtle *earth*, until the *moist* is tempered by the *dry*, and the *dry* by the *moist*, equally. Therefore, it easily runs upon a flat *surface* because of its *watery moisture*; but it does not stick, even though it has a viscous *moisture*, because of the *dryness* of that which tempers it and does not permit it to stick. It is also (as some say) the *matter of metals* along with *sulphur*. And it easily adheres to three *minerals*, namely: to *Saturn* <note>lead</note>, *Jupiter* <note>tin</note>, and *Sol* <note>gold</note>, but to *Luna* <note>silver</note> with more difficulty. To *Venus* <note>copper</note> with more difficulty than to *Luna*; but to *Mars* <note>iron</note> not at all, unless by *artifice* <note>skillful chemical process</note>. Therefore, from this you may gather a very great *secret*. For it is friendly and pleasing to *metals*, and is the *medium for joining tinctures*; and nothing is submerged in *argent-vive* unless it is *Sol*. Yet *Jupiter* and *Saturn*, *Luna* and *Venus* are dissolved and mixed by it; and without it, none of the *metals* can be gilded. It is *fixed*, and it is a *tincture of redness* of most exuberant

It explains how Mercury interacts with various metals like gold, lead, and tin, highlighting its role as a essential medium for gilding and joining tinctures.</summary>
<keywords>Argent-vive, Mercury, alchemy, metals, Saturn, Jupiter, Sol, Luna, Venus, Mars, Tincture, gilding</keywords>

->CHAPTER VII.<-

## Of the Effects of the Principles of Nature, which are Metallic Bodies.

WE now speak of *metallic bodies*, which are the *effects* of these *principles of nature*. These are six in number, *namely* <note>v.z. is an abbreviation for videlicet</note>: *gold*, *silver*, *lead*, *tin*, *copper*, and *iron*. Therefore we say, a *metal* is a mineral fusible *body*, extensible in all *dimensions* under the *hammer*. But a *metal* is (as we said) of a dense *substance*, and of most strong and firm *composition*. And *metals* have great affinity with each other, yet the perfect does not perfect the diminished by its *mixture*. For if *gold* is mixed in fusion with *lead*, this *lead* does not become *gold*, but vanishes from the *mixture* and is burnt; the *gold* in the meanwhile stands the *trial*. So likewise, in the instance of the others, it happens according to the common *course*. But, according to our *magistery* <note>alchemical practice or mastery</note>, the *perfect* helps the *imperfect*; and the *imperfect*, in our *magistery*, by itself is *perfected*, without the administration

# CHAPTER VIII.

## Of Sol, or Gold.

WE have already given you, in a *General Chapter*, the *Sum* of the *Intention* of *Metals*; and here we now intend to make a special *Declaration* of each one. And first of *Gold*. We say, *Gold* is a *Metallic Body*, *Citrine* <note>lemon-yellow in color</note>, ponderous, mute, fulgid <note>shining or bright</note>, equally digested in the *Bowels* of the *Earth*, and very long washed with *Mineral Water*; under the *Hammer* extensible, fusible, and sustaining the *Trial* of the *Cupel* <note>a shallow, porous cup used in refining precious metals</note>, and *Cement* <note>a process for purifying gold by heating it with other substances</note>. According to this definition, you may conclude that nothing is true *Gold*, unless it has all the *Causes* and *Differences* of the definition of *Gold*. Yet, whatever *Metal* is radically *Citrine*, and brings to *Equality*, and cleanses, it makes *Gold* of every kind of *Metals*. Therefore, we consider by the *Work* of *Nature*, and discern that *Copper* may be changed into *Gold* by *Artifice*. For we see in *Copper Mines*, a certain *Water* which flows out, and carries with it

<summary>This page defines Gold as a dense, yellow, and durable metallic body formed naturally in the earth and capable of withstanding rigorous purification processes. thin *scales of copper*, which (by a continual and long-continued course) it washes and cleanses. But after such *water* ceases to flow, we find these thin *scales* with the dry *sand*, in three years' time to be digested with the *heat* of the *sun*; and among these *scales* the purest *gold* is found. Therefore, we judge those *scales* were cleansed by the benefit of the *water*, but were equally digested by heat of the *sun*, in the *dryness* of the *sand*, and so brought to *equality*. Therefore, imitating *nature* as far as we can, we likewise alter; yet in this we cannot follow *nature*.

Also *gold* is the most precious of *metals*, and it is the *tincture of redness*; because it tinges and transforms every *body*. It is calcined and dissolved without profit, and is a *medicine* rejoicing and conserving the *body* in *youth*. It is most easily broken with *mercury*, and by the odor of *lead*. There is not any *body* that in act more agrees with it in *substance* than *Jupiter* <note>tin</note> and *Luna* <note>silver</note>: but in *weight*, *deafness* <note>lack of resonance</note>, and *putrescibility* <note>the quality of being prone to decay or oxidation</note>, *Saturn* <note>lead</note>; in *color* *Venus* <note>copper</note>; in *potency* indeed *Venus* is closer to *Luna* than *Jupiter*, and then *Saturn*: but lastly *Mars* <note>iron</note>. And this is one of the *secrets of nature*. Likewise *spirits* are mixed with it and fixed by it, but not without very great *ingenuity*, which does not come to an *artificer* of a stiff neck <note>a stubborn or inflexible person</note>.

## C H A P. I X.
### Of Luna, or Silver.

Having presented the *Chapter* on *Sol* <note>gold</note>, we come now to speak of *Luna*, commonly called *Silver*. Therefore, we say, *silver* is a *metallic body*, white with pure whiteness, clean, hard, sounding <note>resonant</note>, very durable in the *cupel* <note>a refining vessel</note>, extensible under the *hammer*, and fusible. And it is the *tincture* of whiteness, and it hardens *tin* by *artifice* <note>skillful means or chemical process</note>, and converts it to itself; and it is mixed with *Sol*, and does not break; but in the *examination* <note>testing or assaying</note> it does not endure without *artifice*. He who knows how to further refine it, and after refining, to thicken and fix it in association with *gold*; it remains with it in the *test*, and will in no way forsake it. Being placed over the fumes of sharp things, such as *vinegar*, *sal ammoniac*, etc., it will take on a wonderful *celestial color*. And it is a noble *body*, but it lacks the *nobility* of *gold*; and its *ore* is found in a distinct state: but it often has an *ore* mixed with other *bodies*, and that *silver* is not so noble. It is likewise dissolved and calcined with great *labor* and no *profit*.

## C H A P. X.

### Of Saturn, or Lead.

OF *Lead* we likewise treat, and say, *Lead* is a *Metallic Body*, bluish-gray, earthy, heavy, dull, partaking of a little *Whiteness* with much paleness, refusing the *Cineritium* <note>the process of cupellation or refining in ashes</note> and *Cement* <note>a dry refining process using corrosive agents and heat</note>, easily extensible in all its dimensions with small compression, and readily melted without *Ignition* <note>heating to the point of glowing</note>. Yet some foolish men imagine and say that *Lead* in its own *Nature* is very close to *Gold*. But because they are stubborn and void of all *Reason*, they cannot conceive of the *Truth* of things most subtle as it is in itself, but judge them according to their *Senses*. And because they see it heavy, and dull, and not prone to rot, they believe it to be very near in *Property* to *Sol* <note>gold</note>; but this is completely erroneous, as shall be clearly proved by us at length in the following. Also, *Lead* has much of an *Earthy Substance*; therefore it is washed and, by a *Lavament* <note>a washing or cleansing process</note>, turned into *Tin*. Hence it is manifest that *Tin* is more similar to the *Perfect* <note>the noble metals, gold and silver</note>. *Lead* is in like manner burnt and made into *Minium* <note>red lead</note>; and it is placed over the *Vapors* of *Vinegar* and made into *Ceruss* <note>white lead</note>. And although it is not very close to *Perfection*,

### C H A P. X I.

And *Jupiter* adheres closely to *Sol* and *Luna*, and therefore does not easily recede

## CHAP. XII.

Our intended discourse now is of *Venus*,
