# CHAP. XIII.
## *Of Mars, or Iron.*

But the *description* of *Mars*, and the whole *secret* thereof, is from the *work* of *nature*; because it is a *metallic body*, very livid, a little red, partaking of *whiteness*, not pure, withstanding *ignition*, fusible though not with a proper flow, extensible under the *hammer*, and ringing loudly. But *Mars* is hard to handle by reason of the *difficulty* of its melting; which, if it be made to flow by a *medicine* changing its *nature*, is joined to *Sol* <note>gold</note> and *Luna* <note>silver</note>, and is not separated by *examination* <note>trial</note> without great *industry*. But if it is prepared, it is joined and not separated by any *artifice*, provided the *nature* of that *fixation* is not changed by it, and only the *impurity* of the *Mars* is removed. Therefore, it is easily a *tincture* of *redness*, but only with difficulty a tincture of *whiteness*. And when it is joined, it is not altered, nor does it change

Therefore, among all *bodies*, *Jupiter* <note>tin</note> is more splendidly and more clearly, more brightly and more perfectly transformed into a *solar* <note>gold</note>, or *lunar* <note>silver</note> *body*. But the *work* of it is of long labor, though easy to handle. Next to *Jupiter*, *Venus* <note>copper</note> is chosen, of more difficult handling, but of shorter labor than *Jupiter*. Next after *Venus*, *Saturn* <note>lead</note> has a diminished *perfection* in *transmutation*; it is easy to handle, but of most tedious labor. Yet *Mars* <note>iron</note>, among all the *bodies*, is of least *perfection* in *transmutation*, most difficult to handle, and of exceeding long labor. Therefore, whatever *bodies* are more remote from swiftness of *liquefaction* <note>melting</note>, they are found to be of more difficult handling in the *work* of *transmutation*. Of this kind are *Venus* and *Mars*; those that are further from it are more difficult, and those that are most remote are the most difficult. Also those *bodies* which partake of greater *lividness* <note>discoloration</note> and *infection* of the *earth*, are likewise found to be of greater *labor*, and less *perfection*.

But whatever *diversities* of *perfections* were a little before determined by us, are found in the *artifice* of the *Lesser*, or *Middle Work*; yet in the *Greater Work* all *bodies* are of one *perfection*, but not all of one handling or labor. But now, according as we promised, it is necessary to supply the deficiency of this *art* by setting down all the *principles* of this *magistery*, in the last part of this our *First Book*; and to demonstrate the *perfection* we have seen, with its *causes*, according to its *requirements*.

## The Fourth Part of this First Book, concerning the Artificial Principles of this Art.

---

### C H A P. I.

### *The Division of Things to be spoken of in this Part, with an introduction to the Perfection to be treated of in the Second Book.*

There are two things that are to be determined, namely the *principles* of this *magistery*, and the *perfection* of the same. The

<meta>catchword: Principles</meta>

<summary>The author concludes the discussion on the natural principles of bodies and introduces the final part of the first book, which will cover artificial principles and the nature of alchemical perfection.</summary>
<keywords>natural principles, occult things, magistery, perfection, artificial principles, First Book, Second Book</keywords>

--- Page 100 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction to the "Artificial Principles" of the alchemical magistery, transitioning from the general concept of these principles to a specific list of operations and the considerations necessary to achieve perfection in the work.</meta>

( 84 )

*Principles* of this *Art* are the *Ways* or *Methods* of its *Operations*, to which the *Artist* applies himself in the *Work* of this *Magistery*. These *Ways* are indeed diverse in themselves. For one *Way* is *Sublimation*, and *Descension* another; and *Distillation* is also one *Way*, *Calcination* another, *Solution* another, and *Coagulation* another: but the seventh *Way* is *Fixation*, and the eighth *Ceration*. Of all these, *We* purpose to give individual *Declarations*.

*Perfection* consists of those *Things*, and from the *Considerations* of those by which it is attained; and from the *Consideration* of things helping; and from the *Consideration* of that thing which finally perfects; and that by which it is known, whether the *Magistery* was in *Perfection* or not. The *Consideration* of those *Things*, by which *We* come to the *Completion* <note>Originally "Complement"</note> of the *Work*, is the *Consideration* of the *Substance* manifest, and of manifest *Colors*, and of the *Weight* in every of those *Bodies* to be changed, and of those *Bodies* that are not changed from the *Radix* <note>Root</note> of their *Nature*, without that *Artifice*; and the *Consideration* of those likewise, in the *Radix* of their *Nature*, with the *Artifice*; and the *Consideration* of the *Principles* of *Bodies*, according to their *Profound*, *Occult*, and *Manifest*; and according to their *Nature* without *Artifice*, and likewise with *Artifice*. For, if *Bodies*, and their *Principles*, are not known in the *Profound* and *Manifest* of their *Nature*, with *Artifice*, and without, what is superfluous, and

### CHAP. II.

->*Of Sublimation, and why it was invented.*<-

**T**herefore, pursuing our purpose, we speak of *Sublimation*. The *Cause* for its *Invention* was because our *Ancestors* could not—nor can we, nor shall those who come after us—find anything that can be united with *Bodies* except for *Spirits only*; or anything that can contain within itself the *Nature* of both *Body* and *Spirit*. And we see these, when cast upon *Bodies* (without *Mundation* <note>Cleansing or purification</note>, or cleansing of them), either fail to give perfect *Colors* or else totally corrupt, burn, blacken, and defile them. This occurs according to the variety of these same *Spirits*. For some are burning, such as *Sulphur*, *Arsenic*, and *Marcasite*; and these indeed totally corrupt. Others do not burn, such as every kind of *Tutia* <note>Tutty, or zinc oxide</note>; yet these give imperfect *Colors*, and they do so for a twofold *Cause*. One is because the burning oiliness <note>Adustive unctuosity</note> of *Sulphur* (of the property of which it

<summary>This page explains that Sublimation was invented to purify "Spirits" so they can be united with "Bodies" without causing corruption or discoloration. It distinguishes between spirits that burn and corrupt, like sulfur and arsenic, and those that do not burn but still produce imperfect results.</summary>
<keywords>Sublimation, Spirits, Bodies, Mundation, Sulphur, Arsenic, Marcasite, Tutia, adustive unctuosity</keywords>

--- Page 103 ---
<meta>This page continues the explanation of Sublimation, specifically addressing the causes of impurity in "Spirits" and how the process of sublimation removes them to allow for the perfection of "Bodies."</meta>

is) which is easily inflamed, and by inflammation blackened, and consequently blackens, is not removed or taken away from them. But the other *Cause* is *Earthiness*, which likewise is not separated from them. For in these, in which a perfect *Color* is not given, *Earthiness* is a *Cause* making it livid. Also *Adustion* <note>Burning or scorching</note> may create a livid *Color*.

Therefore, we were constrained to cleanse these from their burning *Unctuosity* <note>Oily or fatty quality</note>, and from the *Earthy Superfluity* <note>Excess or waste matter</note>, which they all have. And this we could effect by no *Magistery* <note>Masterly alchemical process</note>, but by *Sublimation* only. For when the *Fire* elevates, it always elevates the more subtle parts; therefore it does not stir the more *Gross*. Hence it is manifest that *Spirits* are cleansed from their *Earthiness* by *Sublimation*; which *Terrestriety* <note>Earthly nature</note> impeded *Ingress* <note>Entry or penetration</note>, and also gave an impure *Color*. But being sublimed, as *Experience makes* sufficiently manifest to your *Sight*, they are freed from that *Impurity*. For you see them more splendid, and more pervious <note>Capable of being penetrated</note>, and more easily to enter and penetrate the *Density* of *Bodies*, and not to impress a foul *Color*, as before. Also that *Adustion* may be taken away by *Sublimation* is manifest by *Experiment*: for *Arsenic*, which before its *Sublimation* was evil and prone to *Adustion*, after its *Sublimation* does not allow itself to be inflamed, but only recedes without *Inflammation*; and the same you may find in *Sulphur*, if you will make a *Trial*. And because, in no other *Things* than in *Spi-*

<summary>This page explains that Sublimation is necessary to remove "earthiness" and "burning unctuosity" from alchemical spirits, which otherwise cause discoloration and corruption. By using fire to elevate only the subtle parts, spirits like arsenic and sulfur are purified, becoming more penetrative and resistant to burning.</summary>
<keywords>Sublimation, Spirits, Earthiness, Adustion, Arsenic, Sulphur, Unctuosity, Terrestriety, purification, alchemical magistery</keywords>

Therefore, there was a necessary *Cause* for the *Invention* of this *Sublimation*; the whole *Order* of which we purpose to declare without *Diminution* <note>Reduction or omission</note>.

### CHAP. III.

#### *What Sublimation is, and of the Degrees of Fire to be observed in it.*

Wherefore we say, *Sublimation* is the *Elevation* of a dry *Thing* by *Fire*, with adherence to its *Vessel*. But *Sublimation* is diversely made, according to the *Diversity* of *Spirits* to be sublimed. For the *Sublimation* of some is made with strong *Ignition* <note>Intense heating or setting on fire</note>, of others with moderate heat, and of some with a remiss <note>Gentle or low</note> heat of *Fire*. Therefore, when *Arsenic* or *Sulphur* are to be sublimed, their *Sublimation* must necessarily be made by remiss *Fire*: because they, having their most subtle parts uniformly conjoined with the *Gross*, their whole *Substance* would ascend without any Purification—indeed, blackened and burned. Therefore, so that the *Artificer* <note>Alchemist or skilled craftsman</note> may separate the unclean *Earthy*

<summary>This page defines sublimation as the elevation of dry substances by fire and explains that different substances, such as arsenic and sulphur, require different intensities of heat to be properly purified. It notes that using too much heat on certain spirits will cause the entire substance to ascend without being cleansed of its impurities.</summary>
<keywords>sublimation, arsenic, sulphur, fire degrees, ignition, purification of spirits, alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 105 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions on the process of sublimation, specifically focusing on how to manage the "Feces" (dregs) and the specific degrees of heat required for purification.</meta>

*Substance*, he has a necessity to find out the *Dispositions* of two *kinds*, namely the *proportion* of the *fire*, and *cleansing* <note>Mundification</note> by mixing with the *dregs* <note>Feces</note>: because mixing with the *dregs* captures the *gross parts* and holds them down at the bottom of the *sublimatory* <note>Vessel used for sublimation</note>, not allowing them to ascend.

From this it is also necessary that the *artificer* apply a threefold *degree of fire* to his *sublimation*: One proportioned in such a way that only the *altered*, *cleaner*, and more *lucid* parts may ascend; until he clearly sees by this that they are cleansed from their *earthy filth* <note>Feculency</note>. The second *degree* is that whatever pure *essence* remains in the *dregs* may be sublimed with a greater force of *fire*, namely by heating the *bottom of the vessel* and the *dregs* within until they glow <note>Ignition</note>, which can be seen by the eye. The third *degree of fire* is that a very weak *fire* be applied to the *sublimate* (once separated from the *dregs*), so that hardly any of it ascends except for the most subtle part, which is of no value in our *work*; for this is the part through which *burning* <note>Adustion</note> occurs in *sulphurs*.

Therefore, the entire *purpose* of *sublimation* is that the *earthiness* of the *sublimate* is removed by a proper administration of *fire*, as is the most subtle and smoky part of it, which causes *burning* <note>Adustion</note>, with *corruption*,

<summary>The author explains that sublimation requires three distinct levels of fire to separate the pure essence from the heavy dregs and the volatile, unwanted parts that cause burning. By controlling the heat, the alchemist ensures that only the clean, useful substance is collected while the impurities remain at the bottom or are discarded.</summary>
<keywords>sublimation, three degrees of fire, dregs, mundification, ignition, adustion, alchemical process, purification</keywords>

ruption, being cast away, to us may be left that *Part*, which consists in *Equality*, which makes simple *fusion* upon the *fire*, and without any *burning* <note>Adustion</note> flying from the *fire*, without *inflammation* of it. That what is most subtle is *combustible* <note>Adustive</note>, is proved by most evident *arguments*. For *fire* converts to its own *nature* every one of those things which has an affinity to it; because it has an affinity to every combustible thing, and to the subtle combustible, it has a greater affinity; and yet more affinity to what is more subtle: therefore also most affinity to what is most subtle. Likewise, the same is proved by *experience*, because *sulphur* or *arsenic* not sublimed, is most swiftly inflamed; but of the two, *sulphur* more easily. Yet either, being sublimed, is not directly inflamed, but flies away, and is thinned <note>extenuated</note> without inflammation; yet with a preceding *fusion*. By these therefore it is manifest that our *discourse* is most true,

<summary>The author argues that substances with the greatest subtlety have the highest affinity for fire and are therefore the most prone to burning. By using sublimation, materials like sulphur and arsenic are refined so they can undergo fusion and evaporate without catching fire.</summary>

### C H A P. IV.

*Of the* Feces <note>residue</note> *of* Metallic Bodies, *to be added to* Spirits *in their* Sublimation; *and of the* Quantity *and* Quality *of them.*

BUT the *proof* of the *application* of *feces*, with their *proportion*, is that such *matter* be chosen with which the *spirits* to be sublimed may best agree, and wherewith they may be more profoundly mixed: because that *matter* with which they are more united is more potent in the *retention* of the *feces* of the *spirits* to be sublimed than that with which they are not so united. The *proof* of this is rational enough and manifest. But the *proof* that the *addition* of *feces* is necessary is because *sulfur* or *arsenic* to be sublimed, if they are not joined with the *feces* of some fixed *thing*, would necessarily ascend with their whole *substance*, not cleansed. And they who are experienced in *sublimation* know by *experience* that we speak the truth. Also the *proof* that the *application* of such *feces* is necessary (with which *things* to be sublimed may agree and be united intimately) is because if the *feces* be not mixed <note>permitted</note> with them through their least

Because *glass* only, and its like (lacking *pores*), is able to prevent *spirits* from *escaping*, so that they are not driven out by the *fire*; but no other *material* is fit: because through the *pores* of them the *spirits* are gradually diminished, and

### CHAP. VIII.

#### Of the Sublimation of Mercury and Argentvive.

Now we will determine the whole *intention* of the *sublimation* of *argentvive* <note>quicksilver</note>. This *work* is completed when its *terrestreity* <note>earthiness</note> is highly purified and its *aquosity* <note>wateriness</note> wholly removed. For we are excused from the labor of removing its *adustion*, because it has none. Therefore, we say that the *ingenuity* of separating its superfluous *earth* is to mix it with *things* with which it has no affinity, and often to repeat the *sublimation* of it from them. Of this kind are *talc*, and the *calx* <note>powdered residue after heating</note> of *eggshells*, and of *white marble*. Likewise

And you find this true by *experience* because, when you sublime it from *tin* or *lead*, you find it (after *sublimation*) infected with *blackness*. Therefore, its *sublimation* is better performed using those *things* which do not agree with it; but it would be better performed using *things* with which it does agree, if they did not have *sulphureity*. ## CHAPTER IX.

### Concerning the Sublimation of Marcasite.

---

Therefore, since the sum of the *intention* of the *sublimation* of *argentvive* <note>quicksilver or mercury</note> has been sufficiently declared, we now come to the *sublimation* of *marcasite*; of which there are two ways. One is performed without *ignition* <note>heating to the point of glowing</note>, the other with *ignition*; and that is because it has a twofold *substance*: namely, one part is *sulfur*, pure in its nature; the other is *argentvive*, mortified. The first is useful as *sulfur*; the second is useful as *argentvive* mortified and moderately prepared. Therefore we take this last, because by it we are excused from the former *argentvive* and the labor of mortifying it.

The entire *way* of *sublimation* of this *subject* is that it be ground to *powder* and put into an *aludel* <note>a pear-shaped alchemical pot used for sublimation</note>, and its *sulfur* be *sublimed* without *ignition*; always, and very often, removing what is *sublimed* for the aforesaid *reason*; and afterward increasing the force of the *fire* until the *ignition* of the *aludel*. And the first *sublimation* of *marcasite* must be made in a *vessel* of *sublimation* and continued so long until the *sulfur* is separated; the *process* being successively and orderly continued until it is manifest that what was
in

For when its entire *sulfur* is elevated, you will see its *color* changed into a most pure *white*, mixed with a very clear, pleasant, and *celestine color* <note>sky blue</note>. You will also prove this in another way, because whatever is of the *nature* of *sulfur* will burn and produce a *flame*, like *sulfur*. But what is *sublimed* second, after that first *sublimate*, will neither be inflamed nor show any *properties* of *sulfur*, but rather those of *argentvive* <note>mercury</note> mortified in the *reiteration* <note>repetition</note> of *sublimation*.

## CHAPTER XI.

### *Of the Sublimation of Magnesia and Tutty : Also of Imperfect Bodies, and of the Addition of Matter elevating them.*

The purpose of the sublimation of *Magnesia* <note>a mineral substance, often referring to manganese ore or a base for the philosopher's stone</note> and *Tutty* <note>zinc oxide</note> is the same as the *purpose* of the last sublimation of *marcasite* <note>iron pyrite or similar metallic minerals</note>. For all of these cannot be sublimed without *ignition* <note>heating to the point of glowing</note>. Therefore, they all have one *purpose*, with the same *causes* and the same *experiments*; and that has one *general procedure*. This is because it necessarily happens that whatever things are sublimed with *ignition* must be sublimed without *residue*; for in themselves they have enough—indeed, too much—*residue*. The sign of this is the difficulty of their *sublimation*.

Likewise, all *bodies* <note>metals</note> lacking *perfection* are sublimed in the same *procedure*, and there is no difference or diversity, except that for *bodies* the *fire* of *sublimation* must be more intense than for *Magnesia*, *marcasite*, and *Tutty*. And so likewise, *bodies* in their *sublimations* are not varied, except that some

But there is one special *Consideration*, found good through experience in the *Sublimation* of *Bodies*; and that is, that no large *Quantity* of the *Body* to be sublimed should be put into the *Bottom* of the *Vessel* at once; because a great abundance of *Matter* hinders *Sublimation*. Also, the *Bottom* of the *Sublimatory* <note>a vessel used for sublimation</note> should be flat, not concave; so that the *Body*, spread equally and thinly upon the *Bottom*, may be elevated equally and significantly in all its *Parts*.

Bodies requiring the *Admixtion* <note>admixture or mixing</note> of elevating *Matters* are *Venus* <note>the alchemical name for copper</note> and *Mars* <note>the alchemical name for iron</note>, because of the slowness of their *Fusion* <note>melting</note>. Therefore, *Venus* needs *Tutia* <note>zinc oxide</note>, and *Mars* needs *Arsenic*; and with these they are easily elevated, because they mostly agree with them. Therefore, after *Consideration* of them, let *Sublimation* be performed as it is with *Tutia*, and in things like it in *Sublimation*; and let their *Sublimation* be arranged in the same *Order*, with its *Causes* and *Experiences*.

<summary>This page describes best practices for the sublimation of metals, recommending flat-bottomed vessels and small quantities for even heating. ### C H A P. XII.

## *Of Descension, and the way of Purifying by Pastilles.*

The Intentions of *Sublimation*, with all their *Causes*, being already declared, it remains, that We now show the way of *Descension*, with its *Causes*, and determinate and complete Order likewise. For there was a threefold *Cause* of its *Invention*. One, that when any *Matter* is included in that *Vessel*, which is called a *Chemical Descensory*, after its *Fusion* it may descend through the *Hole* thereof, and by its *Descent* We be assured that it has admitted *Fluxing*. Another *Cause*, that weak *Bodies* may by it be preserved from *Combustion*, after *Reduction* from their *Calxes* <note>the powdery remains or oxides of a metal after being burnt</note>. For when We attempt to reduce weak *Bodies* from their *Calxes*, we cannot reduce all their whole *Substance* at one time. Therefore, if that *Part*, which is first reduced into *Body*, should expect the *Reduction* of the whole, a great *Quantity* of it would vanish by the *Fire*. Wherefore it was necessarily devised, that one part, as soon as reduced, might be taken from the *Fire*. And this is done by a *Descensory*. The third *Cause* of the *Invention* of it, was the *Depuration of Bodies* <note>purification of substances</note> from every thing

In order to do this, we say that the *Form* of it must be such that its *Bottom* is pointed, and its sides are without *Roughness*, ending equally at the aforementioned *Acuity*, or *Point* of the *Bottom*. Its *Cover* (if it needs one) must be made in the likeness of a plain or flat *Dish* and be well-fitted to it. The *Vessel* and its *Cover* must be made of good and firm *Earth*, not easily cracking in the *Fire*. Then, covering the *Vessel* and luting <note>sealing a joint with clay or cement to make it airtight</note> the *Juncture*, set it within a *Fire* of *Coals* and blow it until the whole *Matter* descends into a subjacent <note>lying or situated underneath</note> *Vessel*. 

Yet (if the *Matter* is difficult to *Fuse*), it may be put upon a flat *Table*, or one of small *Concavity*, from which it may easily descend by tilting the *Head* of the *Descensory* when it is in *Flux*. For by this, *Bodies* are purified. But they are better purified by *Pastills* <note>small tablets or pellets made of the substance</note>, which method of *Purification* is the same as the method of *Purifying* by the *Descensory*. ## CHAP. IV.

### Of Calcination, *as well of Bodies as of Spirits, with its Causes and Methods.*

---

After the account of distillation, we
proceed to discourse on calcination.
Calcination is the pulverization of a thing by
fire, through the deprivation of the humidity con-
solidating the parts. The reason for the inven-
tion of it is that the adustive <note>combustible or parched</note>, corrupting, and
defiling sulphureity may be abolished by fire.
Yet it varies according to the diver-
sity of things to be calcined. For bodies are
calcined, and spirits are calcined; indeed, o-
ther things also extraneous to the nature of
these; yet with a diverse intention. And see-
ing there are imperfect bodies of two kinds:
namely hard, as *Venus* <note>copper</note> and *Mars* <note>iron</note>, and soft, as *Ju-*
*piter* <note>tin</note> and *Saturn* <note>lead</note>; all of which are calcined, there
was a necessity of calcining them with a di-
verse intention, namely general and special. They
are all calcined with one general intention,
which is that their corrupting and defiling
sulphureity may be abolished by fire. For so
every adustive sulphureity, which could not
be removed without calcination, is burnt a-
way from every substance whatsoever. And be-
cause the body itself is solid, and by reason of

And that is because a more easy combustion of *sulphur* must necessarily occur from the parts nearer the surface.

This being done, the *solution* must be filtered and kept aside. But that which is not dissolved must be calcined again, and again dissolved in the same manner, until by repeating the labor, the whole is dissolved.

# CHAP. XVI.

## *Of Coagulation and its Causes, and of diverse ways of coagulating Mercury, and of dissolved Medicines.*

*Coagulation* is the *reduction* of a *liquid thing* to a *solid substance* by the *deprivation* of *humidity*. But there is a twofold *cause* for its discovery: one is the *induration* or *hardening* of *quicksilver* <note>The original text uses "Argentvive"</note>; the other *cause* for its discovery is the freeing of dissolved *medicines* from the *wateriness* mixed with them. There—

<meta>catchword: fore</meta>

<summary>The author details a method for dissolving substances using boiling water and introduces coagulation as the process of hardening liquids like quicksilver by removing moisture.</summary>
<keywords>dissolution, boiling water, distillation, coagulation, quicksilver, Argentvive, medicines, humidity</keywords>

--- Page 146 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on coagulation, specifically focusing on the challenges of solidifying Argentvive (quicksilver) and the various failed attempts by previous alchemists.</meta>

<page-num>( 130 )</page-num>
<meta>catchword: proved</meta>

Therefore it is diversified, according to the multiplicity of things to be coagulated. For *Argentvive* <note>quicksilver</note> needs one *coagulation*, but dissolved *medicines* another. Yet there is a twofold *coagulation* of *Argentvive*. One, by washing away its whole innate *moisture* from it; the other, by *inspissation* (or thickening) of its *moisture*, until it is hardened. Yet it is a most difficult and laborious *work* to congeal it, even with the depth of clear-sighted *industry*. Therefore we will declare the whole *ingenuity* of its coagulation.

Some thought the *ingenuity* of its *coagulation* was to keep it for a long time in a temperate *fire*; those who supposed they had coagulated it, after removing it from the *fire*, found the same to flow as before. By this they were driven to amazement and wonder, strenuously arguing that this was not possible to be accomplished. But others, from *natural principles*, supposing that every *moisture* must necessarily be converted into *dryness* by the heat of *fire*, endeavored with *persistence* of *perseverance* to continue its *preservation* in the *fire*; and by this *continuation*, they at last reached this: namely, that some of these *men* converted it into a *white stone*, others into a *red*, and others into a *citrine* or *yellow stone*, which had neither *fusion* nor *ingress*; and because they could not judge the *cause* of these *diversities*, they threw it away.

Others endeavored to coagulate it with *medicines*, and they did not achieve this; but it

In like manner, *argent-vive* has sulphurous parts naturally mixed with it; yet some *argent-vive* has more, others less, which is impossible to remove by *artifice* <note>skill or chemical process</note>. Therefore, seeing it is the property of *sulphur*, with *argent-vive*, to create a red or citrine <note>lemon-yellow</note> color (according to its measure), when the removal of that is performed, the property of *argent-vive* is to give a white *color* by *fire*. This is, therefore, the cause of the variety of colors after its *coagulation* into a *stone*. Likewise, it has the earthiness of *sulphur* mixed with it, by which all its *coagulations* must necessarily be tainted. And this is the cause of the *error* of those who coagulate it into a perfect body. Therefore it happens, from the diversity of the *medicines* of its coagulation, that diverse bodies are created in its coagulation; and also from the diversity of that which is to be coagulated.

### C H A P. XVII.

->*Of Fixation, and its Causes, and of*<-
->*the diverse ways of fixing Bodies and*<-
->*Spirits.*<-

Fixation is the proper preparation of a *fugitive* <note>Volatile or easily evaporated.</note> thing to endure and withstand the *fire*. The *cause* of the *invention* of this *fixation* is so that every *tincture* and every *alteration* may be made permanent in the thing altered and not changed. But it also varies according to the *diversity* of things to be fixed, which are certain *bodies* diminished from *perfection* <note>Base metals, such as lead or copper, which the alchemist seeks to "perfect" into gold or silver.</note>, such as *Saturn*, *Jupiter*, *Mars*, and *Venus*; and according to the *diversity* of *spirits* also, which are *sulphur* and *arsenic* in one *degree*, and *argentvive* <note>Quicksilver or mercury.</note> in another; but *marcasite*, *magnesia*, *tutia*, and the like of these, in the third.
Therefore these *bodies* diminished from *per-*

The author notes that fixation methods vary depending on whether one is working with base metals or various alchemical spirits.</summary>

fection, are fixed by their *Calcination*; because thereby they are freed from their *Volatile* and corrupting *Sulphureity*. And this We have sufficiently declared in the *Chapter* of *Calcination*. But *Sulphur* and *Arsenic* <note>Modern spelling for Arsnick.</note> are fixed two ways, *namely* by repetition of their *Sublimation* in the *Vessel Aludel*, until they remain fixed. Therefore, according to this, the *intention* of hastening their *Fixation* is that the *method* of repeating a manifold *Sublimation* in a short time be observed therein; which repetition is made by two *Aludels*, with their two *Heads* or *Covers*, in the following *order*, so that you may never cease from the *work* of *Sublimation* until you have fixed them. Therefore, as soon as they have ascended in one *vessel*, put them into the other; and do so continually, never allowing them to remain long adhering to the sides of either *vessel*; but constantly keep them in the *elevation of fire* <note>The raising or intensification of heat.</note> until their *elevation* ceases. For the sooner you can multiply the many *repetitions* of *sublimation*, the more swiftly and better will you shorten the time of its *fixation*. For this *cause*, there was a second way of *fixation* found out, which is by precipitating that which is sublimed into *heat*, so that it may constantly remain there until it is fixed. And this is done by a long *glass vessel*, the *bottom* of which (made of *earth* <note>Clay or ceramic.</note>, not of *glass*, because that would crack) must be skillfully connected with good *luting* <note>A sealant or paste used to make joints airtight or protect vessels from heat.</note>; and

<summary>This page explains that substances are fixed through calcination to remove volatility, while sulphur and arsenic require repeated sublimation in aludels or precipitation into heat to achieve stability.</summary>
<keywords>Calcination, Volatile, Sulphureity, Sulphur, Arsenic, Sublimation, Aludel, Fixation, Elevation of Fire, Luting</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of fixation, specifically describing the technique of precipitating substances that adhere to the sides of a vessel and comparing the requirements for fixing quicksilver, sulfur, and arsenic.</meta>

<page-num>( 138 )</page-num>
<meta>catchword: CHAP.</meta>

and the ascending *matter*, when it adheres to the sides of the *vessel*, is thrust down to the *heat* of the *bottom* with a *spatula* of *iron* or *stone*; and this *precipitation* is repeated until the whole is fixed.

The way of fixing <term>Argentvive</term> <note>Quicksilver or mercury.</note> is the same as the way of *fixation* of *sulphur* and *arsenic*; and these ways do not differ, except that *sulphur* and *arsenic* cannot be fixed if their thinnest flammable *parts* are not separated from them by the subtle *artifice* of *division* through this ultimate way of *fixation*. But <term>Argentvive</term> does not have this *consideration*; therefore, in <unclear>this</unclear> *method*, they need a more temperate *heat* than <term>Argentvive</term>. In like manner they differ, because these must be elevated higher than <term>Argentvive</term> by reason of their slowness, and also because they are fixed in a longer time than it. Therefore, they require a longer *vessel* for their *fixation* than <term>Argentvive</term>.

The *fixation* of <term>Marchasite</term> <note>A metallic mineral, likely iron pyrite.</note>, <term>Magnesia</term> <note>In alchemy, this refers to various mineral ores, not modern magnesium.</note>, and <term>Tutia</term> <note>Tutty, an impure zinc oxide residue found in furnaces.</note> is that after their first *sublimation* is finished, casting away their *feces* <note>Solid residue or dross remaining at the bottom.</note>, we repeat their *sublimation*, returning what ascends upward to that which remains below of either of them so often until they are fixed. The *description* of the *vessels* for these has already been given.

<page-num>139</page-num>

### C H A P. XVIII.

#### *Of <term>Ceration</term>, and its Cause.*

<term>Ceration</term> is the <term>mollification</term> <note>The process of softening a substance.</note> of a hard *thing*, not fusible enough for *liquefaction*. From this, it is manifest that the *cause* of the *invention* of this process was so that the *matter*—which had no *ingress* <note>Entry or penetration into a substance.</note> into the *body* for *alteration* by reason of the *privation* <note>Lack or absence.</note> of its *liquefaction*—might be softened so as to flow and have *ingress*. Therefore, some thought *ceration* was to be made with *liquid oils* and *waters*; but that is erroneous and completely far from the *principles* of this *Natural Magistery*, and refuted by the manifest *works* of *nature*. For we do not find in those *metallic bodies* that *nature* has placed a *humidity* that is soon ended; but rather one that is long-lasting, for the necessity of their *fusion* and *mollification*. Because, if she had instilled in them a *humidity* that was soon ended, it would necessarily follow that the *bodies* must be totally deprived of it in only one *ignition* <note>The alchemical process of heating a substance to high temperatures, often until it glows.</note>. From this, it would also follow that every *body* could neither be hammered nor melted after one *ignition*.

Wherefore, imitating the *works* of *nature* as much as we can, we must necessarily follow her *way* in *cerating*. She *cerates* in the *radix* <note>The root or fundamental base of a substance.</note> of fusible *things* with a *humidity*,

<summary>Chapter XVIII defines ceration as a method for softening hard, non-fusible substances so they can penetrate and alter other bodies. The author argues that this must be achieved through a durable humidity that mimics nature's own methods, rather than using temporary liquids like oils or water which evaporate during heating.</summary>
<keywords>ceration, mollification, liquefaction, Natural Magistery, metallic bodies, ignition, alchemical ingress, radix, humidity</keywords>

--- Page 156 ---
<meta>This page concludes Chapter XVIII and the first book of the treatise, focusing on the specific substances used to provide the lasting humidity required for ceration.</meta>

<page-num>140</page-num>

which is above all *humidities*, able to sustain the *heat of fire*: therefore it is necessarily expedient for us also to cerate with a similar *humidity*: But this *cerative humidity* is nothing better, more possible, or more nearly found than in these—namely, in *sulfur* and in *arsenic* nearly, but more nearly in *argentvive* <note>Quicksilver or mercury.</note>. Therefore we do not see the *humidity* of these leave their *earth*, by reason of the strong union which they have in the *work of the mixture of nature*. But in all other things having *humidity*, you will find by experience that the same is separated in *resolution* <note>The process of reducing a substance into its component parts or a liquid state.</note> from their *earthy substance*; and, after its separation, they are deprived of all *humidity*. Yet in the *spirits* aforesaid, it is not so. Therefore, there is no other *thing* by which we may be excused from using them in the *work of ceration*.

The way of *ceration* by them is that their sublimation should be so often multiplied upon the *thing* to be cerated until, remaining with their *humidity* in it, they provide good *fusion*. Yet this cannot be effected before the perfect cleansing of them from every corrupting *thing*. But it seems better to me that the *oils* of these should be first fixed by *oil of tartar*, and every *ceration* competent and necessary for this *art* be made with them.

---

->*of the Sum of Perfection, or of the*<-
->*Perfect Magistery.*<-

## *The AUTHOR’S PREFACE,*
### Dividing this Second Book into Three Parts.

The discourse *of the* principles *of this* magistery <note>The mastery or the supreme alchemical process.</note> *being completed, we must necessarily (according to our* promise) *exactly pursue the* intent *of this* art, *in a* speech *convenient and proper to it. Which* intent *is, a* consideration *of every* thing, *by which the* perfection *of this* work *may be more clearly shown. And it is a* consideration *of the* necessity *of* perfection *of the* medicine, *namely, how it may be understood from what thing*

<summary>Geber introduces the second book of his treatise, stating his intention to transition from general principles to the specific methods required to achieve the perfection of the alchemical medicine.</summary>

*that may best and most closely be extracted, for the entire perfection of the imperfect. ## CHAP. II.

### *Of the Nature of Sulphur and Arsenic.*

Therefore first, inferring the *Nature* of *Spirits*, which are the *Principles* of those *Bodies*; we say, that *Sulphur* and *Arsenic* are a *Fatness* of the *Earth*, as is declared above; the *Experience* and manifest *Probation* <note>Proof or demonstration.</note> of which you may gather from its easy *Inflammation*, and its easy *Liquefaction* <note>The act of melting or becoming liquid.</note> by *Heat*. For nothing is inflamed except what is oleaginous <note>Oily or fatty.</note>, or melts easily by *Heat*, unless it has that nature. Therefore *Sulphur* and its *Compeer* <note>Its companion or equal; here referring to arsenic.</note> have an inflammable *Substance* and *Earthy Feculency* <note>Dregs or impurities.</note>, which is the *Cause* of their *Corruption*. ### CHAP. IV.

#### *Of the Nature of Marcasite, Magnesia, and Tutia.*

But it is necessary that we should also treat other *Spirits*, namely *Marcasite*, *Magnesia*, and *Tutia*, which make a great *impression* on *bodies*. Therefore, what they are, with their *proofs*, we shall show in the present chapter. *Marcasite* has in its *creation* a twofold *substance*: namely, *Argent-vive* <note>Mercury</note> mortified and approaching *fixation*, and burning *sulphur*. That it has *sulphureity* we find by manifest *experience*; for when it is *sublimed*, a manifest burning sulphurous *substance* proceeds from it, and its *sulphureity* is likewise found without *sublimation*. For if it is put into the *fire*, it is no sooner red hot than it is inflamed with a *sulphurous flame* and *burns*. Also, it is sensibly manifested that it has the *substance* of *Argent-vive* <note>Mercury</note>; for it gives to *Venus* <note>Copper</note> the *whiteness* of pure *silver*, as also to *Argent-vive* <note>Mercury</note>; and we see it, in its *sublimation*, to yield

For *citrinity* <note>yellowness</note> is nothing other than a certain *proportion* of *white* and *red*. Therefore, because of its *subtlety*, it penetrates deeper into the *depth* of the *body*; and consequently it alters it more than its own *body* does; and it adheres more in the *testing* with little *skill*, as has now been declared to you. Therefore, whatever *bodies* are altered by the *power* of *argent-vive* <note>mercury</note>, or of *sulphur*, or the like, must necessarily be altered, because only these share a *nature* with those *bodies*.

## C H A P. V.

### Of the Nature of Sol, or Gold.

Now regarding bodies, we will more fully declare their intimate *nature*. And first of *Sol* <note>gold</note>, but afterward of *Luna* <note>silver</note>, and then of all the others, as shall be thought appropriate, with their *probations* <note>tests or proofs</note>, which are acquired by *experiment*.

*Sol* is created of the most subtle *substance* of *argent-vive* <note>mercury</note>, and of most clear *fixture*; and of a small *substance* of clean *sulphur*, and of pure *redness*—fixed, clear, and changed from its own *nature*, tinting that substance. And because there happens a *diversity* in the *colors* of that *sulphur*, the *citrinity* (or *yellowness*) of *gold* must necessarily have a similar *diversity*. For some is more intense, while others are less so in *yellowness*. That *gold* is of the most subtle *substance* of *argent-vive* is most evident, because *argent-vive* easily retains it. For *argent-vive* does not retain any *thing* that is not of its own *nature*. And that it has the clear and clean *substance* of that is manifest by its splendid and radiant brightness, manifesting itself not only in the day, but also in the night. And that it has a fixed *substance* void of all burning *sulphureity* <note>flammability or sulfurous impurity</note> is evident by every operation of it in *fire*:

<summary>This page explains that gold (Sol) is composed of highly refined mercury and a pure, fixed red sulfur, attributing its varying yellowness and its stability in fire to the specific quality of these components.</summary>

A large quantity of *Argent-vive* <note>quicksilver/mercury</note> is the *Cause* of *Perfection*, but too much *Sulphur* is the *Cause* of *Corruption*. And *Uniformity* in *Substance*, which through the *Mixture* is made in *Natural Decoction* <note>heating or boiling</note>, is the *Cause* of *Perfection*; but *Diversity* in *Substance* is the *Cause* of *Corruption*. And *Induration* (or *Hardening*) and *Inspissation* <note>thickening</note>, which is made by long and temperate *Decoction*, is the *Cause* of *Perfection*; but the contrary is the cause of *Corruption*. 

Therefore, if *Sulphur* shall not duly combine with that *Argent-vive*, diverse *Corruptions* must necessarily result, according to the *Diversity* of it. For the *Sulphur* which combines with it in a fixed state might not be entirely adustible <note>combustible</note>, or it might be entirely adustible and flying <note>volatile</note>, in the *Nature* of *Sulphur*; or flying, and not in the *Nature* of *Sulphur*; or it may contain part of the flying and part of the fixed; or in part hold the *Nature* of *Sulphur* and in part not; or be entirely clean, or half unclean; or be of much or little *Quantity*: either much excelling in the *Mixture*, or a little *Quantity* excelled in it; or neither overcoming nor overcome; or white, or red, or between both. 

Therefore, from all these *Diversities*, it was necessary that diverse *Bodies* <note>metals or substances</note>, and others like these, should be created in *Nature*. All of which *Diversities* We intend to speak of with manifest *Probations* <note>proofs or tests</note>.

## C H A P T E R V I.

### Of the Nature of *Luna*, or *Silver*.

Therefore, it is now clear from the preceding, that if clean, fixed, red, and clear *sulfur* falls upon the pure *substance* of *Argent-vive* <note>quicksilver or mercury</note> (it being itself not excelling, but of small *quantity*, and excelled), out of it is created pure *gold*. But if the *sulfur* is clean, fixed, white, and clear, which falls upon the *substance* of *Argent-vive*, pure *silver* is made, if in *quantity* it does not exceed. Yet this has a *purity* short of the *purity* of *gold*, and a coarser *inspissation* <note>thickening or density</note> than *gold* has. The *sign* of this is that its *parts* are not so condensed that it can be equal in *weight* to *gold*; nor does it have as fixed a *substance* as that. The *sign* of this is its *diminution* <note>reduction or loss of mass</note> in *fire*; and its *sulfur*, which is neither fixed nor incombustible, is the *cause* of that *diminution*. But it is not impossible or improbable to judge the same as fixed or not fixed, in respect to one *body* <note>metal</note> or another. For the sulfureity of *Luna* <note>silver</note>, compared with the sulfureity of *Sol* <note>gold</note>, is not fixed and is combustible; but in respect to the *sulfur* of other *bodies*, it is fixed and not combustible.

### C H A P. VII.

### *Of the Nature of Mars, or Iron. Also of the Effects of Sulfur and Mercury; and of the Causes of Corruption and Perfection.*

But if fixed earthy *sulfur* is mixed with fixed earthy *Argent-vive* <note>quicksilver or mercury</note>, and both of these are not pure but of a dull *whiteness*—the *quantity* of the *dominance* of which is highly fixed *sulfur*—*iron* is made from these: because the *dominance* of fixed *sulfur* prevents *fusion* <note>melting</note>. Therefore, from this it is manifest that *sulfur*, by the work of *fixation*, destroys the ease of *liquefaction* more quickly than *Argent-vive*. But *we* see that *sulfur* which is not fixed melts sooner than *Argent-vive*. By these things, the *cause* of the *speed* and *slowness* of *fusion* in every *body* <note>substance or metal</note> is revealed. For that which has more of fixed *sulfur* admits *fusion* more slowly than that which partakes of burning *sulfur*, which flows more easily and sooner; and this has already been clearly enough declared by *us*. But that fixed *sulfur* makes *fusion* slower is evident by this: namely, that it is never fixed unless it is calcined, and no *calcined substance* allows for *fusion*; therefore, in all *things* it must hinder it. That it is not fixed, unless

unless it is calcined, is manifest by the *experiment* of him who tried to fix the same without it being calcined: because he always found it to fly <note>volatilize or evaporate</note>, until it was turned into *earth*, the likeness of which is of the nature of *calx*.

Yet this does not happen in *Argent-vive* <note>mercury</note>; because that may be fixed without being turned into *earth*, and likewise fixed with the conversion of it into *earth*. For by hastening its *fixation*, which is achieved through *precipitation*, it is fixed and turned into *earth*. Also, by successive and often repeated *sublimation*, it is likewise fixed and not changed into *earth*, but yields *metallic fusion* <note>the ability to melt</note>. This is manifest to, and proved by him, who has experienced both *fixations* of it, even to the *consummation* of the *work*; by hasty *precipitation*, and also by the slow method of continually repeated *sublimations*. For he saw and found it to be so, just as he saw it written by *us*. And this is because it has a viscous and dense *substance*: the *sign* of which is the *grinding* of it, by imbibition and mixing with other things. For *viscosity* is clearly perceived in it by its great adherence. That it has a dense *substance*, even he who has but one eye <note>meaning someone with very little insight</note> may clearly see by its appearance and by weighing its immense weight. without consuming its *Humidity*, and without converting it into *Earth*. For, by reason of the good adherence of its parts and the strength of its *Mixtion*, if its parts are in any way thickened by *Fire*, it allows itself no further to be corrupted; nor does it suffer itself (by the entrance of a fierce Flame into it) to be raised into *Fume*. This is because it does not allow itself to be thinned out (rarefied), by reason of its *Density* and its lack of *Adustion* <note>burning or scorching</note>; which is caused by combustible *Sulphureity*, which it does not have.

Therefore, by what has been mentioned, there is found (*through an indubitable discovery*) the wonderful double *Kind* of two *Secrets*: namely, the *Causes* of *Corruption* of every one of the *Metals* by *Fire*. One of these is the *Inclusion* of a burning *Sulphureity* deep within their *Substance*, which diminishes them by *Inflammation*, and also drives out into *Fume*, with extreme consumption, whatever *Argent-vive* <note>mercury</note> in them is of good *Fixation*. But the other cause is an increase upon them of an external *Flame* that penetrates and breaks them down along with itself into *Fume*, no matter how great the *Fixation* in them may be. A third *Cause* of *Corruption* is the thinning out of them by *Calcination*; for then the *Flame*, or *Fire*, can penetrate into and destroy them. Therefore, if all *Causes* of *Corruption* occur together, such *Bodies* must necessarily be exceedingly corrupted. But if not all occur, the speed of *Corruption* of every *Body* is reduced, according to the reduction <note>remission</note> of those causes.

The second Kind is *Goodness*, which by it is considered in *Bodies*. For seeing that *Argentvive* <note>mercury</note>, for no causes of destruction, permits itself to be divided into parts in its composition (because it either with its whole substance retreats from the *Fire*, or with its whole remains permanent in it), a cause of *Perfection* is necessarily observed in it. Therefore, praised be the glorious and blessed *Most High* GOD, who created it and gave to it a *Substance*, and the properties of a *Substance*, which are given to none of the other things in Nature to possess; so that this *Perfection* might be found in it, by a certain *Artifice*, as we have found therein with near potency: For it is that which overcomes *Fire*, and by *Fire* is not overcome; but in it amicably rests, rejoicing therein.

## CHAPTER VIII.

### Of the Nature of *Venus*, or Copper.

Therefore, returning to our purpose, we say that if the *Sulphur* is unclean, coarse, and fixed as to its greater part; but as to its lesser part not fixed, red, and livid; in relation to the whole, neither overcoming nor overcome; and if this falls upon coarse *Argent-vive*, Copper must necessarily be created from it.

of. The **Proof** of all these is easy, by things given from the Nature of them. And the loss of its *Quantity* by *Exhalation*, through the frequent *Combustion* of it, signifies that it has fixed *Sulphur*. This happens by *Reason* of the *Subtilization* of the parts by *Fire*. One of these is removed with *Labour*, but the other cannot possibly be taken away by any *Artifice* performed by *Fire* to which our *Operation* can congruously and profitably reach, as it is so firmly and radically united within it.

Or else, it is done by a *Lavation* <note>washing or purification</note> with *Commixtion* <note>mixing</note> with *Argentvive*, as we have told you. For *Argentvive* holds what is of its own *Nature*, but casts out what is alien.

## C H A P. IX.

### *Of the Nature of Jupiter, or Tin.*

**T**his *investigation* being proposed, following our purpose, we say that if *sulfur* in the <term>*Radix*</term> <note>root or fundamental base</note> of the *mixture* participates in a small amount of *fixation*, being *white* with a *whiteness* that is not pure—not dominant, but dominated—and mixed with <term>*Argentvive*</term> <note>mercury</note> that is partly fixed and partly not fixed, white and impure; from that *mixture*, *tin* must necessarily follow. The *proof* of this you will find through *preparation*. For when you calcine <note>heat to a powder through oxidation</note> *tin*, you find a *sulfurous* smell arises from it, which is a sign of *sulfur* that is not *fixed*. And although it yields no *flame*, you must not therefore think it is fixed. For it gives no *flame*, not because of *fixation*, but because of the *dominance* of <term>*Argentvive*</term> in the *mixture*, which prevents *combustion*. Therefore, in *tin* is proved a twofold *sulfur content*, and also a twofold *substance* of <term>*Argentvive*</term>. One *sulfur content* is less fixed, because in calcining it gives off a smell like *sulfur*. The experience of the *mixture* is proved by the first. The other is proved to be more fixed by its remaining in its <term>*Calx*</term> <note>the powder remaining after a metal is heated</note> while in the *fire*, and yet it does not smell. That the fugitive *substance* of <term>*Argentvive*</term> is a *cause* making that *stridor*, or crashing, is proved by washing *lead* with <term>*Argentvive*</term>. For if *lead* is washed with <term>*Argentvive*</term>, and after its washing melted in *fire* not exceeding the *fire* of its *fusion*, part of the <term>*Argentvive*</term> will remain with it, which gives this *stridor* to the *lead* and turns it into *tin*. But on the contrary, you may also consider that by the *mutation* of *tin* into *lead*: for by a manifold *repetition* of its *calcination* and the *administration* of *fire* convenient for its *reduction*, it is turned into *lead*; especially when, by subtraction of its *scoria* <note>refuse or dross</note>, it is calcined with great *fire*.

Touching the diversities of these *substances*, you may be assured by the knowledge of how to preserve them in proper *instruments* and in the measure of *fire* dividing them: to which we have attained with *diligent* *labor*, and have seen with certain assurance that we judged the *truth* by the same. But since it is expedient, we should inform you what remains after the removal of these from *Jupiter* <note>tin</note>...

The sign of this is the easy *fusion* of one, but the most difficult *fusion* of the other.

## C H A P. X.

### Of the Nature of Saturn, or Lead.

**IT** now remains that we come to the *description of Saturn*; of which we say it differs not from *tin*, after repeating its *calcination* to the *reduction* thereof; except that it has a more unclean *substance*, mixed of the two more gross *substances*, namely of *sulphur* and <term>argentvive</term>; and that the *sulphur* in it is burning and more adhesive to the *substance* of its own <term>argentvive</term>; and that it has more of the *substance* of fixed *sulphur* in its *composition* than *Jupiter* has. The *proofs* <note>probations</note> of these we infer by manifest *experiments*.

That it is of greater *earthy* <term>feculency</term> than *Jupiter* is manifested by the *sight* and by the *washing* of it with <term>argentvive</term>, in this, namely: that more <term>feculency</term> comes from it in the *washing* than from *Jupiter*; and that it takes the first *degree* of *calcination* more easily than *tin*, which is a *sign* of much *earthiness*. For we find bodies of more *earthiness* to be of more easy *calcination*, and those of less *earthiness* to be of more difficult *calcination*. The *proof* <note>probation</note> of this is the most difficult *calcinati<unclear>o</unclear>n* of *gold* completely. And because its *foulness* is not rectified, as in *Jupiter*, by repeated *calcinations*, that

Therefore, the cause of the *impediment* <note>hindrance</note> of every *fusion* is fixed *sulphur*.

From what is now mentioned, you may gather a very great secret: namely, that since bodies of greatest *perfection* <note>referring to gold and silver</note> are found to contain the greatest quantity of *argentvive*, bodies lacking *perfection* that hold a greater quantity of *argentvive* must necessarily be closer to the *perfect*. Therefore, it also follows that bodies of much *sulphureity* <note>sulfur content</note> are bodies of much *corruption*. Why, from the above-alleged, it is now evident that *Jupiter* <note>tin</note> is most closely approximated to the *perfect*, since it possesses more *perfection*, while *Saturn* <note>lead</note> has less; *Venus* <note>copper</note> yet less, and *Mars* <note>iron</note> the least of that on which *perfection* depends.

# The Second Part of this Second Book: Of *Medicines* in General, and of the Necessity of *Perfection* of the *Medicine* Perfecting all Imperfect *Bodies*; and from what *Thing* it may best, and most closely, be extracted.

---

## C H A P. I.

### That for every imperfect *Body*, and also for *Argentvive* <note>quicksilver or mercury</note>, the *Medicine* must necessarily be twofold, *namely*: One for the *White*, and the other for the *Red*; yet that *We* are excused from these by one single, most perfect *Medicine*.

WE prove that *Spirits* are more assimilated to *Bodies* than any other *Thing* in *Nature* by this: *namely*, because they are more united and more friendly to *Bodies* than all other *Things*. Therefore, *We* accordingly affirm that these *Alterations* of *Bodies*, in the first *Invention*, are their true *Medicine*. And *We* have exercised ourselves in every kind of *Ingenuity* so that by it *We* might transform every one of the imperfect *Bodies* with firm *Mutation* <note>transformation or change</note>,

## C H A P. III.

### *That the Defect of imperfect Metals ought to be supplied by Medicine, but their Superfluity removed by Preparation.*

A various *Preparation* may be applied to the *Essence of <term>Saturn</term>* <note>lead</note>, and of *<term>Jupiter</term>* <note>tin</note> likewise, according to the *degree* of their *various approximation* to *perfection*, or *distance* from it. Therefore, since among things indeed corrupting in their *depth*, there is one *thing* coming from the innate *root* of their *nature*, and that is the *earthiness* of *sulfureity*, and the *impurity* of the *earth* of <term>Argentvive</term> <note>mercury</note>, mixed with their *essential nature* in the *beginning* of their *creation*; and another *thing* added after their first *mixing* that causes *corruption*, and this is a *burning sulfureity* of the first kind, and the *impurity* thereof, and a foul *substance* of <term>Argentvive</term>; therefore these are the *things* corrupting the *substance* of *perfection* of *Saturn* and *Jupiter*. One of these it is impossible to remove by the *medicine*

The first indeed seems impossible to be uprooted; because in the *principles* of the proper *nature* of bodies of this kind, the aforesaid were mixed into a true *essence*, and made one true *essence*. Therefore, seeing it is not possible to remove the true *essence* of any *thing* in *nature* while the *thing* itself remains, it is impossible to separate these *corrupting things* from them.

For this *cause*, some *philosophers* have thought the *art* not possible to be attained by *this*: and *we*, and indeed other *searchers* of this *science* in our time, have come to this very *state*, namely: that we likewise could by no way of *ingenious preparation* brighten bodies to the completion of their *brightness*; instead it happened that they were totally infected, and blackened rather. By reason of this, we also, as well as they, were driven to *amazement*, and for a long space of time lay under the *shade* of *desperation*. Yet returning to *ourselves*, and being perplexed with the immense *trouble* of infinite *thoughts* and *meditations*, we considered *bodies* diminished from *perfection* to be foul in the *profundity* of their *nature*, and nothing fulgid (or purely clean) to be found in them; because it was not in them according to *nature*. For that which is not in a *thing* cannot be found in it. Therefore, seeing nothing of *perfection* is

<meta>catchword: found</meta>

Therefore, by this we found something to be diminished in them, which must necessarily be completed by *matter* suitable for it, thereby completing the *defect*.

*Diminution* in them is the *paucity* of *argentvive* <note>quicksilver or mercury</note>, and the improper *spissation* <note>thickening or solidification</note> of the same. We will declare this *medicine* in its due time and place.

Therefore,

## C H A P. IV.

### Of the Preparation of Saturn and Jupiter.

Saturn and Jupiter are prepared with many *Preparations*, according to the necessity of greater *Approximation* to *Perfection*: namely, by a common and *special* way of *Preparation*. A *second* is *Hardness*, with *Ignition* <note>heating to a state of glowing</note> of its *Fusion* <note>melting</note>. And a *third* is *Fixation*, by the removal of its *fugitive* <note>volatile</note> *substance*. ## C H A P.  VI.

### Of the Preparation of Mars.

The Preparations of *Mars* <note>iron</note> are likewise manifold. For some are completed by *Sublimation*, and some without *Sublimation*. That which is made by *Sublimation* is with *Arsenic*, and that in this manner: We endeavor to unite with it *Arsenic* not fixed <note>volatile</note>, as profoundly as We can; so that in *Fusion* it may melt with the same. But afterward it is sublimed in a proper *Vessel* of *Sublimation*. And this *Preparation*, among all others, is found the best and most perfect. There is also another *Preparation* of *Mars* by *Arsenic* sublimed many times from it, until some *Quantity* of the *Arsenic* itself remains. For if this be reduced, it will flow out white, clean, fusible, and well prepared. There is likewise a third way of *Preparation* of the same, by *Fusion* of it with *Lead* and *Tutia*. For from these it flows clean and white.

### C H A P. VII.

->Of the *Mundification* <note>cleansing or purification</note> of *Argentvive* <note>mercury</note>.<-

Therefore, it is now necessary to completely declare the *Mundification* of *Argentvive*. In order to do this, We say that *Argentvive* is cleansed in two ways: either by *Sublimation* <note>the process of vaporizing and re-solidifying a substance to purify it</note>, of which We have already shown the way; or by a *Lavament* <note>a washing</note>, the method of which is this: Pour *Argentvive* into an *earthenware* or *stone dish*, and pour as much *Vinegar* upon it as is sufficient to cover it. Then set it over a gentle *fire*, and let it heat only so far that you may comfortably hold your fingers in it, and no more. This being done, stir it about with your *fingers* until it is divided into very small particles, in the likeness of *powder*; and continue stirring it until all the *Vinegar* you added is completely consumed. Then wash the remaining *earthiness* <note>impurities or residue</note> with *Vinegar*, and cast that away: repeating the same washing as often as necessary, until the *earthiness* of the *Mercury* is changed into a most perfect *celestine color* <note>a sky-blue color</note>, which is a sign that it is perfectly washed. From these things, We must now pass to *Medicines* <note>alchemical preparations used for transmutation</note>.

### CHAP. VIII.

->*That five different properties of perfection necessarily constitute a most perfect medicine: from which it may be conjectured from what things this medicine is to be taken.*<-

We first offer a *universal discourse* concerning *medicines*, with their *causes* and manifest *experiences*. We therefore affirm that unless everything superfluous (either by *medicine* or by way of *preparation*) be taken away from imperfect *bodies*—that is to say, if from them every superfluous *sulphureity* and every unclean *earthiness* is not removed—they cannot be purified: namely, so that in *fusion* they are not separated from the *mixture* after the projection of the *medicine* altering them. When you have found this, you have found one of the five *differences* of *perfection*. Likewise, if the *medicine* does not illuminate and alter into a *white* or *citrine color* (according to what your intent is), which implies a splendid *brightness* and pleasant *lucidity*, *bodies* diminished from *perfection* are not perfected in an entire *completion*. Moreover, if you do not give to it *lunar* or *solar fusion* specifically,

It further explains that a true medicine must provide a specific color, brightness, and fusion point to imperfect metals to achieve total perfection.</summary>

( 188 )
<meta>catchword: CHAP.</meta>

...ly, the *Body* is not alterable in its *Completion*; because it does not remain through the *Trials*: but is altogether separated and recedes from the *Mixture*. But this, which we have more fully determined, is demonstrated in what follows, in the Chapter of <term>Cineritium</term>. Fourthly, if the *Medicine* is not made permanent with a firm *Alteration* of *Impression*, its *Mutation* is of no use, because it is not permanent, but the *Impression* vanishes. Lastly, if it does not provide the *Weights* of *Perfection*, it does not change with a firm and true *Completion* of *Nature*, in which no fraud may be admitted through the *Error* of *Credulity*. For the *Weight* of *Nature* is one of the signs of *Perfection*.

Therefore, since the differences of *Perfection* are five, it is manifest that it is necessary that the *Medicine* of our *Magistery* should exhibit these differences in *Projection*. By this, it is apparent from what things our *Medicine* should be extracted. For it must be prepared from those things which, when closely joined to *Bodies*, readily alter them and amicably adhere to them in their depth. Therefore, searching into all other things through our *Investigation*, we did not find any other thing more friendly to the natures of *Bodies* than <term>Argentvive</term>, prepared by this *Work* of ours; we clearly discerned it to be the true *Medicine* of *Alterables* in their *Completion*, with a true and remarkably unique *Alteration*.

# CHAPTER IX.

## *Of Preparations to be applied to the Medicine, so that it may acquire the due Differences of Properties.*

NOW it remains that we precisely describe its *Substance* and the differences of the *Properties* of the *Substance*. And since we found it does not change without the administration of the *Alteration* of its *Nature*, we also found that it must necessarily be prepared; because it cannot be mixed into the depths of *Bodies* without the *Method* of its *Preparation*. This is so that its *Substance* may be made such that it can be mixed into the depths, even to the very core of the alterable *Body*, without *Separation* forever. But this cannot be achieved unless it is very much refined with the certain and determinate *Preparation* mentioned in the Chapter of Sublimation. Likewise, its *Impression* cannot be permanent unless it is fixed; nor can it illuminate unless its most splendid *Substance* is extracted from it by the *Ingenuity* of its *Method* and way of *Operation* using appropriate *Fire*. Also, this Medicine cannot have perfect *Fusion* unless great *Caution* is used in its *Fixation*, so that it may sof-

is only such when a sufficiency of its *Hu-

*Weight* is the *Subtilty* of the *Substance* of
*Bodies*, and *Uniformity* in the *Essence*. And
by this, the parts of them may be so con-
densed that nothing can come between; and the
condensation of *Parts* is the increase of *Weight*,
and the perfection thereof.

<summary>The author explains that the alchemical medicine can either soften or harden bodies depending on the intensity of the fire used and the preservation of its humidity. It is further noted that the weight and perfection of a substance are determined by the extreme condensation and uniformity of its parts.</summary>

### CHAPTER X.

->*On the Differences of Medicines, namely that some are of the first Order, some of the second, and others of the third.*<-

Therefore it is evident that *subtlety* is necessarily required, both in the *preparation* of *bodies* and in the *way* of perfecting the *medicine* by the artifices of the *work*: because the greater the *weight* of the *bodies* to be transmuted, the greater the perfection they are found to have in our *investigation* by *art*. Therefore, our *discourse* on *medicines* will be rendered profitable if we declare the *differences* of all *medicines*. In order to do this, we say there is necessarily a threefold *difference* of *medicines*. One is of the first *order*, another of the second, and another of the third.

I call a *medicine* of the first *order* every *preparation* of *minerals* which, when projected upon *bodies* falling short of *perfection*, impresses an *alteration*: which does not result in a sufficient *completion*, but rather the altered *body* happens to be changed and corrupted, along with the total *evaporation* of the *impression* of the *medicine* from it: such as is every whitening *sublimation*.

<summary>This page introduces a classification system for alchemical medicines, dividing them into three "orders." A medicine of the first order is described as a mineral preparation that only temporarily alters a metallic body without achieving a permanent or perfect transformation.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, medicine, first order, second order, third order, transmutation, minerals, subtlety, weight, sublimation, dealbation</keywords>

--- Page 208 ---
<meta>This page continues the classification of alchemical medicines begun on the previous page. It concludes the description of the first order and defines the second and third orders based on their ability to permanently and completely perfect metallic bodies.</meta>

<page-num>( 192 )</page-num>
<meta>catchword: CHAP.</meta>

of *Venus*, or *Mars*, which does not receive *Fixation*. And of this kind is every *Addition* of the color of *Sol*, and *Luna*, or of *Venus* mixed together, and set in a *Furnace of Cementation*, such as *Ziniar*, and the like. For this changes with a *Transformation* that is not durable, but rather diminishes itself by *Evaporation*.

A *Medicine* of the second *Order*, I call every *Preparation* which, when it is projected upon *Bodies* falling short of Perfection, alters them toward some *quality* of *Completion*, while entirely leaving the other *qualities* of *Corruption*; such as the *Calcination* of *Bodies*, by which all that is fugitive is burned away. And of this kind is a *Medicine* coloring *Luna* permanently yellow, or permanently whitening *Venus*, while leaving other *qualities* of *Corruption* in them.

---

A *Medicine* of the third *Order* I call every *Preparation* which, when it is applied to *Bodies* by projection, removes all *Corruption* and perfects them with every *quality* of *Completion*. But this is only one. And therefore, by it, we are spared the labors of the *invention* of ten *Medicines* of the second *Order*.

Therefore, the *Work* of the first *Order* is called the *Lesser*; the *Work* of the second, the *Middle*; but of the third, the *Greater Work*. And this *distinction* of all *Medicines* is sufficient.

<summary>This page defines three classes of alchemical medicines: the first order provides temporary change, the second provides permanent but partial completion, and the third—the Greater Work—achieves total perfection of the metal. These correspond to the Lesser, Middle, and Greater Works of alchemy.</summary>

## CHAP. XI.

### *Of the Medicine of the first Order, deal-*
### *bating <note>whitening</note> Venus.*

According to our promise, we intend to declare the differences of all *medicines* of a specific order (since there is one *medicine of bodies*, and another of *Argentvive* <note>quicksilver</note>; and of *bodies*, one is of the first order, another of the second, and another of the third; and the same for *Argentvive* likewise). First, we will speak of the first; then of the second; and lastly, of the third order. And we will, in a complete speech and appropriate order, first speak of the *medicine of bodies*, but afterward of *Argentvive*. Therefore, concerning the *medicine of bodies* of the first order, we say there is one for hard bodies, another for the soft. Of hard bodies, one is for *Venus* <note>copper</note>, another for *Mars* <note>iron</note>, another for *Luna* <note>silver</note>. That of *Venus* and *Mars* is the pure *Dealbation* <note>whitening</note> of their substance; but *Luna*, the *Rubification* <note>reddening</note> of it, with Citrinity <note>yellowing</note> of a pleasing Brightness. For *Rubification* <note>reddening</note> with apparent Brightness is not given to *Venus* and *Mars* by *medicine* of the first order; because they, being totally unclean, are unsuitable to receive the splendor of *Redness* before they have been prepared with a Preparation inducing Brightness.

<summary>This chapter explains the first order of alchemical medicines, distinguishing between those used for hard metals like copper, iron, and silver, and those for quicksilver. It notes that first-order medicines can whiten copper and iron or redden silver, but only after proper preparation of these unclean substances.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, first order, medicine of bodies, Venus, Mars, Luna, Argentvive, dealbation, rubification, citrinity</keywords>

Therefore, we will first speak of all the *medicines of Venus*, and afterward of *Mars*, which are comprised in the first *order*. There is one medicine whitening *Venus* by *Argent-vive* <note>quicksilver</note>, and another by *Arsenic*. By *Argent-vive*, the medicine dealbating <note>whitening</note> it is thus completed. First, precipitated *Argent-vive* is dissolved, then calcined *Venus* is dissolved likewise. Both these *solutions* are mixed into one and projected upon the *body* of *Venus* after they are coagulated. This medicine whitens and cleanses *Venus*. Again, otherwise: *Argent-vive* and *Lithargyry* <note>litharge or lead oxide</note> are dissolved apart and the *solutions* joined together; then a *calcination* of the *body* which is intended to be *whitened* is dissolved likewise, and that *solution* joined with the former, and then coagulated together: this *coagulate* is projected upon the *body*, and that is dealbated <note>whitened</note> thereby. Otherwise, a *quantity* of *Argent-vive* is sublimed often from its *body* until part of it remains with it, with complete *ignition* <note>heating to the point of glowing</note>. Then this *mixture* is very often imbibed and ground with distilled *vinegar*, so that it may be better mixed into the depth of it; then it is assated <note>moderately calcined</note>, and lastly fresh *Argent-vive* is in like manner sublimed from it, and the remaining *matter* again imbibed and moderately calcined as before. And this *work* is so often repeated until a large *quantity* of the *Argent-vive* resides in it with complete *ignition*. This is a good

The way of this is thus: every kind of *Tutia* is calcined and dissolved, as is *Venus* likewise; then both these *solutions* are joined, and with them the *substance* of *Venus* is citrinated. But it is whitened with *Marchasite* <note>marcasite</note> sublimed, as with *Sublimate Argentvive* <note>sublimated quicksilver</note>, and the way is the same.

## CHAPTER XII.

### *Of Medicines Whitening Mars.*

IT remains now that we declare the *whitening of Mars* <note>iron</note> by medicines proper to it; which are, as to its *essence*, of the first *order*, according to which it does not have the right *fusion* <note>melting</note>. Therefore, it is expedient that we should whiten it with a *medicine* making it flow. Every whitening medicine for *Venus* <note>copper</note> and *Mars* likewise is of the same *order* in its preparation. But its special melting agent <note>fusive</note> is *arsenic* of every kind. Therefore, with whichever agent it is whitened and melted, it is appropriate that it should be joined and washed with *quicksilver* <note>argent vive</note> until all its *impurity* is removed and it becomes *white* and fusible; or else, let it be made red hot with intense *ignition* <note>heating</note>, and have *arsenic* projected upon it; and when it is in a state of flux <note>melted</note>, cast a *quantity* of *Luna* <note>silver</note> upon it. For when that is united with it, it is not separated from it by any easy *artifice* <note>method</note>.

Or else, let *Mars* be calcined <note>burned to powder</note>, and all its *soluble alum-like quality* <note>aluminosity</note>, which causes *corruption*, be washed from it by the method of *solution* already mentioned. But afterward, cause cleansed *arsenic* to be sublimed from it, and repeat that *sublimation* many times, until some part

Others have invented many *medicines*, yet one of these two things necessarily happens, *namely*: either they are constrained to create the *medicine* from the same *things*, or from *things* having the same *nature*; or else they compound a *medicine* which, through its *alteration*, is equivalent to that which it is not, and which contributes neither to the *clean* nor to the *parts* of the *clean*, until the *mover*, in the sublime *mobile* of *nature*, rests uncorrupted.
