## CHAP. XIV.

### *Of the Difference of Properties of Medicines of the second Order.*

Now it is time we should pass on to *medicines* of the second *order*, with a *discourse* truly sufficient according to their *requirements*, and manifest *proofs*, with true *experience*. Therefore, seeing the *medicine* of *bodies* to be cleansed is one thing, but that of *argentvive* <note>mercury</note> perfectly coagulable is another; we will first of all completely declare the *medicines* of *bodies*, and afterward the *medicine* of the same *argentvive* <note>mercury</note> coagulable into a true *solific* <note>gold-making</note>, and *lunific* <note>silver-making</note>, or *solar* and *lunar body*.

   A *medicine* of the second *order* is that which does indeed complete imperfect *bodies*; but with only one difference of *perfection*. Yet seeing there are many *causes* of *corruption* in every one of the imperfect *bodies*, namely: in *Saturn* <note>lead</note>, a volatile *sulphureity* <note>sulfur-like quality</note>, the flight <note>evaporation or escape</note> of its *argentvive* <note>mercury</note> (by both of which *corruption* must necessarily be induced) and its *terrestreity* <note>earthiness</note>; therefore the *medicine* is made so that it can indeed totally remove one of them, or by covering it, adorn the same; only leaving all other *causes* of *imperfection*. Therefore, forasmuch as in *bo-*

<meta>catchword: dies</meta>

<summary>This chapter introduces medicines of the second order, which are used to refine imperfect metals and coagulate mercury into gold or silver by addressing specific causes of corruption like volatility and earthiness.</summary>
<keywords>medicines of the second order, alchemy, argentvive, mercury, solific, lunific, Saturn, lead, sulphureity, terrestreity</keywords>

--- Page 219 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on medicines of the second order, specifically addressing how they interact with the unchangeable innate qualities within the "Radix" (root) of imperfect bodies to achieve a state of perfection similar to gold or silver.</meta>

<page-num>203</page-num>

*bodies* there is something unchangeable, which is innate in their *Radix* <note>root</note>, and which cannot be taken away by *Medicine* of the second *Order*; that *Medicine*, which totally removes that from the *Mixtion* <note>mixture</note>, is not called *Medicine* of the second, but of the third and higher *Order*. And, because We found *Superfluities* <note>excesses</note> of volatile things to be removed by the way of *Calcination*, and the *Earthiness* not innate abolished by the reiteration of *Reduction*; therefore, there was a necessity of inventing a *Medicine* of the second *Order*, which might indeed palliate (or cover) the innate, and mollify the hard, and harden the soft, namely in hard and soft *Bodies*, according to completion—not sophistically <note>deceptively</note>, but perfectly to constitute a true *Solific* <note>gold-making</note>, or *Lunific* <note>silver-making</note>, of imperfect *Bodies*.

Therefore, seeing it is manifest that in *Bodies* that are only soft, the swiftness of *Liquefaction* <note>melting</note> cannot be taken away by the ingenious *Artifices* of this *Work*, nor the *Impurity* innate in the *Radix* of their *Principles* removed; the invention of a *Medicine* necessarily happened, that in projection could thicken the *Tenuity* <note>thinness or fluidity</note> of them, and by thickening harden the same to a *Sufficiency* of *Ignition* with their *Liquefaction*; and likewise in hard *Bodies*, attenuate <note>thin out</note> their *Spissitude* <note>thickness or density</note>, and by attenuating lead them to a sufficient *Velocity* of *Liquefaction*, with their own property of *Ignition*; and masking, adorn the *Cloudiness* of *Bodies* of either *Kind*; and transform the one into *White*, and the other into *Red*, most perfect.

<summary>This page explains that medicines of the second order work by masking innate impurities and adjusting the density and melting points of metals to simulate the qualities of gold and silver. Unlike medicines of the third order, these do not totally remove the root impurities but rather "palliate" or transform the metal's properties to a state of perfection.</summary>

<keywords>Radix, Medicine of the second Order, Calcination, Reduction, Solific, Lunific, Liquefaction, Tenuity, Spissitude, Ignition, Alchemy</keywords>

This *Medicine* is not different from a *Medicine* of the third *Order*, except by the *Imperfection* of a lesser *Preparation*. And that it is not different from itself is manifest in the *Projection* of diverse *Bodies* and its *Acceptation* of *Pigments*, but in the way of *Preparation* only. For the *Medicine* thickening the *Tenuity* <note>thinness or fluidity</note> of soft *Bodies* needs one industry of *Preparation*; but thinning the *Spissitude* <note>thickness or density</note> of hard *Bodies* needs another. These indeed need the *Method* of *Consumptive Fire*; but those need the *Administration* of *Conservation* of their *Humidity* <note>moisture</note>.

### CHAP. XV.

## *Of a Medicine Lunar and Solar, for imperfect Bodies.*

**WE** now intend to complete our *Universal Discourse* of the *Medicine* of this second *Order* with a certain and true determination. And first, we will describe the *Lunar Medicines* <note>silver-making medicines</note> of all imperfect *Bodies*, with the *Differences* of their *Preparations*; afterward the *Solar* <note>gold-making medicines</note>, with their proper *Differences* likewise. We have already proved in our *Discourse* that *Sulphur* is *Corruptive* of every kind of *Perfection*. But *Argentvive* <note>quicksilver or mercury</note> is *Perfective* in the *Works* of *Nature*, with complete *Regimens* <note>controlled processes</note>.
<meta>catchword: Therefore</meta>

Therefore we also, not changing but imitating *Nature* in whatever *works* it is possible for us to follow her, do likewise use *quicksilver* <note>Argent-vive</note> in the *magistery* of this work, for a *medicine* of every kind of *perfection*—namely, *lunar* and *solar*—both for imperfect *bodies* and for *quicksilver* that can be solidified. But since, according to what we mentioned above, we have said that there is a twofold *difference* in the *medicine*—one for *bodies*, and the other for truly solidifiable *quicksilver*—we are therefore led to give a certain *account* of *bodies* first, and afterward of *quicksilver*.

The *matter* itself of this *medicine* of every kind is only one, and it is that which is already sufficiently known. Therefore take that, and if you wish to work according to the *lunar order* we promised to show, learn to be expert in *operating*, and prepare *it* using the known *ways* of this *magistery*. The goal of this is that you should separate the pure *substance* from it and fix part of it, but leave a part for *cerating* <note>softening a substance to a wax-like consistency</note>; and so continue through the entire *magistery* until you complete its desired *fusion*. If it flows quickly in hard *bodies*, it is perfect; but in soft *bodies*, it is the opposite. For this *medicine*, when projected upon any of the imperfect *bodies*, changes it into a perfect *lunar body* <note>silver</note>, if the known preparations have been first applied to this medicine; but if not, it leaves it diminished, yet with only one difference.

## C H A P. X V I.

### *Of the Medicine Coagulating Argent-vive.*

According to the order of the *premises* <note>preceding chapters</note>, it concerns us, for the completion of the work, to proceed to the narration of the medicine coagulating *argent-vive* <note>quicksilver</note>. Therefore, we say it is taken from such matter as the matter itself is, *namely*, according to what is specified in many *chapters* of this *work*. And that is because *argent-vive*, seeing it is easily fugitive <note>volatile</note> without any *inflammation*, may suddenly adhere to it in its depth and be conjoined with it through its smallest parts, and likewise thicken and preserve it in *fire* by its own *fixation*; until it is better able to sustain the force of *fire* consuming its *humidity*, and convert it, by the benefit of this in a moment, into true *solific* <note>gold-making</note> and *lunific* <note>silver-making</note>, according to that for which the medicine was prepared.

But seeing we find not anything more to agree with it than *that* which is of its own nature, therefore, by reason of this, we judged that with that the medicine of it might be completed; and we endeavored by ingenuity to make the *form* of the

In *bodies* more difficultly, in *argent-vive* more nearly, but not more perfectly. Therefore, of whatever kind the medicine is to be, the medicine of the precious *stone* must be sought as well in *bodies* as in the *substance* of *argent-vive*.

## CHAPTER XVII.

### How *Ingress* is Procured in *Medicines* by *Artifice*.

**B**ut, because it happens that a *Medicine* is sometimes mixed and sometimes not, we will declare the *way* of mixing—namely, how everything, or each peculiar *medicine* that does not enter, may most profoundly acquire *ingress* <note>entry or penetration</note> into a *body*. The *way* is by the *dissolution* of that which enters, and by the *dissolution* of that which does not enter, and by the *mixture* <note>original: Commixtion</note> of both *solutions*. For it makes everything to be ingressive, of whatever kind it may be, that is conjoined with it through its smallest parts. Yet this is completed by *solution*; and *fusion*, in things that are not fusible, is completed by the same process; therefore, they are more apt to have *ingress* and to alter. This is the *reason* why we calcine <note>reduce to powder by heat</note> some things which are not of the *nature* of these: namely, so that they may be better dissolved. And they are dissolved so that *bodies* may the better receive *impression* from them, and likewise be prepared and cleansed by them through these means. Or we give *ingress* to those which are not permitted to enter by reason of their *thickness* <note>original: Spiffitude</note>, with manifold reiteration of the *sublimation*

<summary>This chapter explains how to ensure an alchemical medicine can penetrate a metallic body by dissolving its components and mixing the resulting solutions. This process of dissolution and calcination allows substances to be joined at their smallest parts, facilitating the cleansing and transformation of the metals.</summary>

<keywords>alchemical medicine, ingress, dissolution, mixture, fusion, calcination, sublimation, metallic bodies, transmutation</keywords>

--- Page 226 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of sublimation and the process of ensuring medicinal substances penetrate metallic bodies through dissolution and repeated applications.</meta>

sublimation of non-flammable spirits upon them—namely, of *Arsenic* and *Argent-vive* <note>quicksilver</note> that is not fixed—or by the manifold *repetition* of the *solution* of that which does not have *ingress* <note>entry or penetration</note>.

Yet, a good *precaution* for giving *ingress* to things that cannot be mixed is that the *body* which you want to be changed and altered by these should be dissolved; and the things you want to both enter and alter it should likewise be dissolved. Nevertheless, *solution* cannot be made of all *parts*, but only of some, with which this or that *body* (and no other) must be soaked time after time. For through this benefit, it necessarily has *ingress* only into this or that specific one; but this does not necessarily happen in any other *body*. Therefore, everything must necessarily have *ingress* by these methods, through which it depends on the *nature* of the substance to have *ingress* (as we said) and to alter once the *mixture* <note>original: Commixtion</note> is discovered. By this preceding *discourse*, the number of ten *medicines* is completed, along with a sufficient *treatment* <note>original: Tractation</note> of them.

# CHAPTER XIX.

### Of the Lunar Medicine of the Third Order.

First, we will declare the way of administration of the *Lunar Medicine*, which is as follows: Take the known *Stone* of it, and by the way of *separation* divide its most pure *substance*, and keep it apart. Then fix some of that *part* which is most pure, leaving the *remainder*. And when it is fixed, dissolve what is soluble of it; but what is not soluble, put to be calcined; and again dissolve the *calcinate*, until again what is soluble of it is altogether dissolved. Continue this *process* until the greater *quantity* is dissolved. Then mix all the *solutions* together and coagulate them. This being done, heating <note>original: cocting</note> gently, keep the *coagulate* in a *temperate fire* until a greater *fire* may conveniently be administered for its *perfection*. Therefore, repeat all these *orders* of *preparation* upon it four times, and lastly calcine it by its own way; for by administering it thus, you have sufficiently governed the most precious *earth* of the *stone*. Then, by the subtle *way* of *ingenuity*, conjoin a *quantity* of the part reserved above with part of this prepared *earth*,

Then fix it again, until it has easy *Fusion* with its *Ignition*. ### C H A P. I.

### *The Division of what follows.*

Omitting manifest experiences, of which we make no mention since they are known and certain to all—namely, of *Weight*, *Color*, and *Extension* under the *Hammer* <note>malleability</note>, which are discerned without any *sagacity* or *industry*—we with *caution* make *trial* by the *experiences* of *craftsmen*, whether the *projection* of the *administration* of this *Art* is a *completion* with *truth*, proving the same. These are *Cineritium* <note>the process of cupellation</note> (or the *test* called a *Cupel*), *Cement*, *Ignition*, *Fusion*; *Exposure* over sharp *Vapor*, the *testing* of *burning sulfur* by *mixing*; *Extinction*, the *repetition* of *Calcination* and *Reduction*, and the easy or difficult *reception* of *Argentvive* <note>mercury</note>. ### CHAPTER II.

->*Of Cineritium <note>cupellation</note>, why some bodies endure it, but others do not.*<-

We come first to speak of *Cineritium*, with all its manifest *causes*, and of the way it is *prepared*. The *Solar* <note>gold</note> and *Lunar* <note>silver</note> *substances* alone are permanent in the *trial* of *Cineritium*. Therefore, by searching out the true differences in the *substances* of these *perfect bodies*, and likewise the *causes* of *cinerition*, we will make a *trial* to see which of the imperfect *bodies* endure more, and which endure less, in the *examination* of this *magistery*. Yet we have already sufficiently declared the *secret* of these two *bodies* in the *profundity* of their *substance*. And it is this: namely, that their first *radix* <note>root or origin</note> was a large *quantity* of *Argentvive* <note>mercury</note>, and its purest *substance*; at first most subtle, but afterward thickened until it could admit *fusion* with *ignition*. Therefore, whatever *bodies* that are diminished from *perfection* have more *earthiness*, they endure less in this *examination*; but those that have less earthiness, endure more. Because these do indeed adhere more, by reason of their subtlety

### CHAP. III.

### *Of the Trial of the Cineritium (or Cupel), how it is to be compounded and used.*

Now We speak of the Way of making it,
which is thus: Take sifted *Ashes*, or *Calx* <note>powdered lime or roasted mineral</note>,
or *Powder* of the *Bones* of *Animals* burnt, or
a *Mixture* of all, or of some of them; mois-
ten these with *Water*, and make the *Mixture*
firm and solid with your hand, and in the midst
of it, worked into a round flattish *Lump*, make
a round and smooth *Hollow*, and upon the
bottom of it strew a small *Quantity* of *Glass*
beaten to *Powder*. Then permit it to be dri-
ed. When dry, put that *Metal* into the *Hol-*
*lowness* thereof, which you intend to try by
this *Examination*, and put *Coals* of *Fire* upon it,
and then with *Bellows* blow upon the *Surface*
of the *Examinable Body*, until it flows. Up-
on which being in flux <note>melted</note> cast part after part
of

Therefore wait until all the *Lead* has exhaled <note>evaporated or oxidized</note>. If that has vanished, and yet the motion does not cease, it is not pure. Therefore again cast *Lead* upon it, and blow as before, until the *Lead* vanishes. If it does not yet rest, repeat the casting in of more *Lead*, and blowing upon it, until it is still, or quiet, and you see it clean and clear in its *Superficies* <note>surface</note>. This being seen, open the *Coals*, and dissipate the *Fire*, and then pour *Water* upon the *Surface* thereof. For you will find it perfectly examined <note>tested or refined</note>. If while you are blowing this *Examination*, you cast in *Glass*, the *Body* will be better and more perfectly purified; because that takes away the *Impurities*, and separates them. Yet instead of *Glass*, *Salt*, *Borax*, or a little *Alum*, may be cast in. In like manner this *Examination* of the *Cupel* <note>Cineritium</note> may be made in a *Crucible* of earth, if the *Fire* round about it is blown, and upon the *Surface* also of the *Crucible*, so that the *Body* to be examined may the sooner flow, and be perfected.

These things being sufficiently declared, We now pass to the *Examination* of *Cement*, with its *Causes*, and manifest and known *Experiences*.

## C H A P. IV.

### Of *Cement*, why some *Bodies* sustain it more, and others less.

WE have said that some *Bodies* are burned more, and others less, by the *Calcination* of *Fire*, namely those which contain a greater *Quantity* of burning *Sulphur* are burned more; but those that contain less, are burned less. Therefore, seeing *Sol* <note>Gold</note> has a smaller *Quantity* of *Sulphur* than other *Metallic Bodies*, it is not (among all *Mineral Bodies*) burned by the *Inflammation* of *Fire*. And *Luna* <note>Silver</note>, next to *Sol*, partakes of a smaller *Quantity* of *Sulphur* than the other four *Bodies*, yet more than *Sol*. Therefore, according to this, it can bear the *Ignition* of *Inflammation* for a long period of time less than *Sol*; and by consequence, it can less bear things burning by a similar *Nature*. *Venus* <note>Copper</note> can bear it even less than silver, because it consists of more *Sulphur* and of greater *Earthiness* than *Luna*; therefore it can less bear the *Inflammation* of *Fire*. *Jupiter* <note>Tin</note> bears it less also than *Sol* or *Luna*, because it partakes of greater *Sulphureity* and *Earthiness* than either of them; yet it is burned less by *Inflammation* than *Venus*, but more than *Sol* and *Luna*. *Saturn* <note>Lead</note> in its *Commixture* by *Nature*, holds more of *Earthiness* and *Sulphureity* than any

<summary>This chapter explains why certain metals are more resistant to fire and chemical "cement" than others, attributing their durability to lower levels of sulfur and "earthiness." *Mars* <note>Iron</note> is not burned by itself, but by *Accident*. For when it is mixed with *Bodies* of much *Humidity*, it imbibes that *Humidity*, by reason of its own *Want* of the same; and therefore being conjoined, it is neither inflamed nor burned, if the *Bodies* with which it is *United* are neither flammable nor combustible. But if combustible *Bodies* are mixed with it, it necessarily happens (according to the *Nature* of their *Combustion*) that *Mars* is burned and inflamed. Therefore, seeing *Cement* is constituted of flammable *Things*, the necessary *Cause* of its *Invention* is manifest; and it was so that all combustible *Things* might be burned. And since there is only one *Body* incombustible <note>Gold</note>, that alone, or what is prepared according to the *Nature* of that, is kept safe in *Cement*. Yet some abide more, others less, in *Cement*: But which abide more, and which less, are known along with their said *Causes*. Therefore *Luna* <note>Silver</note> abides *Cement* more, but *Mars* less, *Jupiter* less than *Mars*, and *Venus* <note>Copper</note> less than *Jupiter*; but *Saturn* <note>Lead</note> least of all.

# CHAPTER V.

## *The Examination of Cement, how it is to be compounded and used.*

---

NOW We will declare the way of *Cementing*. Since it is known to us that *Cement* is very necessary in the *Examination* of *Perfection*, we say it is compounded of inflammable *things*. Of this kind are all blackening, volatile, penetrating, and burning *things*; such as *Vitriol*, *Sal ammoniac*, *Flos Æris* <note>Flowers of copper; copper oxide</note>, and the ancient *Fictile Stone* <note>Potter's clay or brick dust</note>, and a very small *quantity* (or none) of *Sulphur*, and *man's urine*, with similar sharp and penetrating *things*. All these are made into a paste with *male urine*, and spread upon thin *plates* of that metal which you intend to examine by this method of *probation*. Then the said *plates* must be laid upon an *iron grate* contained within an *earthen vessel*; yet in such a way that one does not touch another, so that the power of the *fire* may have free and equal *access* to them. Thus, the whole must be kept in the *fire*, in a strong *earthen vessel*, for the space of three days. But here, great *caution* is required that the *plates* may be kept red-hot, but do not melt. After the third *day*, you will find the *plates* cleansed of all *impurity*, if their substance was

## CHAP. VI.

### *Of Ignition.*

**IT** remains now that we treat of *Ignition*. Having declared that *Bodies* of greatest *Perfection*, with determinate *Ignition*, are found to receive the *Fire* before their *Fusion*; therefore we say here, if our *Aim* be to find out their complete *Alteration*, there is a necessity to bring such *Bodies* to their *Fusion*. And before these *Bodies* of *Perfection* are fused, to see them admit *Ignition*, with an inflammation of a pleasing *Celestial Color*, and this before their *Ignition* reaches the *Whiteness* of *Fire*, which the *Eye* can in no way discern.

Therefore, it is manifest that their perfect *Ignition* is (before *Fusion*) with intense *Redness*, and not with *Whiteness*, which the *Eye* cannot behold. For if the administered *Bodies* are melted before they are red-hot with *Fire*, they are not in a state of *Completion*. And if they are made *fire-hot* with labor and strong *Expression* of *Fire*, their *Administration* is not true; and this indeed occurs if it happens in soft *Bodies*. Because the same is only found in *Mars* <note>Iron</note>. For *Ignitable Bodies* do not easily, in the way of *Preparation*, admit *Ignition*; nor do fusible *Bodies*,

If *Bodies* administered <note>treated or processed</note> do not yield a *Flame* of a pleasing *Celestial Color* during their *Ignition*, their *Administration* is not complete. And if any part of the *Weight*, *Color*, *Beauty*, *Ignition*, and the like is found to be diminished by reason of the *Differences* in the *Goodness* of *Preparation*, the *Indagation* <note>investigation or research</note> of the *Artist* was not sufficient. Therefore, let him make a new *Search* until he finds his *Error*, with *Ways* of *Operating* corresponding to *Divine Goodness*.

# C H A P. VII.
## *Of Fusion, or Melting.*

IN the account of *Fusion*, we shall sufficiently discourse according to the *Examen* <note>examination or testing</note> of all *Bodies* by the certain *Ignition* of them. Therefore we say that *Fusion* with *Ignition* is the only *Argument* <note>proof or evidence</note> of *Perfection*; yet not with every kind of *Ignition*, but with *Ignition* in which the *Body* grows not altogether *White*; and with *Ignition* in which is not made a dull paleness of *Fire*, and in which the *Body* is not suddenly melted, or does not flow immediately after *Ignition*. For when a *Body* flows with very small pressure of a weak *Fire*,

And if the *Ignition* of a *Body*, before its *Fusion*, is achieved with laborious and violent *Expression* <note>application or force</note> of strong *Fire*, and with an inestimably bright *Ray*, being altogether *White* and shining; it is not a *Body* of *Perfection*, but a *Body* of altered *Hardness*. Also, if after its *Fusion*, and when taken from the *Fire*, it is suddenly hardened so that it does not flow, even while its brilliant *Ignition* remains, it is not a *Body* of *Lunar* <note>silver</note>, or *Solar* <note>gold</note> *Perfection*, regardless of what kind and preparation the *Body* administered was; but it comes under the *Nature* of the *Differences* of *Mars* <note>iron</note>.

Therefore, by the above-stated it is evident that in fusible *Bodies*, through the *Experiment* of them, a threefold *Ignition* may be found before the *Liquefaction* of their *Substances*: namely, one, *Pale*; the other, *Red* and *Clear*; and the third most *White*, shining with a *Ray*. ## C H A P. V I I I.
### Of the *Exposition* of *Bodies* over the *Vapours* of acute *Things*.

But the purest *Gold* does not flower. Yet we find that *Luna* <note>silver</note> or *Sol* <note>gold</note>, when they are not pure and are exposed over the *Vapours* of *Acute Things*, do flower and yield a most pleasant *Celestine Flos*; yet that of *Sol* is more delightful than that of *Luna*. ## C H A P. IX.

### *Of the Extinction of Bodies Fire-hot.*

the *Magistery* <note>alchemical mastery or process</note> consists in *Perfection* or not.

it totally loses its proper *Color*, or creates a
*Scoria*, it is manifest, that the *Body* does yet

# C H A P. X.

## *Of the Admixture of burning Sulphur.*

Likewise, by the *Mixing* of *Sulphur* it is proved whether the *Magistery* <note>alchemical mastery or process</note> consists in *Perfection* or not. For by our *Experience* we find that *Sulphur* mixed with *Bodies* burns some more and others less; and by our *Artifice* <note>skill or technical process</note> we have discerned that some retreat from its combustion and some do not. And from this may be noted the difference between those *Bodies* diminished from *Perfection* that are prepared in a *Sophistical* <note>deceptive or false</note> completion. Therefore, among all *Bodies* of whatever kind, we find *Sol* <note>gold</note> is not burned by *Sulphur*. The next to this, and least burned, is *Jupiter* <note>tin</note>; then *Luna* <note>silver</note>; and after that *Saturn* <note>lead</note>; but *Venus* <note>copper</note> is burned more easily than all these; and *Mars* <note>iron</note>, by reason of the *Oleaginy* <note>oiliness or fatty nature</note> of *Sulphur*, is most easily burned. Hence it is observable that a *Body* is burned more if it is less close to the nature of the *Perfect*.

Also, by the *Diversity* of *Colours* after the combustion of *Bodies*, it may be known in what way the altered *Body* is composed, based on the *Radix* <note>root or origin</note> of its own *Nature*; because after the *Mixing* of *Sulphur*, *Sol* <note>gold</note> gives an intense *Citrine* <note>lemon-yellow</note> or clear *Red Colour*; *Luna* <note>silver</note>, a black mixed with *Celestine* <note>sky-blue</note>; *Jupiter* <note>tin</note>, a black *Colour* mixed with a small *Tincture* of *Redness*; *Saturn* <note>lead</note> a dull black mixed with much *Redness* and a livid <note>bluish-gray</note> *Colour*; *Venus* <note>copper</note>, from the *Mixing* of *Sulphur*, yields a black mixed with livid if much combustion of *Sulphur* has preceded; but if little combustion was pre-existent, it shows a most clean and pleasant *Violet Colour*; but *Mars* <note>iron</note>, in every kind of combustion, creates a most black, dull *Colour*.

*Likewise*, a *Diversity* is observed in *Bodies* after their *Reduction* <note>restoration to a metallic state</note> from the combustion of *Sulphur*. Some do indeed return; but others, by the force of *Fire*, recede from the *Reduction* along with the *Sulphur*, either totally or the greater part of them: some return into *Bodies* of their own *Nature*; others return from the combustion into *Bodies* other than those of their own *Nature*. *Sol* and *Luna* return from the combustion of *Sulphur* into the *Nature* of their proper *Body*; but *Jupiter* and *Saturn* recede. *Jupiter* recedes totally, or for the greater part; *Saturn* does not recede totally; yet sometimes a greater and sometimes a lesser part of it is destroyed. The *Diversity* of these is by reason of the *Nature* of *Things* and *Bodies*, and by

Therefore, by these means, the *nature* of all altered *bodies* may be discovered.

## C H A P. XI.

### *Of Calcination, and Reduction.*

We must now speak of the *examen* <note>test or examination</note> of the repetition of *calcination* and *reduction*. Therefore, we say that *bodies* are found to be of *perfection* through the repetition of *calcination* and *reduction*, according to their degrees of *goodness*, if they lose nothing of their *colour*, *weight*, *quantity*, or *brightness*;

Therefore, accustom yourself to these *works*, so that you may know them.

### C H A P. XII.
#### *Of the Easy Susception of Argentvive.*

IT is now undeniably manifest to you that *bodies* containing the greatest *quantity* of *argentvive* <note>mercury or quicksilver</note> are *bodies* of *perfection*. Therefore, it is to be supposed that those *bodies* are closer to *perfection* which more amicably absorb *argentvive*. The sign of this is the easy *susception* <note>absorption or taking up</note> of *argentvive* by a *solar* <note>gold</note> or *lunar body* <note>silver</note> of *perfection*. For this same *reason*, if an altered body does not easily receive *argentvive* into its *substance*, it must be very far from the completion of *perfection*.

---

## CHAPTER XIV.
### *What Order the Author has observed in treating of the aforementioned.*

**BUT**, so that the *envious* may not calumniate <note>slander</note> us, we declare that we have not treated of our *science* with a continuous series of *discourse*, but have dispersed it in various *chapters*. And this was done because, if it had been delivered in a continuous *series* of *speech*, the just *man*, as well as the evil man, might have unworthily seized it. Therefore we have concealed it in places where we more openly speak; yet not under an *enigma* <note>riddle</note>, but in plain discourse to the *artist* <note>alchemist</note>: and indeed we have described it in such a way of speaking as is agreeable to the *will* of the *Most High*, blessed, sublime, and *glorious God*, and our own *mind*, who wrote the same as it happened to be remembered, or was infused by the *grace* of his divine *goodness*, who gives it to and withholds it from whom he will.

<summary>The author explains the varying transformative power of alchemical medicines and justifies his decision to scatter the secrets of the science throughout the book to protect it from the unworthy.</summary>
<keywords>multiplication, transmutation, solific, lunific, magistery, concealment, divine grace, alchemist</keywords>

--- Page 254 ---
<meta>This page concludes the second book of Geber's "Sum of Perfection," emphasizing the importance of practical investigation over mere book-learning and providing a final encouragement to the student.</meta>

Therefore, let the *Son* of *Learning* not despair: for if he seeks it, he may find it through the *investigation* of the *movement* of his own *nature*, rather than through *doctrine* alone. Because he who seeks by the goodness of his *industry* finds it; but he who seeks it by following *books* only will very slowly attain this most precious *art*. For we published this *art*, discovered by *us* alone, for ourselves alone and not for others, although it is most true and altogether certain. Therefore, we invite only the *prudent* to this *art*, and (through the *skills* taught by *us*) we reveal to them the way of *investigation*. For we wrote it down once it was found, together with the *way* of its *invention* and the *skillfulness* of its *methods*, for no one but ourselves. Therefore, let the well-intentioned *artificer* exercise himself by those *precepts* which we have delivered; and he will greatly rejoice when he has found the *gift* of the *Most High God*. These words may suffice concerning the *investigation* of this sublime *art*.

---

## C H A P. X V I.

### Of the Seven Properties of the Medicine.

**T**hese things being considered, We find by Our *Investigation* several Properties of Things necessary and useful in our *Stone*; and they are these: *Oiliness* <note>Oleaginy</note>, *Thinness* <note>Tenuity</note> of *Matter*, *Affinity*, *Radical Humidity*, *Clearness* of *Purity*, a *fixing Earth*, and *Tincture*.

The first *Property* of the *Differences* of the *Medicine* is *Oiliness* <note>Oleaginy</note>, which in *Projection* gives *Universal Fusion* and *Sprinkling* <note>Aspertion</note> of the *Matter*. For the first thing which is necessary after the *Projection* of the *Medicine* is the sudden and convenient *Fusion* of the *Medicine* itself, which is perfected and rendered viscous with *Mineral Oiliness* <note>Oleaginy</note>.

The third *Property* is *Affinity* (or *Proximity* <note>Vicinity</note>) between the *Elixir* and the *Body* to be transmuted, giving adherence in the *Encounter* <note>Obviation</note> and *Retention* of its like; because immediately after the *Ingress* of the *Medicine*, *Adherence* is convenient and necessary.

The fourth *Property* is *Radical Humidity*, fiery, congealing, and consolidating the *Parts* retained, with *Adherence* of its own like, with the *Union* of all similar parts inseparably forever; because, after *Adherence*, *Consolidation* of the *Parts*, with their *Radical*, viscous, and necessary *Humidity*, is opportune.

The fifth is a *Cleansing* <note>Mundificative</note> *Clearness* of *Purity*, giving evident *Splendor* in the existing *Combustion*, yet not burning. For, after *Consolidation* of the purified *Parts*, it is left to the *Actual Fire* to have the power of burning all extraneous *Superfluities* that are not consolidated. Therefore *Purification* follows and is necessary.

### CHAPTER III.

### *The Division of this Book into four Parts.*

According to the requirements of *Art*, We have argued against *Men* denying *Art*; and have discussed *Natural Principles*, which are according to the *Intention* of *Nature* in the procreation of *Metals*; and of *Bodies*, and their *Procreations* and *Preparations*; and lastly of the *Medicines* and *Examinations* of those same *Bodies* transmuted, whether their completion consists in *Perfection*; all of which has been done by Us in our book titled, *The Sum of Perfection, or of the Perfect Magistery.* And there We have written the *Way* of the *Discovery* of *Art*, and our *In-*

<meta>catchword: genious</meta>

<summary>This page describes the final property of the alchemical Medicine—its ability to tint and transmute metals into gold or silver—and introduces the third chapter, which summarizes the author's previous writings in "The Sum of Perfection."</summary>
<keywords>Tincture, Citrine, Lunification, Solification, Transmutation, Sum of Perfection, Natural Principles, Metals</keywords>

--- Page 260 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction to the "Invention of Perfection," outlining the four parts of the book which cover middle minerals, the purification of spirits, the preparation of metals, and the final medicinal transmutations into gold and silver.</meta>

<page-num>( 244 )</page-num>

ingenious *Works*, which We have seen and touched: But here, in this *Book*, We will treat of the *Invention of Perfection*, and in a special manner declare those *Things* which are necessary in this our *Magistery*; and the *Way of Preparing* the same, with their weight and measure, and certain *Probation* <note>testing or proof</note>, not conjectural.

Our *Intention* is to divide this little *Book* into *Four Parts*; and in the First to treat sufficiently of *Middle Minerals* and their *Preparations*, so that they may be aptly disposed in the *Extraction of Spirits* and *imperfect Bodies*: in the Second, of the *Mundifications* <note>purifications</note> of *Spirits* and the entire *Preparations* of the same: in the Third, of the diverse *Preparations* of *imperfect Bodies*, according to the requirements for the *Completion of the Work*: and in the Fourth, especially of all *Medicines* for transforming every *Body* into *Sol* <note>gold</note> and *Luna* <note>silver</note>, with *Projection*, *Weight*, and *Measure*, according to the requirements of necessity for the *Completion of the Work*.

## C H A P. IV.

### *Of the Preparation of Middle Minerals.*

Common Salt is dissolved in clear *fountain water*, and distilled through a *filter*, and afterwards congealed in an *earthen vessel*, or in a *vessel* of *lead*, or other *metal*. *Salt-peter* is dissolved in *fountain water*, distilled through a *filter*, and congealed in a *vessel* of *glass*, so that it may there be crystallized. *Salt-nitre* is prepared in this way, *namely*: it is dissolved in clear *fountain water*, filtered, etc. *Salt-gem* <note>rock salt</note> is dissolved, etc. as before, and congealed in a *glazed earthen vessel*. True *salt-alkali* is made of *zoza* (or *soda*) dissolved and filtered, and the *solution* boiled away to one-third part, and then the *salt*, in a short time, settles to the *bottom* in *crystals*; and so it is prepared. Some also make *salt-alkali* thus: they take 5 pounds <note>lib. 5.</note> of *ashes* of *heartsease*, and 1 pound <note>lib. 1.</note> of *quicklime*, and by boiling in *water* they make a *lixivium* <note>a liquid solution of alkaline salts leached from ashes</note>, which they filter and congeal; they repeat this labor once, and it is prepared. *Sal ammoniac* is made of five parts of *human urine*, and one part

These being boiled together until the *Humidity* <note>moisture</note> is consumed, from the remainder sublime a true and profitable *Sal ammoniac*. Dissolve this again in *Sweat*, and sublime it from *Common Salt* once; then it is prepared. *Salt* of *Tartar* is made of the calcined *Feces* <note>dregs or sediment</note> of distilled *Wine*; the *Tartar* being dissolved from them and congealed, it is thus prepared. *Salt* of *Urine* is likewise made of the calcined *Feces* of distilled *Urine*; this is again dissolved in its own *Water* and congealed, and so it is prepared. It is of great *Benefit* <note>emolument</note> and *Use*.

Many men treat the *Preparations* of *Salts* in various ways; but we, by our *Invention*, have found that these *Preparations* of *Salts* are shorter, more subtle, and more profitable for our purpose; since from all things truly calcined, *Salts* are extracted by *Solution*, which must be prepared by the *Way* alleged above. Therefore it suffices for us to pass them by, as these are more useful. *Glassy* or *Rock Alum* has a twofold way of *Preparation*: one for washing calcined *Bodies*, and the other for subliming *Spirits*. According to the first *Way*, it is prepared thus: Dissolve it in clear *Fountain Water*, distill it through a *Filter*, and boil it down to a third part. Put this *Part* in *Glazed Dishes*, and it will settle around the sides of the *Vessels* and at the *Bottom*; thus you will have prepared *Crystalline Alum*. The second

Way is this: Boil the *Alum* in an *Earthen Vessel* until the *Humidity* <note>moisture</note> vanishes and you find it spongy, white, and light; then it is prepared for *Sublimations* and various other *Operations*. *Plumose Alum* is dissolved and congealed just like the former; and so it is prepared.

Now, it is expedient to speak of the *Preparations* of *Atraments* <note>black metallic salts or inks</note>, seeing that this is a very necessary thing; because we find a necessary use for them in *Tinctures* and *Ligaments* of *Spirits*, and other things which belong to the *Work*. *Black-Atrament* is dissolved in boiling *Water*, distilled through a *Filter*, and congealed; then it is prepared. *Copperas* <note>ferrous sulfate</note> is dissolved in distilled *Vinegar*, clarified by filtering, and congealed: so it is clean. *Roman Vitriol* needs no other *Preparation* than only (in some cases) to be held in a moderate *Fire* and rubified <note>reddened by heating</note>.

Moreover, it is certain that various things necessary for us are extracted from imperfect *Bodies* which need *Preparation* and are helpful in this *Case*: and these are, first, *Ceruse of Lead* <note>white lead</note>, which is thus prepared. *Ceruse of Lead* must be washed in distilled *Vinegar*, then purged of its coarser parts; and that which flows out from there like *Milk* must be congealed in the *Sun*, and it is prepared. The preparation of *Spanish White*, *Tin*, and *Minium* <note>red lead</note> is done in the same manner. For they must be washed in distilled *Urine* and pre-

The Second Part.

## C H A P. V.

### *Of the Purifications, or Cleansing of Spirits.*

IN the first *Part* we sufficiently treated of those things which (in our certain *discovery*) seemed to us necessary for the preparation of *spirits* and *bodies*. Here we now intend to enlarge our *discourse* concerning the *sublimation* or *preparation* of *spirits* of every *species*, according to the *requirement* for the completion of our *mastery* <note>magistery, the alchemical process of transformation</note>. But do not deviate from this *doctrine* of our *discovery*, which, through profound *meditation* and in *works* <note>practical application or experiments</note>, we see as very necessary for us and most certain. Here we investigate the *weights* and the special way of preparing and subliming them with *experience* altogether certain; and we first begin with the *preparation* of *sulfur* and its *companion* <note>compeer; likely referring to arsenic</note>, and then others in their order.

<meta>catchword: CHAP.</meta>

## C H A P. VI.

### Of the Preparation of Sulfur.

Grind clear and gummy *native sulfur* <note>Sulphurvive</note> into a very fine *powder*. Boil this in a *lye* <note>Lixivium</note> made of the ashes of *heartsease* <note>the pansy, Viola tricolor</note> and *quick-lime*, skimming the oily combustibility from the surface until it is seen to be clear. Once this is done, stir the whole mixture with a stick, and then carefully take off that which passes out with the *lye*, leaving the coarser parts at the bottom. Allow that *extract* to cool a little, and pour a fourth part of its own volume of distilled *vinegar* upon it; then the whole will suddenly congeal like *milk*. Remove as much of the clear *lye* as you can, but dry the residue with a gentle *fire* and keep it.

In the management of the *residue* <note>feces</note>, observe this *method*. For every one pound of this prepared *sulfur*, take 1 lb. of *iron scales* well-calcined to redness, 1 lb. of *rock alum* <note>Roch-Allom</note> also well-calcined, and half a pound of prepared *common salt*. Incorporate all these well by grinding them together with *vinegar* so that the whole mixture is liquid; then boil it, stirring it, until it becomes entirely black. Then dry,

<summary>This chapter provides detailed instructions for purifying sulfur through boiling in a lye solution and congealing it with vinegar, followed by mixing it with iron and alum.</summary>

<keywords>sulfur, native sulfur, lye, heartsease, quick-lime, vinegar, iron scales, rock alum, calcination, alchemical preparation</keywords>

--- Page 267 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for the preparation of sulfur from Chapter VI and begins Chapter VII on the preparation of arsenic.</meta>

dry and grind it to a fine *powder*, which you should put into an *aludel* with a large *cover*. Let the *cover* of the *alembic* have a large and great *zone* (or *girdle*) for the preservation of the elevated *spirits*: the *aludel* itself must be one and a half *feet* high, so that the *heat* may not touch the *zone* of the *alembic*. This being done, sublime it as we taught you in our *book, "The Sum of Perfection"*, etc. But gather whatever is dense (or closely compacted) in the *zone*; and cast away the light, powdery *flos* <note>literally "flower," referring to the light, volatile substance</note> which ascends above it and adheres to the sides of the *alembic*: for it is combustible, defiled, and defiling. Keep the dense *matter* that has been sublimed by itself in a *phial*, and cook <note>coct</note> it upon *ashes* until its combustible *humidity* is gradually removed. Then preserve it clean, for it is perfectly cleansed.

## C H A P.  V I I.

->Of the Preparation of Arsenic.<-

After its *compeer arsenic* is beaten to *powder*, it must be boiled in *vinegar*, and all its combustible *fatness* extracted, and then dried. Then take <note>℞</note> 1 lb. of calcined *copper*, 1/2 a pound of calcined *alum*, and as much prepared *common salt* as there is *alum*.

[a]lum. Mix these with your prepared *arsenic*. After having ground all well together, moisten the *mixture* with distilled *vinegar* (so that it may be liquid) and boil the same, as you did with *sulfur*; and then sublime it in an *aludel* (without an *alembic*) one *foot* high. Gather what ascends white, dense, clear, and lucid, and keep it; because it is sufficiently prepared for the *Work*.

## C H A P. VIII.
### *Of the Preparation of Quicksilver <term>Argentvive</term>.*

Sublime *quicksilver* <term>Argentvive</term> thus: Take <note>Rx</note> 1 lb. <note>lib. j.</note> of it, 2 lb. <note>lib. ij.</note> of rubified *vitriol*, 1 lb. <note>lib. j.</note> of calcined *rock alum* <term>Roch-Allom</term>, 1/2 lb. <note>lib. ß.</note> of *common salt* <term>Common Sal</term>, and one-fourth part of *saltpeter*. Incorporate all together and sublime; gather the *white*, *dense*, and *heavy* <note>ponderous</note> substance which shall be found about the *sides* of the *vessel*, and keep it, as we have directed for other things. But if, in the first *sublimation*, you find it cloudy or unclean (which may happen by reason of your own negligence), sublime it again with the same *dregs* <term>Feces</term>, and reserve it for use.

## C H A P. IX.

### *Of the Preparation of Marcasite.*

Spread ground *<term>marcasite</term>* to the thickness of a finger over the *bottom* of a large *<term>aludel</term>*, and first collect the *<term>sulfur</term>* with a gentle *fire*. When that has ascended, take off the *head* (or *<term>alembic</term>*) and, having applied another, increase the *fire*; then, that which takes the *place* of *<term>argentvive</term>* ascends, as we have fully described in our *Sum of Perfection*.

---

## C H A P. X.

### *Of the Preparation of Tutia, &c.*

Put pulverized *<term>tutia</term>* into an *<term>aludel</term>*, and with the application of great *heat* <note>ignition</note> sublime it, and it is prepared.
*<term>Sal ammoniac</term>* is sublimed from *common salt*, as we have declared to you in the *Praxis of Salts*.

Mortify the *<term>mercury</term>* with these,

But the consideration of *feces* <note>residue or dregs</note> is to be observed in the *sublimation* of *sulfur* and its *compeer* <note>partner or equivalent substance</note>: for *sulfur* sublimed from the *calx* of *copper* is more whitened than when sublimed from the *calx* of *iron*. ## C H A P. X I.

### *How bodies ought to be prepared.*

Our third *part* declares the complete *preparation* of imperfect *bodies*. For we have found out the *way* how they should be prepared, so as to be perfected (as to the completion of the first or second *order*) by themselves, without *medicine*. This we intend to show; and secondly, the *preparation* of them for receiving *medicine*, white or red.

## C H A P. XII.

### *Of the Preparation of Saturn.*

**C**alcine *Saturn* <note>lead</note> melted with prepared *Common-Salt*, stirring it continually with an *Iron Spatula*, until it is turned to *Ashes*. Cook it for one natural *Day*, and let it be a little fiery-hot, but not much: then completely wash it with *Sweet Water* <note>fresh water</note>; afterward calcine it for three *Days*, until it is red within and without. If you would have it be prepared for the *White*, soak it with the *Water* of white *Alum*, and reduce it with *Oil of Tartar*, or its *Salt*. But if for the *Red*, soak it with *Water* of *Crocus of Iron* <note>iron oxide</note>, and of the *Verdigris* aforesaid; and reduce it with *Salt of Tartar* as before. Repeat this labor as often as shall be necessary.

---

Destroy without *Anger*, and you shall complete your *Work* with *Joy*.

## CHAPTER XIV.

->*Of the Preparation of Mars.*<-

Prepare *Mars* <note>iron</note> thus: Grind one pound of its *filings* with half a pound of sublimed *arsenic*. Saturate <note>imbibe</note> the *mixture* with the *water* of *saltpeter* and *alkali salt*, repeating this *saturation* three times; then make it flow <note>melt</note> with violent *fire*, and you will have your *iron* white. Repeat this *work* until it flows sufficiently, with a specific *whitening* <note>dealbation</note>.

---

## CHAPTER XV.

and you will find the *Body* of *Venus* <note>copper</note> white and splendid, fit for receiving its *Medicine*.
For the *Red*, its *Preparation* is thus:
Grind one pound of the *Filings* of *Venus* with four ounces of *Sulphur*; or *Cement Plates* of *Copper* with *Sulphur*, and so calcine <note>heat to oxidize</note>; and wash the *Calcinate* <note>the residue of calcination</note> with the *Water* of *Salt* and *Alum*, and then (with reducing agents) reduce it into a clean *Body* suitable for the *Red Tincture*.
There is also a third *Preparation* of it: *Calcine* it with *Fire* only, and then dissolve a part of it, and likewise dissolve as much of calcined *Tutia* <note>zinc oxide</note>; join both *Solutions*, and with the same saturate <note>imbibe</note> the remaining part of the *Calx* of *Venus* four or five times. Or you may make this *Saturation* <note>imbibition</note> with *Tutia* alone dissolved; provided that more of the *Tutia* (than half of the *Calx* is) be saturated into the said *Calx*. This being done, reduce with reducing agents, and you will have the *Body* of *Venus* clean and splendid; which with a little help may be brought to a higher *State*, if you are a true *Searcher* of the *Truth*.
There is likewise a fourth *Preparation* of it, and it is thus: Of it, by itself calcined (I mean with *Fire* only), you may make an intense *Greenness*, which is called the *Flos* <note>flower</note> of *Copper*. Dissolve this *Greenness* with distilled *Vinegar*; and then congeal it; afterward, with reducing agents reduce the *congealed substance* <note>congelate</note>, which when reduced will be suitable for many things, if

Your *investigation* in such *works* given by us has not been too remiss. Because here we posit the *practice step-by-step* <note>gradatim</note>, but there the *theory* of our *speculation*, disposed of in a more *general* way, with manifest demonstrations.

## The fourth part of this book.

---

# Chapter XVI.

## *Of Medicines.*

According to the *order* of the *premises*, we will (in this our fourth *part*) again practically speak of the *way* of *investigation*, compounding every *medicine*, namely *white* and *red*, according to the *nature* and *property* of the *body* to be transmuted, or of *mercury* itself, with all its attributes <note>pertinencies</note>, both hidden and manifest. And this, in the *second* or *third order*. For this our *book*, being itself entitled *Of the Invention of Truth* <note>Invention of Verity</note>, contains nothing that is sophistical. Therefore, we will begin to speak first of *white elixirs*.

## C H A P. XVII.

### Of White Medicines for Jupiter, and Saturn.

A *White Medicine* for *Jupiter* prepared:
Take <note>Rx</note> 1 lb. of most pure *Luna*, and 8 lbs. of *Living Mercury*. Amalgamate these together; then wash the *Amalgam* with distilled *Vinegar* and prepared *Common Salt*, until it acquires a *Celestine* or *Azure Color*. When this is done, extract as much of the *Mercury* as you can by strongly squeezing the *Mixture* through a thick *Cloth*. Then add of *Sublimate Mercury* double the weight of your *Luna*, and grind them well together; afterward cook the *Mixture* in a *Phial* firmly closed for one *Day* and *Night*; then take out the *Matter*, grind it, and again *Cook* the same; then break the *Vessel*, and separate that which is sublimed from the *Lower Reddish Powder*. But take heed of giving too great a *Fire*, for that would cause the whole to flow into one black *Mass*. ## CHAPTER XVIII.

### Of *Solar Medicines* for *Jupiter* and *Saturn.*

THE first *Solar Medicine* is made as follows. Calcine *Sol*, first amalgamating it with *Mercury*, and as with *Luna*, press the *Mercury* through a *Cloth*: then grind it with twice as much as its own weight of prepared *Common Salt*, and set the whole over a gentle *Fire*, so that the remaining *Mercury* may evaporate <note>recede</note>. # CHAPTER XXI.

## Of a Medicine of the Third Order, for the White.

From here we ascend to the degree of the third *Order*. First, we will treat of the *Lunar Medicine*, both for perfecting imperfect *Bodies* and for coagulating *Mercury* itself into true *Luna* <note>silver</note>. Therefore, dissolve calcined *Luna* in solutive *water* <note>a solvent or acid</note>, as before; which being done, cook <note>coct</note> it in a *Phial* with a long *Neck*, the orifice of which must be left unstopped, for one day only, until a third part of the *Water* is consumed. This being accomplished, set it with its vessel in a cold place, and then it converts to small fusible *Stones*, like *Crystal*. This is *Silver* reduced to our *Mercury*, fixed and fusible;

## CHAPTER XXII.

### *Of a Solar Medicine of the Third Order.*

The *Solar Medicine* is made of *Sol* <note>gold</note> dissolved and prepared after the manner of *Luna's* <note>silver's</note> *Preparation*, to which you must add three *parts* of dissolved *Sulphur*, and one part of *Arsenic* (as is shown at the end), doing everything as in the *Lunar Chapter*; and it will be a *Medicine* tinging every *Body*, and *Mercury* itself, into true *Sol*, or better, according to the method now shown.

Read and peruse our *Sum of Perfection*; and you will find the *Method* of the *Regimen* prescribed there, by which you may tinge to *Infinity*, if you possess a sharp and good *Wit*, and are not moved this way and that way by the various ambiguous *Sayings* of *Philosophers*. For they all tend toward the one *Perfection*, which has been sufficiently described to you by Us. You may make a *Trial*, if you are able; if not, forbear; because you will sustain *Loss* and reap nothing but *Emptiness*.

## CHAPTER XXIII.

### Of Solutive Waters and Incerative Oils.

**S**on of *doctrine*, search out *experiments*, and do not cease; because in them you may find *fruit* a thousandfold. For we wrote this *book* only for you, which we are willing to complete with *certain waters and oils*, very necessary in *our Magistery*: With these we shall conclude our *Book of the Invention of Perfection*. And first we shall begin with *our dissolutive water*, of which we made *mention* in *our Sum of Perfection*, when we speak of *dissolution* with the *acuteness of waters*.

First, take <note>℞: take</note> 1 pound <note>lib 1.</note> of *vitriol of Cyprus*, half a pound <note>lib. ß.</note> of *saltpeter*, and a fourth part of *jamenous alum*; extract the *water with redness of the alembic* (for it is very *solutive* <note>dissolving</note>) and use it in the previously cited *chapters*. This is also made much more acute if you dissolve a fourth part of *sal ammoniac* in it; because that dissolves *gold*, *sulfur*, and *silver*.

Our other *philosophical cerative water* is this: Take *oil distilled from the whites of eggs*, grind it with half as much *saltpeter* and equal parts of *sal ammoniac*, and it will be very good. Or mix it with *alkali salt*, and distill as before. And the more you repeat

A *red incerative oil* is made in this way: Take <note>℞</note> *oil of the yolks of eggs*, or of *human hair*, to which add as much *sal ammoniac*, and distill the *mixture*; repeat this *distillation* three times, and it will be a very red *incerative oil*.

*Oil of verdigris* is made when *verdigris* is dissolved in *water of sal ammoniac*, and when the same is congealed, the *oil of eggs* is mixed with it, the *mixture* distilled, and the *distillation* repeated three times. *Oil of gall* is made by extracting an *oil* from *gall*, just as from *human hair*; in all *things* doing as in the other.

I do not say that these can give a *mineral radical humidity*, as *we* proved regarding *sulfur* and *arsenic*: but they preserve the *tincture* from *combustion* until it enters, and afterward they fly off in the augmentation of the *fire*, as is elsewhere declared by *us*. Whatever *artist* shall perfectly and studiously operate according to *our sayings* contained in this *our book*, he, after the *completion* of his *work*, will find that *we have truly searched*. And with this, *our book* is ended, which is entitled *Of the Invention of Truth, or Perfection*.

## The PREFACE, dividing this BOOK into Three Parts.

*We have considered with a consideration that is not fanciful, that in our volumes we have amply treated of the whole art. Yet, so that we may not be vulnerable to the criticism of the envious, we have written this book of furnaces, in which we shall deliver the manual practice for the preparations of both spirits and bodies, so that craftsmen may better attain the completion of the work.*

*Therefore, seeing that the ultimate consideration consists in the knowledge of things*

## The First Part of this Book; Of the Ways of Operating.

---

### CHAP. I.

### Of the Calcinatory Furnace.

Let the calcinatory furnace be made square, four feet in length and three feet in width, and let the thickness of the walls be half a foot, in this manner: *Luna* <note>Silver</note>, *Venus* <note>Copper</note>, *Mars* <note>Iron</note>, or other *things* to be calcined, must be put into *dishes* or *pans* of very strong *clay*, such as those from which *crucibles* are made, so that they may endure the *intensity* of *fire*, even to the total *combustion* of the *thing* to be calcined. *Calcination is the treasure of a thing*; do not grow weary of *calcination*; but study what we have said in our *volumes*. For *imperfect bodies* are cleansed by *calcination*, and

## CHAP. II.

### Of the Sublimatory Furnace.

Let your *sublimatory furnace* be made in the manner that was fully taught before in our *Sum of Perfection*, regarding the *sublimation of spirits*, according to this *form*.

In the *sublimation of sulphur*, the cover of the *sublimatory* must be made with a large internal *concavity*, like an *alembic* <note>A vessel used for distillation</note> without a *nose* <note>A spout or delivery tube</note>; otherwise, the entire *sublimate* may descend to the *bottom* of the *vessel* because of excessive *heat*. Because at the end of the *sublimation*, the *sulphur* does not ascend unless forced by *fire*, even to the *ignition* <note>Making the vessel glow red-hot</note> of the *aludel* <note>A pear-shaped pot or condenser used in sublimation</note>; and if the *sulphur* is not retained in the *concavity* above—since it flows easily—it will descend again down the *sides* of the *vessel* to the very *bottom*, and so nothing will be found sublimed, as is known to the *expert*.

<summary>This page describes the construction of a sublimatory furnace and the specific design requirements for subliming sulphur. It explains that the vessel must have a large upper concavity to prevent the sulphur from melting and flowing back to the bottom during the intense heat of the process.</summary>

### C H A P. III.

**The** *distillatory furnace* is the same as the *sublimatory*. But *fire* must be administered according to the *necessity* of the *things* to be *distilled*. We have sufficiently described the way of *distilling*, both of *minerals* and of *vegetables*, in our *Sum of Perfection*.

## CHAP. V.

### *Of the Fusory, or Melting Furnace.*

**The** *fusory furnace* is that in which all *bodies* are easily melted by themselves; and it is a *furnace* much in use among *melters* of *metals* for coining money. Also <term>aurichalcum</term> <note>Brass or a gold-colored copper alloy</note> is melted in these *furnaces* and tinged with <term>tutia</term> <note>Zinc oxide, often used in making brass</note> or <term>calaminaris</term> <note>Calamine, a zinc ore</note>, as is known to the experienced.

---

## CHAP. VI.

### *Of the Solutory, or Dissolving Furnace.*

**The** *dissolutory* or *dissolving furnace* is made with a *pan* full of *water*, with *iron instruments* in which other *instruments* are *skillfully* retained so that they do not *fall*. These are the *vessels* in which every *dissolution* is made. And this is the *form* of the *furnace* and *vessels*.

### CHAP. VII.

#### Of the *Fixatory Furnace*, or *Athanor*.

**The** *fixatory furnace* must be made in the same manner as the *furnace of calcination*; and in it must be set a deep *pan* full of sifted *ashes*. But the *vessel*, containing the *matter* to be fixed, being first firmly sealed, must be placed in the middle of the *ashes*, so that the thickness of the *ashes* underneath, and above around the *vessel*, may be equal to the thickness of four *fingers*; or according to whatever you desire to fix, because in fixing one substance, a greater *fire* is required than in fixing another. By this *furnace*, and by this *way*, the *ancient philosophers* attained the *work* of the <term>magistery</term> <note>The mastery of alchemical transformation, often referring to the creation of the Philosopher's Stone</note>; which, to *men truly philosophizing*, is known to be sufficiently demonstrated in *our books*; and especially by those who are true *searchers of truth*.

This is the *figure* of the *athanor*. Yet if any *one* can more ingeniously invent something similar, do not let *our invention* prevent him from doing so.

## C H A P. VIII

### *Of the Preparations of Middle-Mineral Spirits, and Alums.*

**In** this *chapter* I will declare the *preparations* of *spirits*, and first of *mercury*: which if you would perfectly sublime, you must add to every pound of it two and a half pounds of *common salt*, and half a pound of *saltpeter*. *Mortify* the *mercury* wholly, grinding all together with *vinegar*, until nothing of the *mercury* appears living in the *mixture*; and sublime it, as you know: because it is profitable.

*Red mercury* is thus sublimed, namely: one pound of it is mixed, and perfectly well ground together, with one pound of *saltpeter*, and one pound of *vitriol*, and from them it is sublimed *red* and *splendid*. But *arsenic* is sublimed thus, namely: from one pound of the *filings of Venus* <note>Copper filings</note>, half a pound of *common salt*, and one quarter of a pound of *alum* calcined. *Sulphur* boiled in a *lixivium* <note>lye</note> and dried, is sublimed with the same *feces* <note>dregs or residue</note> as *arsenic*; except that, instead of the *filings* of *Venus* <note>copper</note>, into the aforementioned *vessel* is put *filings* of *Mars* <note>iron</note>, or the *scales* of it beaten to *powder*. *Sal ammoniac* is sublimed from *common salt*, etc. *Tutia* and *marcasite* are sublimed as is declared in *Our Sum of Perfection*. ## CHAPTER X.
### Of the Calcination of *Venus* and *Mars*.

*Venus* <note>Copper</note> is calcined thus: In the aforementioned furnace is put either the *filings of copper*, either by itself, or with pulverized *arsenic*; or with *sulphur*, being anointed with *common oil*, and so it is calcined for three or four *days* with a very strong *fire*. Strike the calcined material so that it may fall off from the *plates*, which you should calcine again; also, when the *calcined material* is beaten, re-calcine it until it is very well rubified; and so keep it.
*Mars* <note>Iron</note>, having been filed, is calcined in the aforementioned furnace until it is very well rubified and becomes an impalpable *powder*, beyond the sense of touch. And it is called *Crocus Martis* <note>Saffron of Mars; iron oxide</note>.

## CHAP. XII.

### *Of the Ablutions of the Calxes of Burned Bodies.*

The *ablution* <note>washing</note> of all *bodies* burned and calcined is performed in this way: First, you must have a large *earthen vessel* full of hot, fresh <note>sweet</note> *water*. Wash any *calx* of a calcined *body* with this, stirring it often so that all the *salt* and *alum* <note>allom</note> may be dissolved. Then, when it has settled, carefully pour off the *water* so that none of the *body* passes out with the *wash-water* <note>lotion</note>. Put the *calcined material* <note>calcinate</note> into *hot water* again and repeat the *labor* as before until it is perfectly well washed; then keep it.

### CHAP. XIV.

### *Of the Reduction of Calxes into a Solid Mass.*

The reduction of that washed and softened <note>incinerated/incerated</note> *calx* is performed in this way: Wash the softened *calx* with distilled *urine* until you have extracted all the *salts* and *alums*, along with the *filth* of the calcined *body*. When it is dried, soak it with *oil of tartar*, in which you should dissolve two ounces of *sal ammoniac* and one ounce of *saltpeter* for every pound of the *oil*. But there must be four pounds of the *calx*; and such *soaking* <note>imbibition</note> must be done several times, drying and soaking alternately. Then finally dry it, and cause it to descend in a large *descensory* <note>a vessel used for distillation by descent</note>, and reduce it into a solid *mass*. This process purges the *body* from *combustible sulfurousness* <note>sulphureity</note> by virtue of the calcining *fire*, and from foul *earthiness* <note>terrestreity</note>, which in *reduction* retain within themselves the *dregs* <note>feculency</note> of the *earth*. Thus the *body* is purified from accidental *impurities* which were present in its *ore* <note>minera</note>.

# CHAPTER XVII.

### Of the Regimen of *Jupiter* and *Saturn*.

**B**ecause we intend in this chapter to demonstrate the *regimen* of *Jupiter* and *Saturn*, we first signify that this *chapter* is for the *white* work, and the method is as follows: Take 1 pound of the cleanest *tin* and melt it; while it is in *flux*, add twelve pounds of well-cleansed *mercury*, stirring the whole so that they may be mixed. Put this mixture into a *vial* having a *neck* one foot in length; place this *vial* in an *athanor*, and likewise another *vial* with *Saturn* prepared in the same way, administering a gentle *fire* to them for a *week*. In that time, you will have a *dissolved paste* fit to be fermented with the *white ferment*, according to the *proportion* which we shall show below. Let there be four parts of the *paste* of *Jupiter*, three parts of *Saturn*, and one part of the *white ferment*. These, being dissolved as we said, must be mixed thoroughly down to their smallest parts and set in *putrefaction* (after the manner of our *dissolution* with moderate *fire*) for seven *days*. When this time has expired, let them be taken out and well mixed, and their more liquid *parts* squeezed through a *cloth*. Put whatever thick material remains into a well-sealed *glass* vessel, which you should place in the *athanor*,

Then put your *Vessel* with its *Matter* in the *Furnace of Fixation* for twelve *Days*; when these have expired, take it out and reduce it with reducing substances. And you will find that which our *Ancestors* discovered only after very great *Study*, namely: The *Generate* generating <note>a substance capable of reproducing its own qualities</note>. You may join the same with *Lead* in the *Cineritium* <note>a vessel or hearth used for refining metals in ashes</note>, and you will find the *Body* perfect in *Whiteness*, perpetually generating its like. The *Exposition* of which, together with what we have written concerning it, I (along with all my *Ancestors*) leave to my *Successors*.

---

# CHAPTER XVIII.

## Of the Regimen of *Venus* and *Saturn*.

Of the *Paste* of *Venus* <note>copper</note> let there be three pounds, of *Saturn* <note>lead</note> two, and of *Ferment* one. Once these are perfectly dissolved, make a *Commixture* through their smallest *Parts*, which you should keep in *Heat*, as was said regarding the *White* work. Extract the *Water*, and put whatever remains in the *Cloth* into a well-sealed *Glass* vessel for three *Weeks*. Then take it out, return to it a third part of its own reserved *Water*, and cook it <note>coct</note> as in the preceding *Chapter*; and do this three times.

But when it has absorbed all its *Water*, put it in its proper *Vessel* and *Furnace* to be fixed. When fixed, reduce it with reducing *Things* <note>substances used for chemical reduction</note> into a *Body* ready to be augmented and tinged.

# CHAPTER XIX.

->## Of the Regimen of Mars.<-

Since the *Solution* of *Mars* <note>iron</note> is found to be very difficult, we shall, at the *End* of this *Book*, treat of many *Ways*, and also set down various other *Experiments* made by us. Therefore, take 2 pounds (*lib.*) of the *Paste* of *Mars*, 4 pounds of *Venus* <note>copper</note>, and also 4 pounds of *Saturn* <note>lead</note>. Mix these without *Ferment*, and cook (*coct*) the *Mixture* for seven *Days*, and you will find the whole *Dry*. Fix it, and put it—together with half its weight of *Lithargy* <note>lead oxide</note> beaten to *Powder*—into a *Reductory* <note>a vessel or furnace used for reducing metals</note>, and you will find a very profitable *Mineral Body*, if you are wise, of which we have often made mention.

---

## CHAPTER XXI.

### Of the Regimen of Mercury.

The *Regimen* of *Mercury* is completed in two ways: First, you must amalgamate it, well washed and purified, in the certain *Proportion* written by *Us* below. In the second way, you must distill it, and from there make an
Aquavitæ <note>literally "water of life," referring to a strong distilled spirit or solvent</note>.

<summary>This page details the specific ratios and heating stages for refining silver (Luna) and introduces the two methods for the Regimen of Mercury: amalgamation and distillation.</summary>
<keywords>Luna, silver, Venus, copper, Ferment, fixation, Fire of Sublimation, Mercury, calcination, transmutation, distillation, Aquavitae</keywords>

--- Page 308 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for the Regimen of Mercury from the previous page, completing the sentence regarding the creation of an aqua vitae, and then detailing the proportions and process for creating an augmentable Stone. *Aquavitæ* <note>literally "water of life," referring here to a strong distilled solvent</note>. For the first way, the proportion is this: of *Mercury* 48 ounces; of *Sol* <note>gold</note> 1 ounce; of *Luna* <note>silver</note> 1 ounce; of *Venus* <note>copper</note> 1 ounce; and of *Saturn* <note>lead</note> 1 ounce. Melt these bodies; first the *Venus* and *Luna*; secondly, the *Sol*; and thirdly, *Saturn*. Take them all out of the fire, having melted them in a large *Crucible*, and have your *Mercury* ready and heated in another. When the said metals begin to harden, pour in the *Mercury* leisurely, stirring the mixture with a *Stick*, placing it again on the fire and taking it off until they are all amalgamated with the whole *Mercury*. Put this *Amalgama* to be dissolved for seven *Days*; extract the water with a *Cloth*, and make the *Residue* volatile by administering the fire of *Ignition*. Soak this again with its whole *Water*, and set it to be generated; then dry it again for forty *Days*, and you will find a *Stone*. Set this to be fixed, and you will have a *Stone* that can be increased to *Infinity*. Therefore, keep this *Book* secret even from your own *Son*, because it explains all *things* which we have written in various *Books*.

### CHAPTER XXIII.

->*Of the Ferment of Sol, for the Red.*<-

**T**he *Ferment* of *Sol* <note>gold</note> is made when *Gold* is dissolved in its own *Water*, and decocted and prepared according to the aforementioned *Chapter* of the *Ferment* of *Luna*. ## CHAPTER XXV.

### *A Recapitulation of the Author's Experiments.*

Since I intended in this *volume*
to declare all doubtful *things*, I will
conclude my *book* with all the true *experi-*
*ments* that have been proved and tried
by me. By these true *operations*, the new
*searcher* may perceive the *truth* or *falsity*
of various *sophistical recipes*, and so not spend
his time unprofitably; and likewise discern
what is good in the *recipes* of false *opera-*
*tors*. And first we intend to speak of *spirits* only, and
afterwards consequently of others, of *bodies*
as well as *spirits*, along with their *methods*.
But this *chapter* is divided into two
*parts*: First we declare the *experiences* of
the *ancients* proved by us; second, the
*rectifications* of them all. Yet, as we have
begun, we must first insist upon those *works*
which are of *whiteness*.

A good *Dealbation* <note>whitening</note> ℞ <note>recipe/take</note> of *Realgar* 1 oz, of *Argentvive* <note>quicksilver</note> sublimed, 3.5 oz, of *Tartar* calcined, 1 oz. Grind and incorporate them, and put them in a *Phial* with a *Neck* a foot in length, and its orifice so wide that two *Fingers* may enter: let it be luted <note>sealed with clay</note>, and set over a *Fire*, covered with a *Cloth*. First make a gentle *Fire* for a quarter of an hour; afterward increase the *Fire* underneath, and all around, until the *Furnace* is very hot with *Ignition*. When all is cold, break the *Vessel*, and take out what you find *Metalline*; and make a great *Quantity* of this. For I will now show you the way how this *Medicine* may be profitably rectified.

An *Artificial Dealbation*. Upon *Tutia*, sublime one part of sublimate *Mercury*, and two parts of *Arsenic* sublimed, until it shall have *Ingress*. This clearly, and very beautifully whitens *Venus* <note>copper</note>.

Another *Dealbation*. Imbibe three parts of *Mercury* sublimed, and two parts of *Arsenic* sublimed, with *Lithargiry* <note>lead oxide</note> dissolved, until they become eight parts. To these eight, add another eight parts of *Arsenic* sublimed; grind them together, and flux them with *Oil of Tartar*, and you will whiten prepared *Venus* at your pleasure.

## C H A P. XXVI.

### *Mercurial Sports.*

Now I begin to speak of *mercurial plays* or (*sports*): make a *cement* of *litharge* of *silver* and *salt alkali* of *zoza* (or *soda*). Put the *cement* first into a *crucible* the *thickness* of one *finger*; upon that, put a *globe* of the *amalgamation* of *mercury* and *Luna* <note>silver</note>, and put on the remainder of the *cement*, so that the *globe* may be in the midst of the *cement*. *Dry*, *lute* <note>seal with clay</note>, and set the *crucible* in a gentle *fire* for half a *day*, leisurely augmenting the *fire*; and so continue its leisurely *increase* from the evening until the dawning of the day, with moderate *ignition* at last. Then take it out, prove it by *cineritium* <note>cupellation, a refining process</note>, and it will be *Luna* in weight and *surdity* <note>density or lack of resonance</note>, and much better in *fixation*.

Also, *amalgamate Luna* with *mercury*, to which adjoin as much of *Saturn* <note>lead</note> as there is of the *Luna*. Put it into such a *crucible* that three-fourths of it may be empty; pour on it *oil of sulphur*, and cook it until the *consumption* of the *oil*. Afterwards, keep it for two *hours* in a moderate *fire*, and

In the end, incerate with the *Oil* of *Ammoniac*, and *Peter* <note>salt-peter</note>, and reduce it into a noble *Body*.
